Paschke duality and assembly maps
Ulrich Bunke, Alexander Engel, Markus Land

Abstract
We construct a natural transformation between two versions of G G -equivariant K K -homology with coefficients in a G G - C ∗ C^{*} -category for a countable discrete group G G . Its domain is a coarse geometric K K -homology and its target is the usual analytic K K -homology. Following classical terminology, we call this transformation the Paschke transformation. We show that under certain finiteness assumptions on a G G -space X X , the Paschke transformation is an equivalence on X X . As an application, we provide a direct comparison of the homotopy theoretic Davis–Lück assembly map with Kasparov’s analytic assembly map appearing in the Baum–Connes conjecture.



Acknowledgements.

Markus Land was supported by the research fellowship DFG 424239956, and by the Danish National Research Foundation through the Copenhagen Centre for Geometry and Topology (DNRF151).

1
Introduction and statements

The main result of the present paper is the construction of a natural transformation

K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}\to K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}

(1.1)

between two versions of spectrum-valued equivariant
K
-homology functors, where
G
is a countable discrete group. The evaluation of this transformation
on proper and
G
-finite
{G}
-simplicial complexes is an equivalence. Following the classical terminology, we call this transformation the Paschke transformation.
The functor
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}
in the domain is derived from a coarse
K
-theory functor using coarse geometric constructions, while the target
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}
is a spectrum-valued version of the classical equivariant analytic
K
-homology. In both versions the subscript
indicates a natural dependence of these functors on a coefficient
G
-
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
.

The Paschke transformation (
1.1
) will be used to compare the domains the Davis–Lück type assembly map with the Baum–Connes type assembly map.
Our second main result is Theorem
1.8

showing that these two assembly maps are equal
on the level of homotopy groups.

In the following we give an informal description of the construction of the two homology theories entering (
1.1
).
Starting from classical Paschke duality we further explain
the development of ideas leading to the details of the construction of the map
in (
1.1
). We then state
the precise version of our main result as Theorem
1.4
, and finally discuss the comparison of assembly maps.

We emphasize that this paper is not the first to treat the topic of equivariant Paschke duality and comparisons of assembly maps, most current are the papers
[
BR
]
and
[
Kra
]
. We explain more about this in Remarks
1.11
and
1.12
.

Constructions with the coefficients

Both
K
-homology functors occuring in (
1.1
)
depend on the choice of a
G
-
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
. We use the symbol
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
in order to denote the multiplier category of
{\mathbf{C}}

[
BEa
, Def. 3.1]
.
Starting from
{\mathbf{C}}
, in Definition
2.15
we describe an exact sequence

0\to{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\to\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\to\mathbf{Q}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\to 0

of
G
-
C^{*}
-categories
defining the
Calkin
G
-
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
.

Example 1.1
.

The case of trivial coefficients is where the
G
-
C^{*}
-category category
{\mathbf{C}}
is the category
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
of Hilbert spaces and compact operators with trivial
G
-action.

The multiplier category of
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
can be identified with the category
\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})
of Hilbert spaces and all bounded operators. By specializing Definition
2.15
the
G
-
C^{*}
-category
{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}}
is the category
\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}
of all pairs
(H,\rho)
of a Hilbert space
{H}
with a unitary
G
-representation
\rho
that are isomorphic to
(L^{2}(G)\otimes H^{\prime},\lambda\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{H^{\prime}})
, where
\lambda
is the left-regular representation and
H^{\prime}
is some auxiliary Hilbert space.
The morphisms
(H_{0},\rho_{0})\to(H_{1},\rho_{1})
in
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
are all compact operators
H_{0}\to H_{1}
.

The
G
-
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
is the category
\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}
which has the same objects, but its morphism spaces are the bigger spaces of all bounded linear operators. In both cases the
G
-action fixes objects and acts by conjugation on the morphism spaces.
The
G
-
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
is the Calkin category
\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}/\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
. Its objects are the objects
(H,\rho)
of
\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}
, and its morphism spaces are the quotient spaces of bounded operators by compact operators with the induced
G
-action. In particular, the endomorphism algebra of each object
(H,\rho)
is the usual Calkin algebra
Q(H)
of
H
with the
G
-action by conjugation, hence the name.
∎

Example 1.2
.

More generally, for a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we consider
the
G
-
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
of Hilbert
A
-modules and compact operators.
Its multiplier category is the category
\mathbf{Hilb}(A)
of Hilbert
A
-modules and all adjointable operators
[
BEa
, Lem. 8.1]
. The
G
-action on both categories is described explicitly in
[
BEa
, Ex. 2.10]
.

If
A
is unital, then the associated
G
-
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}

consists of pairs
(H,\rho)

of a Hilbert
A
-module together with a unitary
G
-action
\rho
such that

H
is isomorphic to an orthogonal sum of a family of finitely generated projective
A
-modules indexed by a free
G
-set.
Since
G
acts non-trivially on
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
the details are slightly more complicated to describe, see Definition
2.15
.

∎

Analytic
K
-homology

The construction of
the equivariant analytic
K
-homology functor
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}
with coefficients in
{{\mathbf{C}}}

employs the functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}
from
[
BEL
, Def. 1.8]
and its extension to
C^{*}
-categories

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}}

introduced in
[
BEL
, Def. 1.29]
, where
\mathrm{incl}
interprets a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra as a
G
-
C^{*}
-category with a single object. The mapping spectrum functor of the stable
\infty
-category

\mathrm{KK}^{G}
will be denoted by

\mathrm{KK}^{G}(-,-)\colon{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to\mathbf{Sp}\,.

For separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras
A
and
B
the homotopy groups

\pi_{*}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A),\mathrm{kk}^{G}(B))
are the classical equivariant
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
-groups of Kasparov associated to
A,B
.
In order to simplify the notation we drop the symbols
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
or
\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
when we express the value of a functor
F
defined in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
on a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
or a
G
-
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
, i.e.
we set
F(A)\coloneqq F(\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A))
or
F({\mathbf{C}})\coloneqq F(\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}({\mathbf{C}}))
, respectively.

The functor
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}
is defined by the formula

K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}\colon G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}\,,\quad X\mapsto\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(X),\mathbf{Q}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)})\,.

(1.2)

The domain of this functor is the category
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
of locally compact Hausdorff
G
-spaces with partially defined proper maps. Equivalently,
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
is the Gelfand dual of the category of non-unital commutative
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras.
The connection with the notation from
[
BEL
, Def. 1.15]
is given by

K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}=K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}}\,,

(1.3)

In particular,
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}
is different from
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}
— we apologize for this notational inconvenience.

In view of (
1.3
) the basic properties of
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}
listed in
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.15]
imply corresponding properties of
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
. In particular, the functor
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
is homotopy invariant, is excisive for closed decompositions of second countable spaces, and it annihilates spaces of the form
[0,\infty)\times X
.

Example 1.3
.

Let us consider the coefficients
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
for a unital
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
.
For a
G
-space
X
which is homotopy equivalent to a
G
-finite CW-complex with finite stabilizers, Proposition
11.15
provides a natural isomorphism

\pi_{*}K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\cong K\!K^{G}_{*-1}(C_{0}(X),{A})\,.

(1.4)

This isomorphism identifies our definition of equivariant analytic
K
-homology with the classical definition given by
the right hand side of (
1.4
), up to a shift of degrees.
∎

In order to deal correctly with non-
G
-compact spaces in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
we will consider
the locally finite version
K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
of
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
which is defined as follows. If
X
is in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
and
U
is an open
G
-invariant subset of
X
with
G
-compact closure, then we have a morphism

X\to U
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
given by the partially defined map

X\supset U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{id}}_{U}}}{{\to}}U
which corresponds to the extension-by-zero homomorphism
C_{0}(U)\to C_{0}(X)
on the level of
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras. We define

K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\coloneqq\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{U\subseteq X}K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(U)\,,

(1.5)

where the limit runs over all open subsets
U
of
X
with
G
-compact closure.
Using right Kan extensions, one can turn this prescription into the definition of a functor

K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\colon G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}\to\mathbf{Sp}\,,

(1.6)

see
[
BE20b
, Sec. 7.1.2]
for a similar construction.
We have a natural transformation

c\colon K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}

(1.7)

of functors from
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
to
\mathbf{Sp}
.
The functor
K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
is homotopy invariant. Its restriction
to second countable spaces with proper
G
-action is excisive for closed decompositions by
[
BEL
, Prop. 1.12.1]
. Finally, it sends countable disjoint unions to products.
If
X
is
G
-compact, then the canonical map

c_{X}\colon K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\to K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
is an equivalence. We refer to
Proposition
11.16
for a calculation of the values of
K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
on more general spaces.

The functors
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
and
K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
depend functorially on the coefficient
G
-
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
for non-degenerate morphisms.

Coarse
K
-homology

We now turn to a brief description of the functor
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
.
For our purposes, the functor
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
is most naturally defined on the category
G\mathbf{UBC}
of
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces.
This category comes with a cone-at-
\infty
functor
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\colon G\mathbf{UBC}\to G\mathbf{BC}
, where
G\mathbf{BC}
denotes the category of
G
-bornological coarse spaces. We shall define our coarse
K
-homology as the composition of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}
with an
equivariant coarse
K
-homology functor
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}\colon G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}

[
BEc
]
. For this to be well-behaved, we will need to assume that the coefficient
G
-
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
satisfies further axioms, namely that it is
is effectively additive and admits countable AV-sums, see Definitions
2.3
and
2.2
.
The coefficient category
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
for a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
satisfies these axioms by
[
BEa
, Lem. 7.9]
since it admits all small AV-sums.
We then set

K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\coloneqq K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}\circ{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\colon G\mathbf{UBC}\to\mathbf{Sp}\,.

(1.8)

This composition is an equivariant local homology theory, i.e. it is homotopy invariant, excisive for closed decompositions, and vanishes on spaces of the form
[0,\infty)\otimes X
, see Proposition
4.6
.

The functor
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{G}
and therefore also
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
depend also functorially
on the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
for non-degenerate morphisms.

A common domain for
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{\mathbf{C}}
and
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{\mathbf{C}}

By now, the functors
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
and
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{\mathbf{C}}
cannot be compared. They are invariants of different objects:
G
-locally compact Hausdorff spaces on the one hand, and
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces on the other hand. In Definition
5.6
we introduce the category
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{scl}}
of scaled
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces which by definition
comes with forgetful functors

\iota^{\mathrm{scl}}
to
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces and
\iota^{\mathrm{top}}
to
G
-locally compact Hausdorff spaces. Equipping a
G
-simplicial complex with the spherical metric on its simplices and a canonical scale
induces the upper part of the following diagram, where
G\mathbf{Simpl}_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\mathrm{prop}}
denotes the category of
G
-finite simplicial complexes with finite stabilizers and equivariant proper simplicial maps.

We can summarize our first main result, slightly informally, by the following diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\mathbf{Simpl}_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\mathrm{prop}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\mathbf{UBC}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{scl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\Longrightarrow}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{\mathbf{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\mathrm{scl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\mathrm{top}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}

The Paschke transformation
p
will be constructed as a natural transformation between the two composites of the lower square. Equivalently, naturality of
p
can be stated by saying that it makes the lower square lax-commutative. We then show that the Paschke transformation renders
the large square commutative. In other words, the Paschke transformation becomes a natural equivalence when
restricted to
G
-finite and
G
-proper simplicial complexes. In addition, the Paschke transformation is natural in the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
for non-degenerate morphisms. We will state our main theorem more formally as Theorem
1.4
below.

A review of classical Paschke duality

In order to motivate the definitions involved in the above diagram, we now review some aspects of classical Paschke duality.
Based on the seminal work of Paschke
[
Pas81
]
, the general theme of Paschke duality is to express the analytic
K
-homology

K_{*}^{\mathrm{an}}(X)\coloneqq\mathrm{KK}_{*}(C_{0}(X),{\mathbb{C}})

in terms of the
K
-theory
of a
C^{*}
-algebra naturally associated to
X
, which is then often referred to as the Paschke dual algebra of
X
.

Classically, this is implemented as follows.
Let
X
be a proper metric space and
\phi\colon C_{0}(X)\to B(H)
be a homomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras, where
H
is a separable Hilbert space. To this data one associates an exact sequence of
C^{*}
-algebras

0\to C(H,\phi)\to D(H,\phi)\to Q(H,\phi)\to 0

(1.9)

where
D(H,\phi)
is the
C^{*}
-subalgebra of
B(H)
generated by the controlled and pseudolocal operators and
C(H,\phi)
, called the Roe algebra, is its ideal generated by the operators which are in addition locally compact.

If
(H,\phi)
is
sufficiently large (very ample
in classical terminology or
absorbing in the sense of Definition
12.1
) and non-degenerate (meaning that
\overline{\phi(C_{0}(X))H}=H
), then the
K
-theory of
Q(H,\phi)
is a well-behaved invariant of
X
. More precisely, for a proper map
f\colon X\to X^{\prime}
and absorbing non-degenerate representations
(H,\phi)
and
(H^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
for
X
and
X^{\prime}
respectively, there exists a unitary, controlled and pseudolocal isometry
(H^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\cong(H,\phi\circ f^{*})
called a covering, which is unique up to conjugation by unitaries in
D(H^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
. This covering induces a homomorphism
D(H,\phi)\to D(H^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
preserving the respective Roe algebras and therefore a homomorphism

Q(H,\phi)\to Q(H^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
between the quotients. For
f={\mathtt{id}}_{X}
, this shows that the
K
-theory of
Q(H,\phi)
is independent of the choice of an absorbing representation
(H,\phi)
. We recall here that Voiculescu’s Theorem grants the existence of such absorbing representations. Furthermore, setting

K_{*}^{{\mathcal{X}}}(X)\coloneqq K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Alg}}_{*}(Q(H,\phi))

for any choice of an absorbing non-degenerate representation
(H,\phi)
, one obtains a functor

K_{*}^{{\mathcal{X}}}(-)\colon\mathbf{Met}^{\mathrm{prop}}\to{\mathbf{Ab}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}}

defined on the category of proper metric spaces and proper maps and taking values in graded abelian groups. The superscript
{\mathcal{X}}
indicates the coarse geometric origin of the construction, whose implementation was
initiated by Roe
[
Roe90
]
.
The functor
K_{*}^{{\mathcal{X}}}(-)
is homotopy invariant and admits Mayer–Vietoris sequences. In addition, there is a natural Paschke duality isomorphism

K_{*}^{{\mathcal{X}}}(X)\cong K^{\mathrm{an}}_{*-1}(X)

(1.10)

given by a concrete cycle level construction, see
[
HR00
]
for details.

The Paschke transformation following Quiao–Roe

The paper
[
QR10
]
discusses a systematic approach to the isomorphism (
1.10
), whose basic idea we will now adapt to the equivariant situation. We continue to assume that the
G
-space
X
is equipped with an absorbing non-degenerate representation
\phi\colon C_{0}(X)\to B(H,\rho)
where
H
is a separable Hilbert space equipped with a unitary
G
-action
\rho
.
The idea is to derive the isomorphism in (
1.10
) from
a multiplication map

\mu_{X}\colon C_{0}(X)\otimes Q^{G}(X)\to Q(H)\,,

(1.11)

Q^{G}(X)\coloneqq Q^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)\coloneqq D^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)/C^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)\,,

where
D^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)
and
C^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)
are defined as in the non-equivariant case by just adding the condition that the controlled generators are
G
-invariant. Furthermore

Q(H)=Q(H,\rho)
is the Calkin algebra of
(H,\rho)
with the induced
G
-action.
Using the multiplication map (
1.11
), one may define a Paschke morphism as the composition

p^{(H,\rho,\phi)}_{X}\colon\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},Q^{G}(X))\xrightarrow{\delta_{X}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(X),C_{0}(X)\otimes Q^{G}(X))\xrightarrow{\mu_{X}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(X),Q(H))\,.

(1.12)

The map
\delta_{X}\coloneqq C_{0}(X)\otimes-
is the exterior product in equivariant KK-theory and is called the diagonal morphism. We note that the algebras
Q^{G}(X)
and
Q(H)
are not separable, which is why
E
-theory instead of
K\!K
-theory is used in
[
QR10
]
. However, the equivariant KK-theory of
[
BEL
]
is well-defined for all
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras, so we can safely work with this version rather than with
E
-theory.

With this more abstract definition, how can one show that the Paschke morphism induces an isomorphism on
K
-groups, at least for suitable spaces
X
? Our strategy to answer this question is as follows.
Suppose one could show that the maps
p^{(H,\rho,\phi)}_{X}
in (
1.12
) were the components of a natural transformation of functors with values in the
\infty
-category of spectra,
and that both the domain and target of the Paschke transformation are homotopy invariant and excisive
1
1

1

This is the spectrum analogue of the property of admitting Mayer–Vietoris sequences for group-valued functors
as functors in
X
.
Then for
G
-finite
G
-CW-complexes
X
, by induction over the number of
G
-cells, one can reduce the verification that
p_{X}^{(H,\rho,\phi)}
is an equivalence
to the cases of
G
-orbits, i.e. spaces of the form
G/H
, where
H
runs over the subgroups of
G
appearing as stabilizer of the
G
-action on
X
. While in the non-equivariant case, only the trivial case
X=\ast
is to be treated, the verification that the Paschke maps are equivalences on general
G
-orbits is a non-trivial matter.

The above strategy will indeed be the essential idea of the proof of our main Theorem
1.4
below. The first difficulty to overcome is to show that the Paschke maps
p^{(H,\rho,\phi)}_{X}
are indeed the components of a natural transformation, in particular, to show that the spectrum
\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},Q^{G}(X))
appearing in the domain of the Paschke map, is a homotopy invariant and excisive functor in
X
(it is not even a functor in any obvious manner).
The origin of the problem is that in order to define
Q^{G}(X)=Q^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)
, one has to
choose
an absorbing non-degenerate representation
(H,\rho,\phi)
, and for a morphism
X\to X^{\prime}
one has to
choose
a covering in order to define the map

\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},Q^{G}(X))\to\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},Q^{G}(X^{\prime}))
.
Defined in this way, the resulting map of spectra depends on these choices and is, at best, unique up to an unspecified homotopy, which is not sufficient for our purposes.

The Paschke transformation in our setup

Our key idea to overcome these functoriality issues is to work with the category of all representations. In fact, the categories of such representations themselves depend on the space in a strictly functorial manner. Their use hence circumvents the need to find absorbing representations.
The idea to work with the whole category of representations is not new; it has first been exploited in
[
BE20b
]
in order to define a spectrum-valued coarse
K
-homology functor
K\!{\mathcal{X}}
.

In the present paper, as indicated earlier, we work with its equivariant generalization, the equivariant coarse
K
-homology functor

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\colon G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}

introduced in
[
BEc
]
, where, as before,
G\mathbf{BC}
denotes the category of
G
-bornological coarse spaces.
Again, the symbol
{\mathbf{C}}
refers to its dependence on a coefficient
G
-
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
.
In the case of trivial coefficients it is shown in
[
BEb
, Thm. 6.1]
that this functor is equivalent to the classical definition of equivariant coarse
K
-homology in terms of Roe algebras. More precisely, if the
G
-space
X
is nice, and
C^{G}(X)\coloneqq C^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)
with
(H,\rho,\phi)
ample,
we have a natural equivalence

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X)\simeq K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Alg}}(C^{G}(X))\,.

By construction, see Definition
3.4
, for
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
we have
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X)=\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X))
, where

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
is a
C^{*}
-category of equivariant
X
-controlled objects in
{\mathbf{C}}
, see Definition
3.2
for the details.
The endomorphism algebras of the objects of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
are natural analogues
of the Roe algebras
C(H,\rho,\phi)
.

We now indicate the relation between the functor
X\mapsto K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{\mathbf{C}}(X)
and the association
X\mapsto\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},Q^{G}(X))
appearing in the source of the Paschke morphism (
1.12
). Recall from (
1.8
) that
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{\mathbf{C}}
is defined as a composition of
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
with the functor
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-)\otimes G_{can,max}
on
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces.

If
X
is in
G\mathbf{UBC}
, then the cone
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
is the
G
-set

{\mathbb{R}}\times X
with a certain
G
-bornological coarse structure described in
[
BEKW20a
, Sec. 9]
and
[
BE20a
, Section 8]
.
It contains the underlying
G
-bornological coarse space of
X
as the subspace
\{0\}\times X
. We further let

{\mathcal{O}}(X)
in
G\mathbf{BC}
be the subset
[0,\infty)\times X
with the induced structures.
The inclusion
X\to{\mathcal{O}}(X)
induces an inclusion of categories

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X\otimes G_{can,max})\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})\,

(1.13)

to be thought of as the analog of the inclusion
C^{G}(X)\to D^{G}(X)
in the classical situation, see Section
11
for more details.
The resulting quotient
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{Q}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)

is then our version of the algebra
Q^{G}(X)
, and we have natural equivalence

K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\simeq\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{Q}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X))\,.

(1.14)

We refer to Section
6
and Lemma
7.1
for more details and necessary additions. In particular we add scales
\tau
(see Def.
4.3
) to the picture which leads to the better behaved version
\mathbf{Q}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,\tau)
of
\mathbf{Q}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
which on the one hand satisfies

\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{Q}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,\tau))\simeq\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{Q}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X))
, but on the other hand allows the construction of a
multiplication map

\mu_{(X,\tau)}\colon C_{0}(X)\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,\tau)\to\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}},

see (
7.19
), where
\mathbf{Q}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,\tau)
is abbreviated to
\mathbf{Q}_{\tau}(X)
.
In complete analogy to the earlier described Paschke morphism (
1.12
), we define our version of the Paschke morphism as the composition:

p_{(X,\tau)}\colon\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{Q}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,\tau))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(X),C_{0}(X)\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,\tau))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{(X,\tau)}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(X),\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\,.

(1.15)

The main result of this paper is then the following theorem.

Theorem 1.4
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is effectively additive and admits countable AV-sums.

1.

The morphisms in (
1.15
) assemble into a natural transformation of spectrum-valued functors on
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{scl}}

p\colon K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\iota^{\mathrm{scl}}\to K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}\circ\iota^{\mathrm{top}}\,

(1.16)

that is natural in the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
for non-degenerate morphisms.

2.

If

(X,\tau)
is in
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{scl}}
and homotopy equivalent to a
G
-finite

G
-simplicial complex with finite stabilizers, then

p_{(X,\tau)}\colon K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}(X)\to K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(X)

is an equivalence.

3.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small AV-sums,
G
is finite,

(X,\tau)
is in
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{scl}}
and homotopy equivalent to a countable finite-dimensional

G
-simplicial complex, then

p^{\mathrm{lf}}_{(X,\tau)}\colon K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}(X)\to K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An},{\mathrm{lf}}}(X)

is an equivalence.

We refer again to Definitions
2.2
and
2.3
for the conditions on
{\mathbf{C}}
appearing in the statement above, and recall again that the coefficient category
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
, for
A
a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra, satisfies these conditions.
In Assertion
1.4
.
3
we use the transformation
c\colon K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
from (
1.7
)
and set
p^{\mathrm{lf}}\coloneqq c\circ p
.

Definition 1.5
.

The transformation
p
in (
1.16
) is called the Paschke transformation.

The proof of Assertion
1.4
.
1
will be finished in Section
8
, and the proof of Assertions
1.4
.
2
and
1.4
.
3
will be completed in Section
10
.
Once
p
is constructed, which is not at all trivial, the verification that it is an equivalence under additional conditions follows the route described above, i.e. by reducing it to the case of orbits. The verification that
p
is indeed an equivalence on
G
-orbits with finite stabilizers also turns out to be quite involved and uses a lot of the properties of the
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories obtained in
[
BEa
]
.

In the case of trivial coefficients and under the assumption of the existence of an absorbing representation
(H,\rho,\phi)
we can compare the version of the Paschke morphism
p^{(H,\rho,\phi)}_{X}
from (
1.12
) with the newly defined Paschke morphism
p_{(X,\tau)}
from (
1.15
) (in particular their domains): Indeed, in Proposition
12.2
we show that there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},Q^{G}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(X),Q(H))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{(X,\tau)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{X}^{(H,\rho,\phi)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

so that, under the assumption that
p_{(X,\tau)}
is an equivalence,
\gamma
is an equivalence if and only if
p_{X}^{(H,\rho,\phi)}
is.

Assembly maps

Our original motivation to show the Paschke duality theorem above
was the wish to write out a complete proof for the fact the homotopy theoretic assembly map of Davis–Lück
[
DL98
]

and the analytic assembly map appearing in the Baum–Connes conjecture
are equivalent.
Such an equivalence was asserted in
[
HP04
]
, but the details of the proof given in this reference
remained sparse. While we were preparing this paper, a comparison of the two assembly maps was recently also carried out by Kranz
[
Kra
]
with methods different from ours, see Remark
1.12
.

Homotopy theoretic assembly maps are generally defined for any equivariant homology theory
G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{M}
with
cocomplete target
\mathbf{M}
and a family
{\mathcal{F}}
of subgroups, see Definition
13.1
. Our comparison concerns
the functor

K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}\,,\quad S\mapsto K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}(S_{min,max})\,,

(1.17)

see Definition
13.2
.
Note that the twist is different from the one used in the Definition (
1.8
) of
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
, namely it is
G_{can,min}
rather than
G_{can,max}
.
For appropriate choice of coefficients
{\mathbf{C}}
, the functor
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}
is equivalent to the functor introduced by Davis–Lück,
Remark
11.12
.

The equivariant homology theory
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}
gives rise to a canonical functor

K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}\colon G{\mathbf{Top}}\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}

denoted by the same symbol, see Definition
11.3
.
For any family of subgroups
{\mathcal{F}}
of
G
the homotopy theoretic assembly map can be described as the map

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{h}_{{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathcal{F}}}}\colon K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\to K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(*)

induced by the projection
E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}\to*
, where
E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}
is a
G
-CW-complex representing the homotopy type of the classifying space of
G
with respect to the family
{\mathcal{F}}
.

For the following we assume that
{\mathcal{F}}\subseteq\mathbf{Fin}
.
We define

RK^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\coloneqq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{W\subseteq E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(W),

where the colimit runs over the
G
-finite subcomplexes of
E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}
. In Definition
13.12
we construct an
analytic assembly map

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},{\mathcal{F}}}\colon RK^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\to\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)\,,

(1.18)

where the
C^{*}
-category
{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
is defined in Definition
2.15
and the reduced crossed product for
C^{*}
-categories is as introduced in
[
BEa
]
, see Section
13
for the precise construction.

The assembly maps
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{h}_{{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathcal{F}}}}
and
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},{\mathcal{F}}}
depend naturally
on the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
for non-degenerate morphisms.

In Definition
13.8
we construct
a spectrum-valued version of the classical Kasparov assembly map

\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{A,{\mathcal{F}}}\colon RK^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\to\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},A\rtimes_{r}G)

(1.19)

which functorially depends on
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.
We consider the spectrum-valued refinement (
1.19
) of Kasparov’s assembly map as an interesting result in its own right. In view of the definition of the domain, one has to construct a family of such assembly maps indexed by the
G
-finite subcomplexes
W

of
E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}
which is compatible with inclusions. While it is easy to lift Kasparov’s construction to a map of spectra for each such
W
individually, and it is also easy to obtain the required compatibility on the level of homotopy groups, it is a non-trivial matter to enhance the compatibility to the spectrum level. We obtain this enhancement in the form of the natural transformation (
13.17
).

For a
G
-
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
let
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
denote the full unital
G
-
C^{*}
-subcategory of unital objects. In Proposition
17.3
we show the following comparison
result.

Proposition 1.6
.

We have an equivalence between the assembly maps

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},{\mathcal{F}}}
from (
1.18
)
and
{\Sigma}\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)},{\mathcal{F}}}
from (
1.19
).

Example 1.7
.

In the case of a unital
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
and for
{\mathbf{C}}:=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
it follows from (
13.18
) and Proposition
1.6
that
the assembly map
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},{\mathcal{F}}}
is equivalent to

{\Sigma}\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{A,{\mathcal{F}}}
.

∎

The following theorem (whose proof will be completed at the end of Section
15
)
now provides a comparison of the Davis–Lück and Baum–Connes assembly maps on the level of homotopy groups. As indicated earlier, a version of this result has recently been shown also by
[
Kra
]
with completely different methods.

Theorem 1.8
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is effectively additive and admits countable AV-sums. We have a commuting square

\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}_{*}^{G}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}\mathrm{Asmbl}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},{\mathcal{F}}}^{h}}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}_{*}^{G}(*)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{RK^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}},*+1}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*+1}\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},{\mathcal{F}}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{*}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

(1.20)

in which all terms are natural in
{\mathbf{C}}
for non-degenerate morphisms.

The left vertical equivalence in (
1.20
) is, in a non-obvious manner, a consequence of our Paschke Duality Theorem
1.4
.
If
A
is a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra, then
{\mathbf{C}}:=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)

admits all small AV-sums (this follows from
[
BEa
, Thm. 8.4]
)
and hence satisfies the assumption of Theorem
1.8
.

We believe that our method can be upgraded to provide a commutative diagram on the spectrum level, but carrying this out would involve to control further large coherence diagrams. We refrain from doing this additional step at this point, but emphasize that the passage to a statement about homotopy groups is really only in the very final step where one filters
E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}
through
G
-finite subcomplexes. For any
G
-finite
X
in place of
E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}
, the diagram in Theorem
1.8
commutes already before applying homotopy groups. In particular, the square in (
1.20
) commutes before applying homotopy groups when there is a
G
-finite model of
E_{\mathcal{F}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}
. It is just that we have not worked out that the homotopies for varying
X
can be obtained in a compatible way. This problem is not visible to homotopy groups, and hence one obtains Theorem
1.8
irrespective of this issue.

We note that it is important to consider the reduced crossed product in the target for the approach presented here.
While the construction of the analytic assembly map easily lifts to the maximal crossed product our method unfortunately does not generalize to produce the corresponding comparison of assembly maps also for the maximal crossed product.

Further remarks

Finally, we explain some relations to previous works on (equivariant) Paschke duality and the analytical assembly map.
We begin with Paschke duality.

Remark 1.9
.

Valette established a non-commutative generalization of the classical Paschke duality
[
Val83
]
whose statement we briefly recall here.
We consider a
C^{*}
-algebra
B
with a strictly positive element. Then we have an exact sequence

0\to B\otimes K(\ell^{2})\to{\mathcal{M}}^{s}(B)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}\mathcal{Q}^{s}(B)\to 0\,,

where
{\mathcal{M}}^{s}(B)
is the stable multiplier algebra and the stable Calkin algebra
\mathcal{Q}^{s}(B)
is defined as the quotient.
In place of
\phi\colon C_{0}(X)\to B(H)
above we now consider a
unital separable nuclear
C^{*}
-algebra
A
with a representation

\tau\colon A\to B(\ell^{2})
such that
\tau(A)\cap K(\ell^{2})=\{0\}
and set

\phi\colon A\xrightarrow{1\otimes\tau}{\mathcal{M}}^{s}(B)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}\mathcal{Q}^{s}(B)
.
We further replace
Q(H,\phi)
from above by the commutant

Q(A,\phi,B)\coloneqq\phi(A)^{\prime}
of the image of
\phi
. The proof of the following result employs Kasparov’s generalization of Voiculescu’s Theorem.

Proposition 1.10

(
[
Val83
, Prop. 3]
)
.

We have an isomorphism

\mathrm{KK}_{*}({\mathbb{C}},Q(A,\phi,B))\cong\mathrm{KK}_{*-1}(A,B)

which is natural in
A
and
B
.

In this statement
\mathrm{KK}_{*}
denote Kapsarov’s
\mathrm{KK}
-groups. Note that
the right-hand side in the original statement of Valette is expressed in terms of
Ext
-groups
which are isomorphic to the
\mathrm{KK}_{*}
-groups under the given assumptions on
A
and
B
.
If
B
is in addition
\sigma
-unital, then by
[
BEL
, Prop. 1.20]
the
\mathrm{KK}
-group on the right-hand side coincides with the

KK
-group obtained from the spectrum-valued
\mathrm{KK}
-theory constructed in
[
BEL
]
.

See also
[
Tho00
, Thm. 3.2]
for a related result.

∎

Remark 1.11
.

Our Theorem
1.4
is similar in spirit to
[
BR
, Thm. 1.5]
.
But while Theorem
1.4
produces a natural transformation between spectrum-valued functors which becomes an equivalence when evaluated on spaces satisfying suitable finiteness conditions,

[
BR
, Thm. 1.5]
states an isomorphism between
K
-theory groups for a fixed space. While the class of spaces to which

[
BR
, Thm. 1.5]
applies is larger than the class of spaces for which
Theorem
1.4
provides an equivalence, our theorem allows to treat more general coefficients.

But even in the case where
both theorems are applicable the technical details of their statements are quite different so that at the moment it is difficult to compare them in a precise way.
In the following we explain this problem in greater detail.

The space
X
in
[
BR
, Thm. 1.5]
(denoted by
Z
in the reference) is a metric space with an isometric proper cocompact action of
G
.
In order to fit into our theorem we must require in addition that
it admits a scale
\tau
such that
(X,\tau)
belongs to
\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{scl}}
,
and that it is homotopy equivalent to a
G
-finite
G
-simplicial complex.
The domain of the Paschke map in
[
BR
, Thm. 1.5]
is the
K
-theory of a certain
C^{*}
-algebra
Q^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)
, where
H
is a sufficiently large Hilbert
C^{*}
-module over a commutative unital
C^{*}
-algebra
A
. In order to compare with our theorem
we would restrict the coefficients to the special case
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
.
We then could ask whether we have

K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}_{*}(\mathbf{Q}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X))\cong K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Alg}}_{*}(Q(H,\rho,\phi))\,,

see (
1.14
).
The construction of a comparison map could proceed similarly as the construction
of the map
\gamma
in Proposition
12.2
once we know that

(H,\rho,\phi)
is absorbing in the sense of the natural generalization of Definition
12.1
to controlled Hilbert
A
-modules.

On the positive side, in the case
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
,
the targets of the two Paschke duality
maps in
[
BR
, Thm. 1.5]
and Theorem
1.4

are equivalent in view of

K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\simeq\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(X),\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Prop.\ }\ref{wegojeogrregregwergwegre}}}{{\simeq}}\Sigma\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(X),A)

provided
X
is homotopy equivalent to a
G
-finite
G
-CW-complex.

∎

Remark 1.12
.

As mentioned earlier, in
[
Kra
]
Kranz also provides an identification of the Davis–Lück assembly map and the Kasparov assembly map. In fact, the contribution of Kranz is a comparison of the Davis–Lück assembly map with the version of the assembly map introduced by Meyer–Nest
[
MN06
]
. The latter is compared in
[
MN06
]
with Kasparov’s assembly map employing work of
Chabert–Echterhoff
[
CE01
]
. In Section
16

we will give a detailed account of the argument of Kranz using the
\infty
-categorical language of equivariant
\mathrm{KK}
-theory developed in
[
BEL
]
.
As an application, in Theorem
17.1
we give an argument (which is independent of Chabert–Echterhoff
[
CE01
]
) that the Kasparov assembly map is an equivalence for compactly induced coefficient categories or algebras.

∎

2
Constructions with
\bm{C^{*}}
-categories

In order to fix size issues we choose a sequence of four Grothendieck universes whose sets will be called very small, small, large, and very large, respectively. The group
G
, bornological coarse spaces or
G
-topological spaces, etc. belong to
the very small universe. The categories of these objects, the coefficient
C^{*}
-categories, the categories of controlled objects, and the
values of the
K
-theory functor
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
will
belong to the small universe. The categories of spectra
\mathbf{Sp}
and
\mathrm{KK}^{G}

are large, but locally small. They are objects of a category of stable
\infty
-categories
\mathbf{CAT}^{ex}_{\infty}
which is itself very large.

We let
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
be the category of small not necessarily unital
C^{*}
-categories with
G
-action and equivariant functors, and
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
be the subcategory of unital
C^{*}
-categories and functors preserving units. Both versions of
K
-homology considered in the present paper depend on the choice of a coefficient
C^{*}
-category

{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.

Example 2.1
.

We let
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
be the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
of

C^{*}
-algebras with
G
-action considered as single object categories. We furthermore set

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}):={\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\cap{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})\ .

Our basic example of a coefficient category is the category
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
of Hilbert
A
-modules and compact operators
for
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
, see Example
1.3
.
∎

Below we will consider conditions on
{\mathbf{C}}
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
which involve
orthogonal sums of possibly infinite families

(C_{i})_{i\in I}
of objects of
{\mathbf{C}}
. Let
(C,(e_{i})_{i\in I})
be a pair of an object of
{\mathbf{C}}
and a family of mutually orthogonal morphisms

e_{i}\colon C_{i}\to C
in the multiplier category
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
of
{\mathbf{C}}
.

Definition 2.2

(
[
BEa
, Def. 3.1]
)
.

We say that
(C,(e_{i})_{i\in I})
represents
an orthogonal AV-sum of the family
(C_{i})_{i\in I}
if

\sum_{i\in I}e_{i}e_{i}^{*}
converges strictly to
{\mathtt{id}}_{C}
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
.

Let
p
be an orthogonal projection on an object
C
in a
C^{*}
-category, i.e., an endomorphism of
C
satisfying
p^{*}=p
and
p^{2}=p
.
A morphism
u\colon C^{\prime}\to C
represents the image of
p
if
u
is an isometry (this means that
u^{*}u={\mathtt{id}}_{C^{\prime}}
) and
uu^{*}=p
.
We say that
p
is effective if it admits an image. In the present paper we will only consider orthogonal projections, and therefore we will omit the word orthogonal from now on. We refer to
[
BEa
, 2.16-2.19]
for more details.

Definition 2.3

(
[
BEc
, Def. 3.12]
)
.

We say that
{\mathbf{C}}
is effectively additive
if for every object
C
of
{\mathbf{C}}
and mutually orthogonal family of effective projections
(p_{i})_{i\in I}
on
C
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
such that
\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}
converges strictly to a projection
p
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
, the latter is also effective in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all small AV-sums or is idempotent complete, then it is effectively additive.
If
{\mathbf{C}}
is in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
, then we will apply the notions introduced above to the underlying
C^{*}
-category obtained by forgetting the
G
-action.

In general the category
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
may contain objects which admit an identity morphism. These objects are called unital. We note that automorphisms of
{\mathbf{C}}
preserve unital objects.

Definition 2.4
.

For
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
, we let
{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})

denote the full subcategory of unital objects in
{{\mathbf{C}}}
.

Example 2.5
.

Let
A
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg})
and
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
as in Example
2.1
. Then

{{\mathbf{C}}^{u}}=\mathbf{Hilb}(A)^{\mathrm{proj},\mathrm{fg}}
is the full subcategory of
\mathbf{Hilb}(A)
of finitely generated projective Hilbert
A
-modules.

∎

For the moment, let
{\mathbf{D}}
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
. Our main example will be the multiplier category
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
of
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.
We fix the following notation convention concerning the
G
-action on
{\mathbf{D}}
.
If
D
is an object of
{\mathbf{D}}
and
g
is in
G
, then we let
{gD}
denote the object
obtained by applying
g
to
D
.
Similarly, if
A
is a morphism in
{\mathbf{D}}
, then we write

{gA}
for the morphism obtained by applying
g
to
A
.

Definition 2.6
.

A
G
-object in
{\mathbf{D}}
is a pair
(D,\rho)
of an object in
{\mathbf{D}}
and a family
\rho=(\rho_{g})_{g\in G}
of unitaries
\rho_{g}\colon D\to{gD}
such that
g\rho_{h}\ \rho_{g}=\rho_{gh}
for all
h,g
in
G
.

Example 2.7
.

If
G
acts trivially on
{\mathbf{D}}
, then the datum of a
G
-object
(D,\rho)
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is the same as an object
D
of
{\mathbf{D}}
with a homomorphism
\rho\colon G\to{\mathtt{Aut}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(D)
,
g\mapsto\rho_{g}^{{-1}}
, such that

\rho_{g^{-1}}=\rho_{g}^{*}
.

∎

Definition 2.8
.

The category of
G
-objects in
{\mathbf{D}}
is
the
C^{*}
-category with
G
-action
{\mathbf{D}}^{(G)}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
defined as follows:

1.

objects: The objects of
{\mathbf{D}}^{(G)}
are the
G
-objects in
{\mathbf{D}}
.

2.

morphisms: The morphisms in
{\mathbf{D}}^{(G)}
are given by

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{D}}^{(G)}}((D,\rho),(D^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}))\coloneqq{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(D,D^{\prime})\,.

(2.1)

3.

composition and involution: The composition and involution are inherited from
{\mathbf{D}}
.

4.

G
-action:

(a)

objects:
G
fixes the objects of
{\mathbf{D}}^{(G)}
.

(b)

morphisms:
g
in
G
acts on a morphism
A\colon(D,\rho)\to(D^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
by

g\cdot A\coloneqq\rho_{g}^{\prime,-1}\,gA\,\rho_{g}\,.

(2.2)

Note that we use the notation
g\cdot-
in order to denote the
G
-action on morphisms between
G
-objects which should not be confused with the original action denoted by
g-
.

Associated to
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
we have two derived objects
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
and
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
in

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
. In the following we will show that they are related by a canonical zig-zag of
fully faithful functors.
To this end we construct a third object
\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
.

1.

objects: The
G
-set of objects of
\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}
is the union of the
G
-sets of objects
of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
and
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
.

2.

morphisms: The morphism spaces of
\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}
are defined such that

{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
and
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
are fully faithfully embedded. If
C
is in
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
and
(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
is in

({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
, then we define

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}}(C,(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})):={\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,C^{\prime})
and

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}}((C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}),C):={\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C^{\prime},C)
.

3.

The composition and the involution are inherited from
{\mathbf{C}}
.

4.

G
-action: The
G
-action is defined such that both the inclusions
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}\to\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}
and

({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}\to\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}
are
G
-equivariant.

If
\hat{f}\colon C\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
is a morphism
in
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}}(C,(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}))
given by
f\colon C\to C^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
, then
g\hat{f}\colon gC\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
is given by

\rho^{\prime,-1}_{g}\circ gf\colon gC\to C^{\prime}
. Similarly, if
\hat{h}\colon(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})\to C
is a morphism in
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}}((C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}),C)
given by
h\colon C^{\prime}\to C
, then
g\hat{h}\colon C^{\prime}\to gC
is given by

gh\circ\rho^{\prime}_{g}\colon C^{\prime}\to gC
.

Definition 2.9
.

We say that
G
weakly fixes the objects of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
if for every object
C
of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
there exists a refinement
(C,\rho)
to an object of
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
.

In other words,
G
weakly fixes the objects of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
if and only if the canonical functor

{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}}^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{2,1}}_{BG}{\mathbf{C}}^{u}\to\mathrm{Res}^{G}({\mathbf{C}}^{u})

from the
2
-categorical
G
-fixed points of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
to
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
with
G
-action forgotten is essentially surjective.

Lemma 2.10
.

1.

The inclusion

{\mathbf{C}}^{u}\to\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}

is a unitary equivalence.

2.

If
G
weakly fixes the objects of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
, then the inclusion

({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}\to\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}

is a unitary equivalence.

Proof.

By construction both inclusion functors are fully faithful. We now argue that they are essentially surjective.
We start with the inclusion of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
. We consider an object
(C,\rho)
in
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
. Then
C
is in
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
and
{\mathtt{id}}_{C}
gives a unitary isomorphism

C\to(C,\rho)
in
\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}
.

We now consider the inclusion of
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
.
Let
C
be an object of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
. By assumption there exists an object
(C,\rho)
in
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}

and again
{\mathtt{id}}_{C}
gives a unitary isomorphism

C\to(C,\rho)
in
\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{u,(G)}
.
∎

For a
G
-
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
and a
G
-bornological space
X
we will introduce the notion of
X
-controlled
G
-objects in
{\mathbf{C}}
. To that end, we recall that
a
G
-bornology on a
G
-set
X

is a
G
-invariant subset of
\mathcal{P}_{X}
which is closed
under forming finite unions, subsets, and which contains all one-point subsets.
A
G
-bornological space
is a pair
(X,{\mathcal{B}})
of a
G
-set
X
with a
G
-bornology
{\mathcal{B}}
whose elements will be called the bounded subsets of
X
. If
(X,{\mathcal{B}})
and
(X^{\prime},{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime})
are
G
-bornological spaces
and
f\colon X\to X^{\prime}
is an equivariant map of underlying
G
-sets, then
f
is called proper
if
f^{-1}({\mathcal{B}}^{\prime})\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}
.
By
G\mathbf{Born}
denote the category of very small
G
-bornological spaces and proper maps.
We refer to
[
BEKW20a
]
for more details. We will usually use the shorter notation
X
for
G
-bornological spaces. To any
G
-set
S
we can associate the following objects in
G\mathbf{Born}
.

1.

S_{min}
is
S
equipped with the minimal bornology consisting of the finite subsets.
The map
S\mapsto S_{min}
is functorial for morphisms of
G
-sets with finite fibres.

2.

S_{max}
is
S
equipped with the maximal bornology consisting of all subsets of
S
. We have a functor
G{\mathbf{Set}}\to G\mathbf{Born}
given on objects by
S\mapsto S_{max}
.

Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{Born}
.

Definition 2.11
.

A subset
L
of
X
is called locally finite if
B\cap L
is finite for every bounded subset in
X
.

The following definition is an expanded version of
[
BEc
, Def. 4.6]
. Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{Born}
.

Definition 2.12
.

A locally finite
X
-controlled
G
-object in
{\mathbf{C}}
is a triple

(C,\rho,\mu)
, where:

1.

(C,\rho)
is an object in
{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}
.

2.

\mu
is an invariant, finitely additive measure on
X
with values in projections in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}}(C)
such that the following properties hold:

(a)

\mu(X)={\mathtt{id}}_{C}
.

(b)

\mu(\{x\})
is effective and belongs to
{\mathbf{C}}
for all
x
in
X
.

(c)

C
is the orthogonal AV-sum of the images of the family of projections
(\mu(\{x\}))_{x\in X}
.

(d)

The subset
{\mathtt{supp}}(\mu)
of
X
is locally finite.

Remark 2.13
.

In this remark we explain Condition
2
in more detail. It first of all says that

\mu
is a function from the power set
\mathcal{P}_{X}
of
X
to the set of projections in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}}(C)
such that for all
Y,Z
in
\mathcal{P}_{X}
with
Y\subseteq Z
we have
\mu(Z)=\mu(Y)+\mu(Z\setminus Y)
. The invariance condition of
\mu
means that

g\cdot\mu(Y)=\mu(gY)

(2.3)

for all
g
in
G
and subsets
Y
of
X
.

Condition
2c
says that
\sum_{x\in X}\mu(\{x\})
converges strictly to
{\mathtt{id}}_{C}
.

The support of
\mu
is the subset

{\mathtt{supp}}(\mu)\coloneqq\{x\in X\>|\>\mu(\{x\})\not=0\}

of
X
.

The Conditions
2d
and
2b
together imply that
\mu(B)
belongs to
{{\mathbf{C}}}
for every bounded subset
B
of
X
.

∎

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and
X
be in
G\mathbf{Born}
.

Definition 2.14
.

We define
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})

as follows:

1.

objects: The objects of
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
are the locally finite
X
-controlled
G
-objects in
{\mathbf{C}}
.

2.

morphisms: The morphisms in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
are given by

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))\coloneqq{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}}((C,\rho),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}))\,.

3.

composition, involution and
G
-action: The composition, involution and the
G
-action are induced from
{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}
.

We have a fully faithful forgetful functor

{\mathcal{F}}\colon{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)\to{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}\,,\quad(C,\rho,\mu)\mapsto(C,\rho)\,.

(2.4)

Definition 2.15
.

1.

We define
{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})

as the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}

of objects which are isomorphic to objects of the form
{\mathcal{F}}((C,\rho,\mu))
for some object

(C,\rho,\mu)
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(Y_{min})
for some free
G
-set
Y
.

2.

We let
{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
denote the
G
-invariant ideal of
{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}
of morphisms belonging to
{{\mathbf{C}}}
.

3.

We define the quotient

\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\coloneqq\frac{{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}}{{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}}

(2.5)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
.

Lemma 2.16
.

The inclusion
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
presents
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}

as the multiplier category of
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
.

Proof.

We have a fully faithful forgetful functor
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
which sends
(C,\rho)
to
C
.
It induces a fully faithful functor
\mathbf{M}({\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\to\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
. This functor has an obvious factorization
\mathbf{M}({\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\to\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
, where the first functor is the identity on objects. Since the composition and the second functor are fully faithful, so is the first which is therefore an isomorphism.
∎

We let
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}
of objects

(C,\rho)
which belong to
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
or
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
. We furthermore let
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}:=\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}\cap{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}
.

Example 2.17
.

For
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}})
and

{\mathbf{C}}:=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})

we let
\hat{A}
be the object of
{\mathbf{C}}
given by

A
with the right-multiplication and the scalar product
\langle a,b\rangle_{\hat{A}}=a^{*}b
.
Left multiplication identifies
A
with
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(\hat{A})
.
For
g
in
G
we have a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear map

\kappa_{g}\colon\hat{A}\to\hat{A}
given by the action of
g^{-1}
on
A
, i.e.
\kappa_{g}(a):={}^{g^{-1}}a
. This map is a unitary multiplier isomorphism
\hat{A}\to g\hat{A}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
.
The family
\kappa:=(\kappa_{g})_{g\in G}
refines
\hat{A}
to an object
(\hat{A},\kappa)
of
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}
. Moreover, the identification

A\cong{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}}((\hat{A},\kappa))
is equivariant.

If
A
is unital, then the object
(\hat{A},\kappa)
belongs to

({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
and hence to
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
. In this case we
have a zig-zag of equivariant inclusions

A\to\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}\leftarrow\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\,,\quad A\to{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}\leftarrow{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\,.

The left functors sends

A
to the object
(\hat{A},\kappa)
and identifies
A
with
{\mathtt{End}}_{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}}((\hat{A},\kappa))

or
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}}((\hat{A},\kappa))
, respectively.

∎

Recall the definitions of a Morita equivalence
[
BEa
, 16.7]
, of a relative Morita equivalence
[
BEa
, Def. 17.1]
, and of a weak Morita equivalence
[
BEa
, Def. 18.3]
. In the equivariant case, an equivariant functor is a Morita equivalence or weak Morita equivalence if it has the respective property after forgetting the
G
-action. In addition we will need in the following a stronger version of the notion of a relative Morita equivalence which we call a split relative Morita equivalence. Let
\phi\colon{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{E}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.

Definition 2.18
.

We say that
\phi
is a split relative Morita equivalence if there exists a diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}/{\mathbf{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}/{\mathbf{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

(2.6)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
with horizontal exact sequences such that the two
right vertical functors are Morita equivalences between unital
C^{*}
-categories and
the functors
p
and
q
admit right-inverses.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.

Lemma 2.19
.

1.

\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
is a Morita equivalence.

2.

{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
is a split relative Morita equivalence.

3.

If
A
is in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg})
and
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
, then
A\to{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}

has a factorization
into the Morita equivalence
A\to({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
followed by the weak Morita equivalence
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
.

Proof.

We start with the Assertion
1
.
The inclusion
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
is fully faithful.
We will show that every object of
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
is a summand of an object of
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
. It suffices to show this for objects of
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
. Thus
let
(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
be an object of

({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
. Then using the fact that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits countable AV-sums one can construct an object
(C,\rho,\mu)
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(G_{min})

such that there exists an isometry
u:C^{\prime}\to C
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
representing an image of

\mu(\{e\})
. Thus for
C
we must take an orthogonal AV-sum of the family
(gC^{\prime})_{g\in G}
.
We consider
u
as an isometry
u:(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})\to(C,\rho)
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
with
(C,\rho)\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})
. It realizes
(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
as a summand of the
object
(C,\rho)
of
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
.
This finishes the proof of Assertion
1
.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G),u}_{\mathrm{std}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G),u}_{\mathrm{std},+}
be the
C^{*}
-categories obtained from

{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
by adjoining units to all non-unital objects.
We then have a diagram of exact sequences

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G),u}_{\mathrm{std}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G),u}_{\mathrm{std}}/{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G),u}_{\mathrm{std},+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{+}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G),u}_{\mathrm{std},+}/{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

Since the objects of
({\mathbf{C}}^{(u)})^{G}
are unital the right vertical morphism sends the objects of

{\mathbf{C}}^{(G),u}_{\mathrm{std},+}/{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
not belonging to
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G),u}_{\mathrm{std}}/{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
to zero objects.
We conclude that this right vertical morphism is a Morita equivalence.
Since the morphisms
u:(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})\to(C,\rho)
from the argument for Assertion
1
actually
belong to
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G),u}_{\mathrm{std},+}
we conclude that the middle arrow is a Morita equivalence, too.
The projections
p
and
p_{+}
have obvious splits.

In order to show Assertion
3

first note that if
(C,\rho)
is an object of
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
, then

C
is a finitely generated projective
A
-module and hence a summand
of a finite sum of copies of
A
. This implies that
A\to({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
is a Morita equivalence. In order to show that the second morphism

({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}

is a weak Morita equivalence we first observe that it is fully faithful. We then use that the morphisms in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
are compact operators between Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules. A compact operator can be approximated arbitrary well by an operator which factorizes over a finitely generated projective
A
-module, i.e., an object of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
.
This implies that the set of objects of
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
is weakly generating in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}
.
∎

Recall the definition of flasque
G
-
C^{*}
-categories
[
BEa
, Def. 11.3]
.

Lemma 2.20
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
admits countable AV-sums, then

\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
is flasque.

Proof.

We claim that

{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
also admits countable AV-sums. Then
\mathbf{M}({\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})
is flasque
by
[
BEa
, Ex. 11.5]
. We finally use Lemma

2.16
in order to conclude that
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
is flasque.

We show the claim.
We consider a countable family
(C_{i},\rho_{i})_{i\in I}
of objects in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
. For every
g
in
G
we can choose an AV-sum
(C_{g},(e^{gC_{i}}_{i})_{i\in I})
of the family
(gC_{i})_{i\in I}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
. We set
C\coloneqq C_{e}
and let
u_{g}\colon C_{g}\to gC
be the canonical multiplier unitary such that
g(e^{C_{i},*}_{i})u_{g}e^{gC_{i}}_{i}={\mathtt{id}}_{gC_{i}}
for all
i
in
I
.
Then
\rho\coloneqq(u_{g}\circ\oplus_{i\in I}\rho_{i})_{g\in G}
defines a multiplier cocycle on
C
such that we have
(C,\rho)\in{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}
. We now show that
(C,\rho)\in{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
.
By assumption, for every
i
in
I
we can refine the pair
(C_{i},\rho_{i})
to an object

(C_{i},\rho_{i},\mu_{i})
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X_{i})
for some free
G
-set
X_{i}
.
Then
(C,\rho,\mu)
belongs to
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
, where
X=\bigsqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}

and the measure
\mu
is given by

\mu(Y)\coloneqq\oplus_{i\in I}\mu_{i}(Y\cap X_{i})
for all subsets
Y
of
X
. Since
X
is again a free
G
-set we conclude that
(C,\rho)
belongs to
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
.

By construction, the sum
\sum_{i\in I}e^{C_{i}}_{i}e^{C_{i},*}_{i}
strictly converges to

{\mathtt{id}}_{(C,\rho)}
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
.
By
Lemma
2.16
it also strictly converges in
\mathbf{M}({\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})
. Therefore
the pair
(C,\rho)
represents the AV-sum of the family

(C_{i},\rho_{i})_{i\in I}
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
.
∎

In the following we describe the crossed product
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G

and a part of its multipliers explicitly.
We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is effectively additive and admits countable AV-sums. In the proof of Lemma
2.20
we saw that
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
also admits countable AV-sums.
The (non-equivariant) forgetful functor
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
defined by
(C,\rho)\mapsto C
is fully faithful
and therefore by
[
BEa
, Cor. 3.17]
and
[
BEa
, Cor. 9.1]
induces an orthogonal sum-preserving fully faithful normal functor

{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathrm{nu}}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathrm{nu}}{\mathbf{C}}
between
W^{*}
-categories (
[
BEa
, Def. 2.35]
,
[
BEa
, Cor. 9.1]
).

The objects of
{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\rtimes_{r}G
are the objects of
{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}
. Following
[
BEa
, Def. 12.9]
we
define
{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\rtimes_{r}G
as the completion of the image of a functor

\sigma\colon{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes^{\mathrm{alg}}G\to\mathbf{L}^{2}(G,{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})
.
The
W^{*}
-category
\mathbf{L}^{2}(G,{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})
has the same objects as
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
. Since
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
admits orthogonal AV-sums and
{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathrm{nu}}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathrm{nu}}{\mathbf{C}}
is fully faithful
we can identify, using
[
BEa
, (12.2)]
, the morphism spaces of
\mathbf{L}^{2}(G,{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})
with

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(G,{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})}((C,\rho),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}))\cong{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathrm{nu}}{\mathbf{C}}}(\bigoplus_{g\in G}C,\bigoplus_{g\in G}C^{\prime})\,,

where
(\bigoplus_{g\in G}C,(e_{l})_{l\in G})
and

(\bigoplus_{g\in G}C^{\prime},(e^{\prime}_{l})_{l\in G})

represent orthogonal AV-sums of the constant families
(C)_{g\in G}
and
(C^{\prime})_{g\in G}
, respectively.
Using this identification of morphism spaces
we now describe the functor
\sigma
explicitly.
On objects
\sigma
acts as the identity. Furthermore,
\sigma
sends
a morphism
(f,g)\colon(C,\rho)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes^{\mathrm{alg}}G
to

\sigma(f,g)\coloneqq\sum_{l\in G}e^{\prime}_{l}\>(lg)\cdot f\>e_{lg}^{*}\colon\bigoplus_{g\in G}C\to\bigoplus_{g\in G}C^{\prime}\,,

(2.7)

see
[
BEa
, (12.8)]
.

If
\mathbf{L}
is a closed wide subcategory of a
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{H}
, then the idealizer of
\mathbf{L}
in
\mathbf{H}
is the maximal wide subcategory of
\mathbf{H}
containing
\mathbf{L}
as an ideal. It consists of all morphisms of
\mathbf{H}
which preserve
\mathbf{L}
by left- and right composition.

Definition 2.21
.

We let
{\mathbf{U}}
denote the idealizer of
{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\rtimes_{r}G
in
\mathbf{L}^{2}(G,{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})
.

We will understand
\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\rtimes_{r}G)
as the
idempotent completion relative to
{\mathbf{U}}
, see
[
BEa
, Def. 17.5]
.
Therefore objects in
\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\rtimes_{r}G)
are triples
(C,\rho,p)
, where
p
is a projection on
(C,\rho)
in
{\mathbf{U}}
.

Using formula (
2.7
), we see that
\sigma
extends canonically to a functor

\sigma\colon\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes^{\mathrm{alg}}G\to{\mathbf{U}}

given by the same formula. By the universal property of the maximal crossed product it further extends to
a morphism

\sigma\colon\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes G\to{\mathbf{U}}\,.

(2.8)

Let
\phi:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
be a morphism in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.

Definition 2.22

(
[
BEa
, Def. 3.11]

)
.

The morphism
\phi
is called non-degenerate if for every two objects
C_{0},C_{1}
of
{\mathbf{C}}
the linear subspaces

\phi({\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C_{1})){\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}}(\phi(C_{0}),\phi(C_{1}))
and

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}}(\phi(C_{0}),\phi(C_{1}))\phi({\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C_{0}))
are dense in
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}}(\phi(C_{0}),\phi(C_{1}))
.

We will consider the chain of subcategories

C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{add}}\subseteq C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\omega\mathrm{add},\mathrm{eadd}}\subseteq C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg}}\subseteq C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ ,

(2.9)

where

1.

C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg}}
is the wide subcategory of
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}

of non-degenerate morphisms,

2.

C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\omega\mathrm{add},\mathrm{eadd}}
is full subcategory of
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg}}

of effectively additive objects which admit countable AV-sums,

3.

C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{add}}
is full subcategory of
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg}}
of objects which admit all small AV-sums.

By
[
BEa
, Prop. 3.16]
a non-degenerate morphism
\phi\colon{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
naturally induces a morphism

\mathbf{M}\phi\colon\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}\to\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
of the associated multiplier categories and, again by non-degeneracy, it restricts to a unital morphism

\phi^{u}\colon{\mathbf{C}}^{u}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime,u}
of full subcategories of unital objects. This implies that the constructions of
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
,

\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
,
\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
,
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
,
({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}
and
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}
extend to functors on
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
. Further,
\phi
induces a morphism
\mathbf{L}^{2}(G,{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)})\to\mathbf{L}^{2}(G,{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{\prime,(G)})
(see the proof of
[
BEa
, Lem. 12.10]
) and hence
{\mathbf{U}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G
also extend to such functors.

3
\bm{G}
-bornological coarse spaces and
\bm{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}}

As in Section
2
, we fix
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.
In the present section we recall the construction
of the equivariant coarse homology theory

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\colon G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}

introduced in
[
BEc
]
which will give rise to the second version of the analytic
K
-homology
K_{\mathbf{C}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}
described in Definition
4.9
.

In order to define the functor
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
must be effectively additive (Definition
2.3
).
If
{\mathbf{C}}
also admits countable AV-sums (Definition
2.2
), then
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
is an equivariant coarse homology theory. Finally, in order to ensure strong additivity of
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
by
[
BEc
, Thm.11.1]
we must assume the existence of all very small AV-sums.

Example 3.1
.

For
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
the category
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
admits all small AV-sums and is idempotent complete, hence in particular effectively additive. It therefore satisfies all the conditions listed above.
∎

Subsets of
X\times X
will be called entourages on
X
.
The set
\mathcal{P}_{X\times X}
of all entourages is a monoid with involution, where
the composition of the entourages
U
and
V
is the entourage

U\circ V\coloneqq{\mathtt{pr}}_{14}[(U\times V)\cap(X\times{\mathtt{diag}}(X)\times X)]\,,

the unit is the entourage
{\mathtt{diag}}(X)
, and the involution is given by the formula

U^{*}\coloneqq\{(y,x)\mid(x,y)\in U\}\,.

The monoid
\mathcal{P}_{X\times X}
acts on
\mathcal{P}_{X}
by

(U,Y)\mapsto U[Y]\coloneqq{\mathtt{pr}}_{1}[U\cap(X\times Y)]\,.

(3.1)

A
G
-coarse structure
{\mathcal{C}}
on a
G
-set
X
is by definition a
G
-invariant submonoid of
\mathcal{P}_{X\times X}
which is
closed under taking subsets, applying the involution, and forming finite unions, and in which the subset of
G
-invariant entourages
{\mathcal{C}}^{G}
is cofinal with respect to the inclusion relation.
A
G
-coarse space is a pair
(X,{\mathcal{C}})
of a
G
-set and a
G
-coarse structure. If
(X,{\mathcal{C}})
and
(X^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})
are two
G
-coarse spaces and
f\colon X\to X^{\prime}
is an equivariant map of the underlying
G
-sets, then
f
is controlled if
(f\times f)({\mathcal{C}})\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
. Finally,
a coarse structure
{\mathcal{C}}
is compatible with a bornology
{\mathcal{B}}
if

{\mathcal{C}}[{\mathcal{B}}]\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}
.

The category
G\mathbf{BC}
of
G
-bornological coarse spaces was introduced in
[
BEKW20a
]
. Its objects are triples
(X,{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{B}})
of a very small

G
-set
X
with a
G
-coarse structure
{\mathcal{C}}
and a
G
-bornology
{\mathcal{B}}
which is compatible with
{\mathcal{C}}
. Morphisms are maps of
G
-sets which are controlled and proper.
We usually use the shorter notation
X
for
G
-bornological coarse spaces.
We refer to
[
BEKW20a
]
and
[
BE20b
]
for details about this category.

Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{BC}
. Then we can consider the category

\mathbf{C}^{G}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)\coloneqq\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{BG}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)

(3.2)

in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
, where
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
is as introduced in Definition
2.14
. Explicity,
\mathbf{C}^{G}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)

is the wide subcategory of
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
consisting of the
G
-invariant morphisms, i.e., morphisms
A
satisfying
g\cdot A=A
for all
g
in
G
, where the
G
-action is given by formula (
2.2
).
Note that this construction does not use the coarse structure yet, but this will be the case in the following.

If
Y,Y^{\prime}
are two subsets of
X
and
U
is an entourage of
X
, then we say that
Y^{\prime}
is
U
-separated from
Y
if
Y^{\prime}\cap U[Y]=\emptyset
, see (
3.1
) for the definition of the
U
-thickening
U[Y]
of
Y
.
We say that a morphism
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{C}^{G}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
is
U
-controlled if

\mu^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})A\mu(Y)=0
for all pairs of subsets
Y^{\prime},Y
of
X
such that
Y^{\prime}
is
U
-separated from
Y
.

Definition 3.2
.

We define
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
as follows:

1.

objects: The objects of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
are the objects of
\mathbf{C}^{G}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
.

2.

morphisms: The space of morphisms
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))

is the closed subspace of
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{C}^{G}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))
generated by those
morphisms which are
U
-controlled for some coarse entourage
U
of
X
.

3.

composition and involution: The composition and the involution of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
are inherited from
\mathbf{C}^{G}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
.

One must check that the composition defined in
Point
3
preserves the morphism spaces
defined in Point
2
. We refer to

[
BEc
, Sec. 4]
for the argument.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
be effectively additive.

Definition 3.3
.

We define a functor

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\colon G\mathbf{BC}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}

as follows:

1.

objects: The functor
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
sends
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
to
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
.

2.
