of
K
and set
Z\coloneqq K\setminus\mathrm{int}(Y)
. Then
(Y,Z)
is a closed decomposition of
K
.

By Lemma
5.12
we can consider
Y
,
Z
and
Y\cap Z
as objects in
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{scl}}
with the scale induced from
(X,\tau)
.
We then have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}(Y\cap Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447ptp_{Y\cap Z}}
\textstyle{K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{p_{Z}}
\textstyle{K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(Y\cap Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(K)}
\textstyle{K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\ p_{Y}}
\textstyle{K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{(K,\tau_{K})}}

(9.2)

where we omitted the symbol for the scale at various places.
Since
Y,Z
and
Y\cap Z
are homotopy equivalent in
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{scl}}
to
G
-finite
G
-simplicial complexes of dimension
<n
with stabilizers in
{\mathcal{F}}
their Paschke morphisms are equivalences by the induction hypothesis. Since the functors
K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}
and
K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}

are excisive for this closed decomposition (for
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
we use
[
BEL
, Prop. 5.1.2]
) the inner and the outer square are push-out squares.
Altogether we can then conclude that the Paschke morphism
p_{(K,\tau_{K})}
is an equivalence, too.
∎

In order to prepare the proof of Theorem
1.4
.
3
we replace the Paschke morphism
p
in Proposition
9.3
by the locally finite version
p^{\mathrm{lf}}
with target
K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
. In Assumption
9.3
.
1
we further replace
G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}
by
G_{{\mathcal{F}}}{\mathbf{Set}}
. Note that this is a stronger assumption.
Let
(X,\tau)
be in
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{scl}}
. The argument for Proposition
9.3

then also shows the following statement.

Proposition 9.4
.

Assume:

1.

The Paschke morphism
{p^{\mathrm{lf}}_{(S,\tau_{disc})}\colon K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(S)\to K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(S)}
is an equivalence for every countable

S
in
G_{{\mathcal{F}}}{\mathbf{Set}}
.

2.

X
is homotopy equivalent to a countable, finite-dimensional
G
-simplicial complex with stabilizers in
{\mathcal{F}}
and with structures induced by its spherical path metrics.

Then the Paschke morphism
{p^{\mathrm{lf}}_{(X,\tau)}\colon K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\to K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Using the stronger Assumption
9.4
.
1
instead of Assumption
9.3
.
1
one can redo the proof of Proposition
9.3
for
p^{\mathrm{lf}}
avoiding the step where we decompose the zero-dimensional complex
K
into a finite union of
G
-orbits.∎

In the following lemma we show that Assumption
9.3
.
1
implies Assumption
9.4
.
1

provided
G
is finite and
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small orthogonal AV-sums.

Lemma 9.5
.

We assume that
G
is finite and that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small orthogonal AV-sums.
If
the Paschke morphism
p_{(T,\tau_{disc})}
is an equivalence for every

T
in
G\mathbf{Orb}
, then the Paschke morphism
{p^{\mathrm{lf}}_{(S,\tau_{disc})}}
is an equivalence for every countable

S
in
G{\mathbf{Set}}
.

Proof.

The functor

K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
sends countable disjoint unions into
products.
Hence we have an equivalence

K^{G,\mathrm{An}{,\mathrm{lf}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(S_{disc})\simeq\prod_{T\in G\backslash S}K^{G,\mathrm{An}{,\mathrm{lf}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(T_{disc})\,.

(9.3)

If
G
is finite, then we have an equality
G_{can,max}=G_{max,max}
. Recall the notion of the free union from
[
BEKW20a
, Ex. 2.16]
.
As in the proof
of
[
BEKW20a
, Lem. 3.13]
, by exploiting the equality
G_{can,max}=G_{max,max}
, we have an isomorphism

S_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,{max}}\cong(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{T\in G\backslash S}T_{min,min})\otimes G_{can,max}\cong\bigsqcup_{T\in G\backslash S}^{\mathrm{free}}(T_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})\,.

(9.4)

in
G\mathbf{BC}
.
The additional assumption on
{\mathbf{C}}
implies that
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
is strongly additive by

[
BEc
, Thm. 11.1]
, see also Theorem
3.5
. It therefore sends free unions to products. Applying now
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
to (
9.4
) and
using Definition
4.9
we consequently have an equivalence

K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(S_{min,min,disc})\simeq\prod_{T\in G\backslash S}K^{G,\mathrm{An}{,\mathrm{lf}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(T_{min,min,disc})

(9.5)

arising in the following way:

\displaystyle K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(S_{min,min,disc})\

\displaystyle=\ K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(S_{min,min,disc}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{!}}}{{\simeq}}\ \Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}(S_{min,min,disc})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{ccdscqcasdcascasdca}}}}{{\simeq}}\ \Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\Big(\bigsqcup_{T\in G\backslash S}^{\mathrm{free}}(T_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})\Big)

\displaystyle\simeq\ \Sigma\prod_{T\in G\backslash S}K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(T_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{!}}}{{\simeq}}\ \prod_{T\in G\backslash S}K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(T_{min,min,disc})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\prod_{T}p_{(T,\tau_{disc})}}}}{{\simeq}}\>\>\>\>\>\prod_{T\in G\backslash S}K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(T_{min,min,disc})

\displaystyle\simeq\ \prod_{T\in G\backslash S}K^{G,\mathrm{An}{,\mathrm{lf}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(T_{min,min,disc})\,.

Here we use
[
BEKW20a
, Prop. 9.35]
for the equivalences marked by
!
.
By naturality of the Paschke transformation, under the equivalences (
9.3
) and (
9.5
) the Paschke morphism
p^{{\mathrm{lf}}}_{(S,\tau_{disc})}
corresponds to the product of the Paschke morphisms
p_{(T,\tau_{disc})}
for the
G
-orbits
T
in
S
.
If the latter are equivalences, then
p_{(S,\tau_{disc})}
is an equivalence.
∎

At the moment we do not know whether this lemma generalizes to infinite groups, possibly with restrictions on allowed stabilizers.

Combining Proposition
9.4
with Lemma
9.5
we get the following result.

Corollary 9.6
.

Assume:

1.

G
is finite.

2.

{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small AV-sums.

3.

The Paschke morphism
p_{(T,\tau_{disc})}
is an equivalence for every

T
in
G\mathbf{Orb}
.

4.

X
is homotopy equivalent to a countable, finite-dimensional
G
-simplicial complex with structures induced by its spherical path metric.

Then the Paschke morphism
p^{\mathrm{lf}}_{(X,\tau)}\colon K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\to K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
is an equivalence.

Remark 9.7
.

We can not expect that the Paschke morphism is an equivalence for spaces which are not proper
G
-spaces. More precisely, we do not expect that Assumption
9.3
.
1
is satisfied if
{\mathcal{F}}
contains infinite subgroups.

Indeed, assume that
S=G/H
with
H
infinite.
Then we have

\displaystyle K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}((G/H)_{min,min,disc})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{def.}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}((G/H)_{min,min,disc})))

(9.6)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}((G/H)_{min,min})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{def.}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}((G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle 0\,,

where the equivalence
(1)
is an instance of
[
BEKW20a
, Prop. 9.35]
since
(G/H)_{min,min,disc}
is discrete.
In order to see the equivalence
(2)
we use that the functor
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
is continuous: We refer
to
[
BEKW20a
, Def. 5.15]
for the definition of this notion and to
[
BEc
, Thm. 6.3]
for the fact.
Continuity implies that the value of
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X)
for any
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
is given as a colimit of the values

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(L)
over the locally finite invariant subsets
L
of
X
.
We now observe that if
H
is infinite, then

(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max}
does not admit any non-empty invariant locally finite subset.
Indeed, if
L
would be such a subset, then on the one hand

(eH\times G)\cap L
is finite, but the infinite group
H
acts freely on this set on the other hand.

In contrast, the spectrum

K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}((G/H)_{disc})\simeq\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}((G/H)_{disc}),\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})

does not vanish in general.
As an example we consider the case
G=H
, and we further specialize to

{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}^{G}_{c}(A)
for a unital
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
.
By Proposition
11.15

we have an equivalence

K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}((G/H)_{disc})\simeq\Sigma\mathrm{KK}^{G}({\mathbb{C}},A)\,.

We claim that this spectrum is non-trivial if we take
A={\mathbb{C}}
with the trivial
G
-action.
Indeed, in this case we have the class
{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{kk}^{G}({\mathbb{C}})}
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{0}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}})
and

{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{kk}^{G}({\mathbb{C}})}\simeq 0
if and only if
\mathrm{KK}^{G}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}})\simeq 0
.
Since
\mathrm{kk}^{G}({\mathbb{C}})
is the tensor unit of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}

we have
\mathrm{KK}^{G}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}})\simeq 0
if and only if
\mathrm{KK}^{G}\simeq 0
.
But since

\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(G),{\mathbb{C}})\simeq K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Alg}}({\mathbb{C}})\simeq KU

by
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.23]

this never happens.

∎

Let
(X,\tau)
be in
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{scl}}
. Then we have the multiplication map (
7.19
)

\mu^{\mathbf{Q}}_{X}\colon C_{0}(X)\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{\tau}(X)\to{\mathbf{Q}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\,.

We add a superscript
\mathbf{Q}
since we are going to consider other versions of this map which will be distinguished by other choices for this superscript. We further omitted the scale
\tau
from the notation in order to simplify.
The main ingredient in the verification that
\mu^{\mathbf{Q}}_{X}
is well-defined was
Lemma
6.2

saying that for a morphism

A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\tau}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})

we have
\phi^{\prime}(f)A-A\phi(f)\in{{\mathbf{C}}}
for all
f
in
C_{0}(X)
.
If
X
is discrete,
then we actually have

\phi^{\prime}(f)A-A\phi(f)=0
for all such
f
.
This has the effect that in the construction of
\mu_{(X,\tau)}
in (
7.19
) on morphisms (see Item
2
in the list below (
7.19
)) we do not have to go to the quotients in order to ensure compatibility with the composition.

From now one we assume that
(X,\tau)
is discrete.
Using the observation just made we can lift
\mu^{\mathbf{Q}}_{X}
to a multiplication map

\mu^{{\mathbf{D}}}_{X}\colon C_{0}(X)\otimes{\mathbf{D}}_{\tau}(X)\to\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\,,\quad f\otimes A\mapsto fA\,,

where
{\mathbf{D}}_{\tau}(X)
is defined in (
7.2
).
Using in addition Lemma
6.2
and the definition (
7.3
) of
{\mathbf{C}}_{\tau}(X)

the map
\mu^{{\mathbf{D}}}_{X}
restricts to a map
\mu^{{\mathbf{C}}}_{X}
so that
we get a morphism of exact sequences in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{0}(X)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{\tau}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{X}^{{\mathbf{C}}}}
\textstyle{C_{0}(X)\otimes{\mathbf{D}}_{\tau}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{X}^{{\mathbf{D}}}}
\textstyle{C_{0}(X)\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{\tau}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{X}^{\mathbf{Q}}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

(9.7)

Here in the upper line we used (
7.4
) and that
C_{0}(X)\otimes-
(involving the maximal tensor product) preserves exact sequences of
C^{*}
-categories by

[
BEL
, Prop. 7.23.1]
.

In the definition (

In particular, the Paschke morphism for

is an equivalence if and only if

.

.
We assume that

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})

Proposition 9.10
.

If

The whole of Section

10
Verification of the Paschke equivalence on
\bm{G}
-orbits

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is effectively additive and admits countable AV-sums.
We fix a finite subgroup
H
of
G
and consider the
G
-set
G/H
in
G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}
.
As a first step we construct an explicit functor
\Theta
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and show in Proposition
10.3
that

p^{{\mathbf{C}}}_{G/H}
is an equivalence if and only if
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Theta)
is an equivalence.
In the second step we then verify in Proposition
10.6
that
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Theta)
is an equivalence.

We form
the
G
-bornological coarse space

(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max}
.
It contains
the locally finite subset

X\coloneqq G(H,e)\,,

(10.1)

the
G
-orbit of the point
(H,e)
in
G/H\times G
.
Note that, in contrast to the example in Remark
9.7
, here the group
H
is finite.
We equip
X

with the bornological coarse structures induced from
(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max}
. The map
g\mapsto g(H,e)
is a
G
-equivariant bijection of sets between
G
and
X
which will be used below to name points and subsets of
X
. The induced bornology on
X
is the minimal one. The induced
G
-coarse structure
reflects the information about the finite subgroup
H
and is in general smaller than the canonical coarse structure on
G
. For instance, the subset
H
is a coarse component of
X
.

The following lemma states
that the inclusion
X\to(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max}

is a continuous equivalence in the sense of

[
BEKW20b
, Sec. 7]
.

Lemma 10.1
.

The inclusion
X\to(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max}
induces an equivalence

E(X)\to E((G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})
for any continuous equivariant coarse homology theory
E
.

Proof.

For
Y
in
G\mathbf{BC}
we let
\mathrm{LF}(Y)
denote the poset of
G
-invariant locally finite subsets.
Let
L
be in
\mathrm{LF}((G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})
.
Then
L_{0}\coloneqq L\cap(\{H\}\times G)
is a finite set which we will sometimes consider as a subset of
G
. Since every
G
-orbit in
L
meets
L_{0}
we have
L=GL_{0}
.

We claim that
for every
L
in
\mathrm{LF}((G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})
the inclusion
i\colon X\to L\cup X
is a coarse equivalence.
Indeed, we can construct
an inverse equivalence
p\colon L\cup X\to X
. The
map
p
is the identity on
X
, and it sends a point
g(H,h)
(with
h
in
L_{0}\setminus\{e\}
) in
L\setminus X
to
g(H,e)
in
X
. Then
p\circ i={\mathtt{id}}_{X}

and
i\circ p
is close to the identity. In order to see the second assertion note that
L_{0}
is finite and therefore
{\mathtt{diag}}(G/H)\times\{(gh,g)\>|\>h\in L_{0},g\in G\}
is a coarse entourage
of
(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max}
. We then use that

({\mathtt{id}}_{X},i\circ p)({\mathtt{diag}}(L\cup X))\subseteq{\mathtt{diag}}(G/H)\times\{(gh,g)\>|\>h\in L_{0},g\in G\}\,.

If
E
is any equivariant coarse homology theory,
then the canonical morphism

E(X)\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{L\in\mathrm{LF}((G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})}E(L)

is an equivalence since the elements of
\mathrm{LF}((G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})
containing
X
are cofinal and for those elements the
inclusions
X\to L
are coarse equivalences. Since we assume in addition that

E
is continuous, the canonical morphism

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{L\in\mathrm{LF}((G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})}E(L)\to E((G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})

is an equivalence. Hence the composition of these equivalences is an equivalence

E(X)\to E((G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})\,.\qed

Using the inclusion

i\colon{X}\to G/H\times G\to Z_{0}

(10.2)

(see (
6.13
) for the notation
Z_{0}
as a subspace of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\tau}((G/H)_{min,min})\otimes G_{can,max}
and (
7.3
) for
{\mathbf{C}}_{\tau}(G/H)
) we get an inclusion

i_{*}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to{\mathbf{C}}_{\tau}({(G/H)_{min,min}})

(10.3)

which identifies
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)

with the full subcategory of objects of
{\mathbf{C}}_{\tau}({(G/H)_{min,min}})
supported on
i(X)
.

In the following
\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)

is
the relative idempotent completion using the embedding of
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H
as an ideal into
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H
,
[
BEa
, Def. 17.5]
. In order to keep the notation readable
4
4

4

i.e., to avoid symbols like
\mathrm{Idem}^{\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)
, in contrast to the reference we will not indicate the bigger unital category by a superscript.
Recall the notation for morphisms in crossed products from
[
Bun
, Def. 5.1]
. In the formulas below, e.g. in order to interpret the term
\mu(H)
in (
10.5
), we use the bijection between
G
and
X
mentioned above.

Definition 10.2
.

We define the functor

\Theta\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)

(10.4)

as follows:

1.

objects:
\Theta
sends the object
(C,\rho,\mu)
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
to the object

(C,\rho,{\pi})
in the category
\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)
,
where the orthogonal projection
\pi
on
(C,\rho)
is given by

\pi:=\frac{1}{|H|}\sum_{h\in H}(\mu(H),h)\,.

(10.5)

2.

morphisms:
\Theta
sends
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
to the morphism

\pi^{\prime}(A,e)\pi\colon(C,\rho,\pi)\to(C^{\prime},{\rho^{\prime}},\pi^{\prime})

in
\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)
.

Note that
A\colon C\to C^{\prime}
belongs to
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
, but since
H
is a finite and hence bounded subset of
X
,
the projection
\mu(H)
belongs to
{\mathbf{C}}
by the local finiteness of
(C,\rho,\mu)
. Therefore
\pi^{\prime}(A,e)\pi
belongs to the ideal

\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)
as stated.
In order to see that
\Theta
is compatible with the composition
note that the relations
A\mu(H)=\mu^{\prime}(H)A
(since
H
is a coarse component of
X
) and
h\cdot A=A
for all
h
in
H
imply that

(A,e)\pi=\pi^{\prime}(A,e)
.

We then have a commutative diagram

Lemma 10.4
.

The morphism
\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},i_{*})\colon\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X))\to\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{C}}_{\tau}(G/H))
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Using the definitions

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(-):=K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(-))
and
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(-)\coloneqq\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-)
and (
10.2
) we can rewrite the morphism in question as

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X)\to K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}((G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})\to K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}({\mathbf{C}}_{\tau}(G/H)){\,,}

where the morphisms are induced by the canonical inclusion of
C^{*}
-categories.
We have seen in the proof of Proposition
7.1
that the second morphism (the left vertical morphism in (
))
5
5

5

one must apply this formula for

(G/H)_{min,min}
inplace of
X
is an equivalence. The first morphism is induced by the inclusion
X\to(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max}
. Since
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
is a continuous equivariant coarse homology theory it is an equivalence by Lemma
10.1
.
∎

10.3
. We define

.
In view of (
) and Lemma
10.4

we conclude that

p^{\prime}\simeq p^{{\mathbf{C}}}_{G/H}\,.

The second and the last middle square commute by the associativity of the composition in
\mathrm{KK}^{H}
and
\mathrm{KK}
, respectively. The first and the third square commute since

\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
and
-\rtimes H
are functors. In order to see that

r^{G}_{H}
and
j^{H}
are equivalences we observe that

\iota
and
\epsilon
are instances of the units of the adjunctions in
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.23.1 & 2]
and that

r^{G}_{H}
and
j^{H}
are precisely the corresponding equivalences
of mapping spectra.

) with (
) we get a commutative square

\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\prime}=\mu^{\prime}\circ\delta^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(G/H),{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{j^{H}\circ r^{G}_{H}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\prime\prime}:=\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(\mu^{\prime}\rtimes H)\circ\delta^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)}

We therefore have an equivalence

p^{\prime\prime}\simeq p^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq14toirj13o}}}{{\simeq}}p^{{\mathbf{C}}}_{G/H}\,.

(10.47)

By construction the morphism
p^{\prime\prime}
is induced
by an explicit functor

\Theta^{\prime}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H\,.

(10.48)

Inserting all definitions we see that
\Theta^{\prime}
is given by follows:

1.

objects:
\Theta^{\prime}
sends the object
(C,\rho,\mu)
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
to

(C,\rho)
in
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H
.

2.

morphisms: The functor
\Theta^{\prime}
sends a morphism
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
to the morphism

{\pi^{\prime}A\pi}\colon(C,\rho)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})

in
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H
, where
\pi
is as in (
10.5
).

The observations made after the Definition
10.2
of
\Theta
also show that
\Theta^{\prime}
is well-defined. Note, however, that
\Theta^{\prime}
is not full.

Let

c\colon\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H\to\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)

be the inclusion into the relative idempotent completion.
We consider the two functors

\Theta,c\circ\Theta^{\prime}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)

in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

Recall the notion of a Murray von Neumann (MvN) equivalence
[
BEa
, Def. 17.12]
.

Lemma 10.5
.

There is a MvN equivalence
\Theta\to c\circ\Theta^{\prime}
.
In particular

K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Theta)\simeq K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(c\circ\Theta^{\prime})\colon K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X))\to K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H))\,.

(10.49)

Proof.

Applying
[
BEa
, Rem. 17.13]
to the inclusion of
\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)
as an ideal into
\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)

it suffices to construct a natural transformation

v\colon\Theta\to c\circ\Theta^{\prime}
implemented
by a family
(v_{(C,\rho,\mu)})_{(C,\rho,\mu)\in\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}
of partial isometries in

\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)
.

We define

v_{(C,\rho,\mu)}\colon(C,\rho,p)\to(C,\rho)
to be the canonical inclusion.
Since the formulas for the actions of
\Theta
and
\Theta^{\prime}
on morphisms are equal, this family is indeed a natural transformation.
∎

We continue with the proof of Proposition
10.3
.
Since the homological functor
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
is Morita invariant by
[
BEa
, Thm. 16.18]

the morphism

K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(c)\colon K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)\to K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H))

is an equivalence by
[
BEa
, Prop. 17.8]
. Therefore

K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Theta)
is an equivalence if and only if
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Theta^{\prime})
is an equivalence.
The Proposition
10.3
now follows from
the
combination of (
10.47
) and the fact that
p^{\prime\prime}
is induced by the functor
\Theta^{\prime}
.
∎

Recall the Definition
10.2
of the functor
\Theta
and that
H
denotes a finite subgroup of
G
.
The next proposition finishes the proof of
Proposition
9.10
and hence of Theorem
1.4
.

Proposition 10.6
.

K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Theta)
is an equivalence.

Proof.

The proof of Proposition
10.6
is based on the factorization of
\Theta
as described by the commutative diagram (
10.51
). The functors in this diagram will all induce equivalences in
K
-theory, but for different reasons. The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Proposition
10.6
which is split in several lemmas.

Lemma 10.7
.

The functor
\Theta
is fully faithful.

Proof.

Recall that
X=G(H,e)
is a subspace of
(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max}
, see (
10.1
). Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
and
(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
be objects of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
.
Then
\Theta(C,\rho,\mu)=(C,\rho,\pi)
with
\pi
given by (
10.5
), and similarly
\Theta(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})=(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\pi^{\prime})
.
Let

B\colon(C,\rho,\pi)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\pi^{\prime})

by any morphism. We can write
B=\sum_{h\in H}(B_{h},h)
, where
B_{h}\colon C\to C^{\prime}
. The condition

\pi^{\prime}B\pi=B
implies that

B_{h}=\mu^{\prime}(H)B_{e}\mu(H)
and
h\cdot B_{e}=B_{e}
for every
h
in
H
. Using
[
BEa
, Lem. 7.8]

we can define the morphism

A\coloneqq\frac{1}{|H|}\sum_{g\in G}g\cdot B_{e}\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})

(10.50)

in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
.
Then
\Theta(A)=B
.
The formula (
10.50
) defines an inverse of
\Theta
on the level of morphisms.
∎

In
\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)

we consider the full subcategory
{\mathbf{D}}
of objects of the form

(C,\rho,(\mu(H),e))
, where
(C,\rho,\mu)
is in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
. We let furthermore

{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)
on objects of the form
(C,\rho,(\mu(Z),e))
,
where
(C,\rho,\mu)
is in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
for some free
G
-set
Y
and
H
-invariant subset
Z
of
Y
. By
\Lambda
we denote the
canonical inclusion of
{\mathbf{D}}
into
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
. Below, the idempotent completions of
{\mathbf{D}}
and
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
are formed relative to the full subcategories of
\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)
on objects from
{\mathbf{D}}
or
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
, respectively.
Then we have the following diagram

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Idem}(\Lambda)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{D}}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)\,,}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Xi}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}

(10.51)

where
\Delta
is again the canonical inclusion.
The upper line is then a factorization of
\Theta
as indicated.

In the following we will show that all solid morphisms in (
10.51
) induce equivalences after applying
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
.
It is clear that this implies that
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Theta)
is an equivalence.
To this end
we use that
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
sends unitary equivalences, Morita equivalences, relative idempotent completions, and weak Morita equivalences (see
[
BEa
, Sec. 16–18]
) to equivalences.
In the following lemmas we argue case by case that all solid arrows in the above diagram have one of these properties.

Recall the notion of a relative idempotent completion
[
BEa
, Def. 17.5]
.

Lemma 10.8
.

\Xi
and
\Psi
are relative idempotent completions.

Proof.

This is true by construction.
∎

Therefore
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Xi)
and
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Psi)
are equivalences by
[
BEa
, Prop. 17.4]
.

Lemma 10.9
.

\Delta

is a unitary equivalence in the sense of
[
BEa
, Def. 3.19]
.

Proof.

It suffices to show the claim that every object of
\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{D}}^{\prime})
admits a unitary isomorphism to an object of

\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)
in
\mathrm{Idem}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H)
.
Since

{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
in particular contains all objects of the form

(C,\rho,(\mu(Y),e))
for all free
G
-sets
Y
and all
(C,\rho,\mu)
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
,
every object of
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}({\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H
is unitarily isomorphic in
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\rtimes H
to an object of

{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
.
This implies the claim by going over to the relative idempotent completions.
∎

Since
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
is a homological functor by
[
BEa
, Thm. 14.4]
the morphism
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Delta)
is an equivalence.

Lemma 10.10
.

\Phi
is a Morita equivalence.

Proof.

The functor
\mathrm{Idem}(\Lambda)
is fully faithful by construction. Since

\Theta
is fully faithful by Lemma
10.7
and
\Delta
is also fully faithful, we can conclude that
\Phi
is fully faithful, too.

Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
. Then we define

U:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{|H|}}\sum_{h\in H}(\mu({\{h\}}),h)

in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}({(C,\rho,(\mu(H),e)))}
.
We calculate that

UU^{*}=(\mu({\{e\}}),e)\,,\quad U^{*}U=\pi\,,

where
\pi
is as in (
10.5
).
This calculation shows that the projection

\pi
is MvN-equivalent to
(\mu({\{e\}}),e)
.
For
h
in
H
we consider the unitary
V_{h}:=(\mu(H),h^{-1})
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}((C,\rho,{({\mu}(H),e))})
.
Then

V_{h}(\mu(\{e\}),e)V_{h}^{*}=(\mu(\{h\}),e)\,.

So the projection
(\mu({\{h\}}),e)
is also MvN-equivalent to
\pi
for every
h
in
H
.
Since the projections

((\mu(\{h\}),e))_{h\in H}
are mutually orthogonal and

\sum_{h\in H}(\mu(\{h\}),e)=(\mu(H),e)

we see that any object of
{\mathbf{D}}
is an orthogonal summand of a finite orthogonal sum of objects in the essential image of
\Phi
. This implies that also every object of
\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{D}})
is an orthogonal summand of a finite orthogonal sum of objects in the essential image of
\Phi
.
∎

Since
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
is Morita invariant by
[
BEa
, Thm. 16.18]
the morphism
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Phi)
is an equivalence.

Lemma 10.11
.

\Lambda
is a weak Morita equivalence.

Proof.

The functor
\Lambda
is fully faithful by definition.
Furthermore,
{\mathbf{D}}
is unital since the identity on an object
(C,\rho,(\mu(H),e))
of
{\mathbf{D}}
is given by
(\mu(H),e)
and
\mu(H)
is in
{\mathbf{C}}
.
It remains to show that the set of objects of
{\mathbf{D}}
is weakly generating in
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
, see
[
BEa
, Def. 18.1]
.

Let
(C,\rho,(\mu(Z),e))
be any object of
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
, where
(C,\rho,\mu)
is in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
for some free
G
-set
Y
and
Z
is a
H
-invariant subset of
Y
.
Let
y
be a point in
Y
.
Then we can form the object
(C,\rho,(\mu(Hy),e))
in
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
.
We claim that this object is isomorphic to an object in
{\mathbf{D}}
. We consider the
G
-equivariant injection
i\colon X\to Y
which sends
(H,e)
to
y
.
We choose an image
u\colon C^{\prime}\to C
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
of the projection
\mu(Gy)
.
Then we define

(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
by setting

\rho^{\prime}_{g}=gu^{*}\rho_{g}u
for every
g
in
G
and
\mu^{\prime}(W)=u^{*}\mu(i(W))u
for every subset
W
of
X
.
Then we have an isomorphism

(u,e)\colon(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},(\mu^{\prime}(H),e))\to(C,\rho,(\mu(Hy),e))

in
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
.
More generally, if
Z
is any finite
H
-invariant subset of
Y
(note that
H
is finite), then
(C,\rho,(\mu(Z),e))
is isomorphic to a finite sum of objects in
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Let now
(A_{j})_{j\in J}
with
A_{j}\colon(C_{j},\rho_{j},p_{j})\to(C,\rho,p)
be a finite family of morphisms in
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
.

Let
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
be given.
Since

C
is isomorphic to the orthogonal AV-sum in
{\mathbf{C}}
of the family of
projections
(\mu(S))_{S\in H\backslash Y}
the sum

\sum_{S\in H\backslash Y}\mu(S)
converges strictly in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
to
{\mathtt{id}}_{C}
.
Since the morphisms
A_{j}
belong to
{\mathbf{C}}

there exists a finite
H
-invariant subset
Z
of

Y
such that

\|A_{j}-(\mu(Z),e)A_{j}\|<\epsilon

for all
j
in
J
. ∎

By
[
BEa
, Thm. 18.6]
the morphism
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Lambda)
is an equivalence.

Applying
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
to the diagram in (
10.51
) and combining the results above we conclude
the proof of Proposition
10.6
.
∎

Therefore the proofs of the Theorems
1.4
.
2
and
1.4
.
3
are also complete.

11
Calculation of the domain and target of the Paschke transformation

The domain of the Paschke transformation is the functor

K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\colon G\mathbf{UBC}\to\mathbf{Sp}\,.

The first goal of this section is to describe its values on sufficiently nice spaces in terms
of the equivariant homology theory

K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{Sp}

introduced in (
1.17
), see Definition
13.2
below for the technical description. Our final result is stated in Proposition
11.10
.

In order to understand why the construction of the comparison map in Proposition
11.10
is difficult, note that
on the one hand for
X
in
G\mathbf{UBC}
the spectrum
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
is defined as the
K
-theory of an explicitly constructed
C^{*}
-category associated to
X
and the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
. On the other hand the spectrum
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(X)
is the value on the underlying topological space of
X
of the equivariant homology theory given by a spectrum-valued functor
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}
on the orbit category
G\mathbf{Orb}
of
G
determined by
{\mathbf{C}}
. The construction of a natural map between
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
and

K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(X)
will involve a classification of functors with certain homological properties on subcategories of
G{\mathbf{Top}}
. This classification is related to
Elmendorf’s theorem
and the techniques behind it.

The second theme of the present section is the calculation of the domain and target of the Paschke transformation. Our main example of a coefficient category is
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
for a
C^{*}
-algebra
A
with an action of
G
. If
A
is unital, then one can express the values of the functors

K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
on
G
-orbits and of
K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
on sufficiently nice spaces directly in terms of constructions with the algebra
A
. The results are stated as Corollary
11.13
and Propositions
11.15
and
11.16
.

We start with the statement of Elmendorf’s theorem.
Let
\mathbf{M}
be a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category.
In the present paper we adopt the following simple definition which in some sense reverses the history of this notion.

Definition 11.1
.

An equivariant
\mathbf{M}
-valued homology theory is a functor

E\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{M}\,.

Recall that a weak equivalence between topological spaces is a continuous map which induces a bijection between the sets of connected components and isomorphisms between the higher homotopy groups at all base points. We have a functor

\ell\colon{\mathbf{Top}}\to\mathbf{Spc}

(11.1)

which presents
\mathbf{Spc}
as the localization of
{\mathbf{Top}}
at the weak equivalences.
We now consider the functor

Y^{G}\colon G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})

(11.2)

which sends

X
in
G{\mathbf{Top}}
to the presheaf

S\mapsto\ell({\mathtt{Map}}_{G{\mathbf{Top}}}(S_{disc},X))\,,

where
{\mathtt{Map}}_{G{\mathbf{Top}}}(S_{disc},X)
in
{\mathbf{Top}}
is the topological mapping space of equivariant maps.
By definition, a map between
G
-topological spaces is an equivariant weak equivalence if it induces weak equivalences between the fixed point subspaces for all subgroups of
G
.

Theorem 11.2

(Elmendorf’s theorem)
.

The functor
Y^{G}
presents
{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})
as the localization of
G{\mathbf{Top}}
at the equivariant weak equivalences.

By the universal property of presheaves, the pull-back along the Yoneda embedding
\mathrm{yo}\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})
induces an equivalence

\mathrm{yo}^{*}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}({\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb}),\mathbf{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{Orb},\mathbf{M})\,.

Let
E\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{M}
be an equivariant homology theory. Its
colimit preserving extension
to presheaves is the left Kan-extension
\mathrm{yo}_{!}E\colon{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})\to\mathbf{M}
of
E
along
\mathrm{yo}
.

Definition 11.3
.

The evaluation of
E
on
G
-topological spaces is defined as composition
(which we will again denote by
E
)

E\colon G{\mathbf{Top}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Y^{G}}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{yo}_{!}E}}{{\to}}\mathbf{M}\,.

(11.3)

If
S
is in
G\mathbf{Orb}
, then the value
of the original functor
E
on
S
and the evaluation of
E
on the discrete
G
-space
S_{disc}
coincide so that there is no conflict of notation.
The value of the equivariant homology theory on a general space
X
in
G{\mathbf{Top}}
is given by the coend

E(X)\coloneqq\int^{G\mathbf{Orb}}E\wedge\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}Y^{G}(X)\,,

(11.4)

where

\wedge\colon\mathbf{M}\times\mathbf{Sp}\to\mathbf{M}
is the tensor structure of
\mathbf{M}
(the same as (
8.6
)) which exists by the cocompleteness and stability assumptions on
\mathbf{M}
.

We let
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc}}
be the full subcategory of

G\mathbf{UBC}
of
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces which have the following properties:

1.

the underlying topological space is Hausdorff,

2.

the bornology is generated by relatively compact subsets,

3.

the coarse structure is generated by all entourages of the form

G(K\times K)
, where
K
is a relatively compact connected subset,

4.

G
acts properly and cocompactly.

The category
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc}}
contains all
G
-finite
G
-simplicial complexes with finite stabilizers with the structures induced by the spherical path metric.
We consider the functor

\iota\colon G\mathbf{UBC}\to G{\mathbf{Top}}
which takes the underlying
G
-topological space.

Lemma 11.4
.

The restriction

\iota_{|G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc}}}\colon G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc}}\to G{\mathbf{Top}}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

It is clear that
\iota_{|G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc}}}
is faithful. We must show that it is full.
Let
X,Y
be in
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc}}
and
f\colon X\to Y
be an equivariant continuous map.
We must show that it is controlled, uniformly continuous and proper.

We first show that
f
is proper.
Let
K
be a relatively compact subset of
Y
and let
(x_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
be a net in
f^{-1}(K)
.
Since
K
is relatively compact, and
G\backslash X
is compact, we can assume by taking a subnet that

(f(x_{\alpha}))_{\alpha}
and
([x_{\alpha}])_{\alpha}
converge in
Y
and
G\backslash X
, respectively.
By the latter there exists a family
(g_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
in
G
such that
(g_{\alpha}x_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
converges.
Since then
(g_{\alpha}f(x_{\alpha}))_{\alpha}
also converges and
G
acts properly on
Y
we can assume after taking a subnet that

(g_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
is constant.
But this means that
(x_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
has a subnet converging in
X
, which shows that
f^{-1}(K)
is relatively compact.

We claim that any invariant open entourage of the diagonal of
X
is uniform.
The claim implies that
f
is uniformy continuous: Indeed, if
V
is any uniform entourage of
Y
, then by the axioms for a
G
-uniform structure there exists an invariant uniform entourage
V^{\prime}
of
Y
such that
V^{\prime}\subseteq V
. But then
(f\times f)^{-1}(V^{\prime})
is invariant and open, hence a uniform entourage of
X
by the claim. The relation
(f\times f)^{-1}(V^{\prime})\subseteq(f\times f)^{-1}(V)
implies that
(f\times f)^{-1}(V)
is uniform.

We now show the claim. Assume by contradiction that
U
is not uniform.
Then for every invariant uniform entourage
V
of
X
there exists

(x_{V},y_{V})
in
V\setminus U
. By compactness of the quotient we can assume, after taking a cofinal subnet
(V_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
of uniform entourages, that

[x_{V_{\alpha}}]\to[x]
and
[y_{V_{\alpha}}]\to[y]
. We can find a net
(g_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
in
G
such that
g_{\alpha}x_{V_{\alpha}}\to x
in
X
.
But then also
g_{\alpha}y_{V_{\alpha}}\to x
since
X
is Hausdorff and the net
(V_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
of uniform entourages is cofinal. Since
U
is
G
-invariant we have

(g_{\alpha}x_{V_{\alpha}},g_{\alpha}y_{V_{\alpha}})\not\in U
for all
\alpha
, and since
U
is open we conclude that also
(x,x)\not\in U
.
But this is impossible since
U
was an open neighbourhood of the diagonal.

We check on generators that
f
is controlled. Let
K
be a relatively compact connected subset of
X
and consider the generator

G(K\times K)
of the coarse structure of
X
. Then
f(K)
is relatively compact and connected, too.
Therefore

(f\times f)G(K\times K)=G(f(K)\times f(K))
is a coarse entourage of
Y
.
∎

Recall that
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
is defined on
G\mathbf{UBC}
. By the Lemma
11.4

we can restrict
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
to a functor defined on the full subcategory
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc}}
of
G{\mathbf{Top}}
. In contrast, the equivariant homology theory
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}
gives rise to a functor defined on all of
G{\mathbf{Top}}
by Definition
11.3
.
Therefore, as a preparation we present a general result which helps to compare a functor with homological properties defined on some full subcategory of
G{\mathbf{Top}}
with an associated equivariant homology theory.

Let
{\mathbf{V}}
be a simplicial model category with weak equivalences
W
, homotopy equivalences
W_{h}
, and
with functorial factorizations.
The associated
\infty
-category of
{\mathbf{V}}
is defined by
{\mathbf{V}}_{\infty}:={\mathbf{V}}[W^{-1}]
. We let
\ell\colon{\mathbf{V}}\to{\mathbf{V}}_{\infty}
denote the canonical functor.
We furthermore let
{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}
denote the full subcategory of cofibrant/fibrant objects in
{\mathbf{V}}
. The following lemma is of course well-known, but for lack of reference, we include a proof here.

Lemma 11.5
.

The inclusion
{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}\to{\mathbf{V}}
induces an equivalence of Dwyer–Kan localizations
{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}[W_{h}^{-1}]\simeq{\mathbf{V}}[W^{-1}]
.

Proof.

We consider the following square

\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{h}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}[W^{-1}_{h}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}_{\infty}}

where the dotted arrow is obtained from the universal property of the localization
\ell_{h}
. We claim that it is an equivalence as desired. In order to produce an inverse
we consider the square

\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\scriptstyle{RL}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}_{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{h}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}[W^{-1}_{h}]}

where
RL
is the fibrant-cofibrant replacement functor.
The dotted arrow is obtained from the universal property
of
\ell
since
RL
sends weak equivalences to homotopy equivalences.
We have a diagram

\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathtt{id}}
\textstyle{RL}

of endofunctors of
{\mathbf{V}}
, where
L
and
R
are the fibrant and cofibrant replacement functors. It is
sent by
\ell
to a diagram of equivalences.
Similarly,
\ell_{h}
sends the restriction of this diagram to
{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}
to a diagram of equivalences.
From this we can conclude that the two dotted arrows are inverse to each other.
∎

Let
E\colon{\mathbf{V}}\to\mathbf{M}
be a homotopy invariant functor.

Lemma 11.6
.

There exists a functor

E^{\infty}\colon{\mathbf{V}}_{\infty}\to\mathbf{M}
such that the following square commutes:

\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{E_{|{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{M}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}_{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{E^{\infty}}

Proof.

We obtain the desired square from

\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{h}}
\scriptstyle{E_{|{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{cf}}[W^{-1}_{h}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathbf{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}_{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{E^{\infty}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{M}}

where the dotted arrow exists since
E
sends homotopy equivalences to equivalences.
∎

We consider some full subcategory
{\mathbf{W}}
of
G{\mathbf{Top}}
and let

E\colon{\mathbf{W}}\to\mathbf{M}
be some functor.
We assume that
{\mathcal{F}}
is a family of subgroups of
G

and that
G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}\subseteq{\mathbf{W}}
.
We then define the equivariant homology theory
E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{M}
as the left Kan extension of the functor
E_{|G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}
along
i_{{\mathcal{F}}}
:

\textstyle{G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{E_{|G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}}
\scriptstyle{i_{{\mathcal{F}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{M}\,.}
\textstyle{G\mathbf{Orb}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}}

Following Definition
11.3
we will consider

E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}
also as a functor
E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}\colon G{\mathbf{Top}}\to\mathbf{M}
.

We now use that
G{\mathbf{Top}}
admits a simplicial model category structure
with the weak equivalences as described after (
11.2
) and such that the notion of homotopy is the usual one. By Theorem
11.2
the functor

Y^{G}\colon G{\mathbf{Top}}\to{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})
is equivalent to the functor
G{\mathbf{Top}}\to G{\mathbf{Top}}_{\infty}

in the notation introduced before Lemma
11.6
. Let
j\colon{\mathbf{W}}\to G{\mathbf{Top}}
denote the inclusion.

Lemma 11.7
.

Assume:

1.

G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}\subseteq{\mathbf{W}}\subseteq G{\mathbf{Top}}^{\mathrm{cf}}

2.

{\mathbf{W}}
is closed under taking the product with
[0,1]
.

3.

E
is homotopy invariant.

Then we have a canonical natural transformation of functors

j^{*}E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}\to E\colon{\mathbf{W}}\to\mathbf{M}\,.

Proof.

Since
j
is fully faithful,
we have an equivalence
E\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}j^{*}j_{!}E
. We claim that
j_{!}E
is homotopy invariant. Let

X
be in
G{\mathbf{Top}}
. Then we must show that
(j_{!}E)([0,1]\times X)\to(j_{!}E)(X)
is an equivalence.
We use the point-wise formula for the left Kan extension in order to rewrite this map as

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{(Y\to[0,1]\times X)\in{\mathbf{W}}_{/[0,1]\times X}}E(Y)\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{(Z\to X)\in{\mathbf{W}}_{/X}}E(Z)\,.

(11.5)

We now observe that the maps of the form

[0,1]\times Z\to[0,1]\times X
for maps
Z\to X
are cofinal in the index category of the left colimit. At this point we use that
{\mathbf{W}}
is closed under taking products with an interval.
Indeed, let
(a,b)\colon Y\to[0,1]\times X
be a map. Then we consider the factorization

Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a,{\mathtt{id}}_{Y})}}{{\to}}[0,1]\times Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\mathtt{id}}_{[0,1]},b)}}{{\to}}[0,1]\times X\,.

Consequently, the morphism in (
11.5
) is equivalent to

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{(Z\to X)\in{\mathbf{W}}_{/X}}E([0,1]\times Z)\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{(Z\to X)\in{\mathbf{W}}_{/X}}E(Z)\,.

This map is an equivalence since
E
is homotopy invariant. This finishes the proof of the claim.

By Lemma
11.6
we get a functor

(j_{!}E)^{\infty}\colon{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})\to\mathbf{M}
fitting into the commutative square in

\textstyle{{\mathbf{W}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\scriptstyle{E}
\textstyle{G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{{\mathcal{F}}}}
\textstyle{G{\mathbf{Top}}^{\mathrm{cf}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(j_{!}E)_{|G{\mathbf{Top}}^{\mathrm{cf}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{M}}
\textstyle{G\mathbf{Orb}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{yo}}
\textstyle{G{\mathbf{Top}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Y^{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{PSh}}(G\mathbf{Orb})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(j_{!}E)^{\infty}}

(11.6)

Here the triangle involving
(j_{!}E)_{|G{\mathbf{Top}}^{\mathrm{cf}}}
commutes since
j^{*}j_{!}E\simeq E
as observed already above.
The commutative diagram provides an equivalence
E_{|G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}\simeq i_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{*}\mathrm{yo}^{*}(j_{!}E)^{\infty}
.
Applying the left Kan extension
\mathrm{yo}_{!}i_{{\mathcal{F}},!}
we get an equivalence

\mathrm{yo}_{!}E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}\simeq\mathrm{yo}_{!}i_{{\mathcal{F}},!}E_{|G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}\simeq\mathrm{yo}_{!}i_{{\mathcal{F}},!}i_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{*}\mathrm{yo}^{*}(j_{!}E)^{\infty}\,.

The counit
\mathrm{yo}_{!}i_{{\mathcal{F}},!}i_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{*}\mathrm{yo}^{*}\to{\mathtt{id}}
then yields the transformation

\mathrm{yo}_{!}E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}\to(j_{!}E)^{\infty}
.
We finally apply
j^{*}(Y^{G})^{*}
and get the desired transformation

j^{*}E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}\to j^{*}(Y^{G})^{*}(j_{!}E)^{\infty}\simeq E\colon{\mathbf{W}}\to\mathbf{M}\,,

where the second equivalence follows form the commutativity of a part of the diagram (
11.6
) above.
∎

Recall that
{\mathbf{W}}
is a full subcategory of
G{\mathbf{Top}}
and that

E\colon{\mathbf{W}}\to\mathbf{M}
is some functor. We call
E
reduced if
E(\emptyset)\simeq 0
.
We let
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{hfin}}
denote the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{W}}
of spaces which are homotopy equivalent to
a
G
-finite
G
-CW complex with stabilizers in
{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proposition 11.8
.

Assume:

1.

{\mathbf{W}}\subseteq G{\mathbf{Top}}^{\mathrm{cf}}
and
{\mathbf{W}}
contains all
G
-finite
CW
-complexes with stabilizers in
{\mathcal{F}}
.

2.

{\mathbf{W}}
is closed under taking the product with
[0,1]
.

3.

E
is reduced, homotopy invariant, and excisive for cell attachments.

Then the natural transformation from Lemma
11.7
induces an equivalence

(j^{*}E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}})_{|{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{hfin}}}\to E_{|{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{hfin}}}\,.

Proof.

We note that
j^{*}E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}\colon{\mathbf{W}}\to\mathbf{M}
is reduced, homotopy invariant, and excisive for cell attachments.

We must show that
E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}(X)\to E(X)
is an equivalence for all
X
in
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{hfin}}
. Since
j^{*}E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}
and
E
are homotopy invariant we can assume that
X
is a
G
-finite
CW
-complex with stabilizers in
{\mathcal{F}}
.

We then argue by induction by the number of cells.
The assertion is clear for the empty
G
-CW-complex since both functors are reduced.
Assume now that the assertion is true for the
G
-CW-complex
Y
, and that
X
is obtained from
Y
by a cell-attachement. Then we have a push-out diagram

\textstyle{G/K\times S^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G/K\times D^{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X}

where
n
is in
{\rm Nature}
and
K
is a subgroup of
G
belonging to
{\mathcal{F}}
.
The natural transformation induces a map of push-out diagrams

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 50.42787pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-44.44102pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}(G/K\times S^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern
