D^{n+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.29936pt\raise-32.32pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.29936pt\raise-32.32pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E(X)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

which is implemented by equivalences at the two upper and the lower left corners by the induction hypothesis.
We conclude that

E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}E(X)
.
∎

We now consider two functors
E,F\colon{\mathbf{W}}\to\mathbf{M}
and assume that we are given an equivalence

\phi\colon E_{|G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}\to F_{|G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}\,.

Corollary 11.9
.

Assume:

1.

{\mathbf{W}}\subseteq G{\mathbf{Top}}^{\mathrm{cf}}
and
{\mathbf{W}}
contains all
G
-finite
CW
-complexes with stabilizers in
{\mathcal{F}}
.

2.

{\mathbf{W}}
is closed under taking the product with
[0,1]
.

3.

E
and
F
are reduced, homotopy invariant, and excisive for cell attachments.

Then

\phi
extends to an equivalence

\tilde{\phi}\colon E_{|{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{hfin}}}\to F_{|{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{hfin}}}\,.

Proof.

The equivalence
\phi
induces an equivalence

\tilde{\phi}\colon E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}F^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}}
.
The desired equivalence is now given by the composition

E_{|{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{hfin}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}(j^{*}E^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}})_{|{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{hfin}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{\phi},\simeq}}{{\to}}(j^{*}F^{\%,{\mathcal{F}}})_{|{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{hfin}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}F_{|{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{hfin}}}

where the outer equivalences are supplied by Proposition
11.8

∎

We let
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc},\mathrm{hfin}}
be the full subcategory of
G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc}}\cap G{\mathbf{Top}}^{\mathrm{cf}}
of
G
-spaces which are homotopy equivalent to
G
-finite
G
-CW complexes with stabilizers in
\mathbf{Fin}
.
We consider
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
which is effectively additive and admits countable orthogonal AV-sums.

Proposition 11.10
.

We have an equivalence

K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(-)_{|G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc},\mathrm{hfin}}}\simeq\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(-)_{|G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc},\mathrm{hfin}}}\,.

Proof.

We start with the equivalence

K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(S_{min,min,disc})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(S_{min,min,disc}))\simeq\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}(S_{min,min})\,,

where the second equivalence is given by the cone boundary
[
BEKW20a
, Prop. 9.35]
.
For every
S
in
G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}
the sets of invariant locally finite subsets

\mathrm{LF}(S_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min})

and

\mathrm{LF}(S_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})
are equal.
Using that
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
is a continuous equivariant coarse homology theory we get the
middle equivalence in

\displaystyle\mathclap{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}((-)_{min,min})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}((-)_{min,min}\otimes G_{can,max})\simeq K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}((-)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(-)}

of functors
on
G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}
.
We now apply Corollary
11.9
with
{\mathbf{W}}=G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{pcc}}\cap G{\mathbf{Top}}^{\mathrm{cf}}
,
{\mathcal{F}}=\mathbf{Fin}
,

E=K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(-)
and
F=\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(-)
in order to get the desired equivalence.
∎

Using Proposition
11.10
we can express the domain of the Paschke transformation in terms of the equivariant homology theory
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}
.
In the following we describe the values of this functor on
G
-orbits in some detail.
We use remark environments in order to be able to refer to this discussion later.

Remark 11.11
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is effectively additive.
By
[
BEc
, Prop. 8.2.3]
we have an explicit description of the values of the functor
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}
on
G
-orbits
S
:

K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(S)\simeq K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{\widetilde{\bar{C}}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(S_{min,max})\rtimes_{r}G)\,.

(11.7)

Here
\mathbf{\widetilde{\bar{C}}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(S_{min,max})
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is the
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(S_{min,max})

with the
G
-action induced by functoriality by the actions of
G
on
S
and
{\mathbf{C}}
,
and
-\rtimes_{r}G
is the reduced crossed product for
G
-
C^{*}
-categories introduced in
[
BEa
, Thm. 12.1]
.
Note that the objects of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(S_{min,max})
are objects of
{\mathbf{C}}
which are decomposed as orthogonal AV-sums of
S
-indexed families of objects of
{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u}
with finitely many non-zero terms, and morphisms are morphisms in
{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}
which are diagonal with respect to this decomposition.
We note that (
11.7
) implies that the functor
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}
is the functor
defined in
[
BEa
, Def. 19.12]
for
\mathrm{Hg}=K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
and denoted there by
(K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}})^{G}_{{{\mathbf{C}}^{u}},r}
.

The right-hand side of the equivalence in (
11.7
) reflects the functorial dependence on
S
in an obvious manner. If one is not interested in functoriality, then one can give even simpler formulas. For a subgroup

H
of
G
we have the equivalence

K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(G/H)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{qrforfofwqewfqefqefe}}}{{\simeq}}K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}((G/H)_{min,max})\rtimes_{r}G)\simeq K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{u}\rtimes_{r}H)

by using
[
BEa
, Cor. 19.13]
and the Morita invariance of
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
.

∎

Remark 11.12
.

We continue the calculations from Remark
11.11
but now specialize further
to the case
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
for an
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg})
.
Since
A
is unital, the inclusion
A\to\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)^{u}
is a Morita equivalence (combine
[
BEa
, Ex. 16.9 & 18.15]
) and therefore induces by
[
BEa
, Prop. 16.11]
(stating that
-\rtimes_{r}H
preserves Morita equivalences) and
[
BEa
, Thm. 16.18]
(stating that
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
is Morita invariant)
an equivalence

K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Alg}}(A\rtimes_{r}H)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)^{u}\rtimes_{r}H)\,.

So in this case

K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(G/H)\simeq K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Alg}}(A\rtimes_{r}H)\,.

We see that the functor
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}
has the same values as the functor introduced in
[
DL98
]
(with additions by
[
Joa03
]

or alternatively by
[
LNS17
]
)
6
6

6

To be precise, in
[
DL98
]
only the case
A={\mathbb{C}}
is considered, but the generalization to unital
C^{*}
-algebras with trivial
G
-action is straightforward.
The additions concern a correction in the construction of a
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories.
.
If
A
is unital and is equipped with the trivial
G
-action,
then by
[
BEa
, Prop. 19.18]
the functor
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}
and the Davis–Lück functor are actually equivalent as functors.

∎

Using (
9.6
) and Proposition
11.10
combined with Remark

11.12
we can describe the values on the orbit category for the functor
K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)}}
appearing in the domain of the Paschke morphism.
Let
A
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.

Corollary 11.13
.

If
A
is unital, then
for every subgroup
H
of
G
we have an equivalence

K^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)}}((G/H)_{min,min,disc})\simeq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&|H|=\infty\,,\\
\Sigma K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Alg}}(A\rtimes_{r}H)&|H|<\infty\,.\end{array}\right.

We now turn our attention to the target of the Paschke morphism. We show that in the case of
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
for unital
A
,
we can express the functor

K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}(-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{vfdsvsvvsvsdvdsadsvdsva}}}{{=}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(-),\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})

in terms of the more familiar functor

K_{A}^{G,\mathrm{an}}(-)\coloneqq\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(-),A)

from
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
to
\mathbf{Sp}
, see
[
BEL
, Def. 1.14]
. In order to state the results properly, we introduce the following notation.

Definition 11.14
.

1.

We let
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,hfin}}_{+}
denote the full subcategory of
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
on spaces which are homotopy equivalent in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
to
G
-finite
G
-CW complexes with finite stabilizers.

2.

We let
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}
denote the full subcategory of
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
of second countable spaces with proper
G
-action which are homotopy equivalent in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
to countable
G
-CW complexes with proper
G
-action.

Let
A
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.

Proposition 11.15
.

If
A
is unital, then
we have an equivalence of functors

(\Sigma K_{A}^{G,\mathrm{an}})_{|G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,hfin}}_{+}}\simeq(K_{{\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)}}^{G,\mathrm{An}})_{|G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,hfin}}_{+}}\,.

Proof.

We abbreviate
{\mathbf{C}}\coloneqq\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
.
Using the notation of
[
BEL
, Def. 1.14]
we have the equality

K_{\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}}^{G,\mathrm{an}}(-)=K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(-)\,.

If
X
is in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,hfin}}_{+}
, then by Lemma
7.2

the functor

{\mathbf{B}}\mapsto K_{{\mathbf{B}}}^{G,\mathrm{an}}({X})
sends exact sequences in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
to fibre sequences of functors on
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,hfin}}_{+}
,
annihilates flasques, and sends relative Morita equivalences to equivalences. By
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.32.3]
it also sends weak Morita equivalences to equivalences.

We apply the exactness property to the exact sequence

0\to{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\pi}}}{{\to}}\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to 0\,.

(11.8)

Since
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
admits countable AV-sums, we know by Lemma
2.20
that
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
is flasque. Therefore
K_{{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}}^{G,\mathrm{an}}(-)\simeq 0

and the boundary map of the fibre sequence obtained by applying
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{-}

to
(
11.8
) is an
equivalence

K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(-)=K_{\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}}^{G,\mathrm{an}}(-)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Sigma K_{{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}}^{G,\mathrm{an}}(-)

(11.9)

of functors on
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,hfin}}_{+}
.
We consider the zig-zag

A\to({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}\leftarrow{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}

(11.10)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
,
where by Lemma
2.19
the first map is a Morita equivalence, the second is a weak Morita equivalence, and the third one is a split relative Morita equivalence. We therefore get an associated zig-zag of equivalences

K^{G}_{A}(-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{G}_{({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}}(-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}}(-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}K^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}}(-)

(11.11)

of functors on
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,hfin}}_{+}
.

Composing the equivalences in (
11.9
) and (
11.11
)
we get the asserted equivalence.
∎

In the next proposition we calculate the values
of the functor
K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}
from (
1.6
).
We use the notation introduced in Definition
11.14
.
2
.
Let
A
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.

Proposition 11.16
.

If
A
is unital and separable, then we have an equivalence

(\Sigma K_{A}^{G,\mathrm{an}})_{|G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}}\simeq(K_{{\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)}}^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}})_{|G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}}\,.

Proof.

The argument is similar as for Proposition
11.15
. However, if
X
is in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}
, then

\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C_{0}(X))
is not ind-
G
-proper in general so that
{\mathbf{B}}\to K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}(X)
does not send all exact sequences to fibre sequences, i.e., Lemma
7.2
is not directly applicable.

In analogy with (
1.5
) we can define
the locally finite evaluation
F^{\mathrm{lf}}
of
any functor
F
on
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
(with complete target) by

F^{\mathrm{lf}}(X)\coloneqq\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{U\subseteq X}F(U)\,,

where
U
runs over the open subsets of
X
with
G
-compact closure.
We have a natural transformation
c_{F}\colon F\to F^{\mathrm{lf}}
, and the transformation

c_{F^{\mathrm{lf}}}\colon F^{\mathrm{lf}}\to(F^{\mathrm{lf}})^{\mathrm{lf}}
is an equivalence by a cofinality argument.

We again abbreviate
{\mathbf{C}}\coloneqq\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
.
We will construct an equivalence

(\Sigma K_{A}^{G,\mathrm{an},\mathrm{lf}})_{|G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}}\simeq(K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}})_{|G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}}

(11.12)

and furthermore show that the canonical morphism
c_{K_{A}^{G,\mathrm{an}}}
induces an equivalence

(K_{A}^{G,\mathrm{an}})_{|G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}(K_{A}^{G,\mathrm{an},\mathrm{lf}})_{|G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}}\,.

(11.13)

The asserted equivalence is then defined as the composition of the equivalences
in (
11.12
) and (
11.13
).

We start with the construction of (
11.12
).
We consider the following diagram in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}

\textstyle{{\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}}({\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}}(\pi)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}(\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{-1}{\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}}(\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{F(\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}}({\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}}(\pi)}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}}(\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\,.}

(11.14)

The lower part is a segment of a fibre sequence with
F(\pi)
defined as the fibre of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}(\pi)
, where
\pi
is the quotient morphism
{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
. The upper composition vanishes since (
11.8
) is exact, but it is not necessarily part of a fibre sequence since
\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}
is not known to send all exact sequences to fibre sequences. The dotted arrow and the corresponding square is then given by the universal property of the fibre.

We consider an ind-
G
-proper object
P
and apply the exact functor

\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,-)\colon\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to\mathbf{Sp}
to (
11.14
). We then get the following diagram in
\mathbf{Sp}
(as usual we drop the symbol
{\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}}
if we insert objects in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}(-,-)
)

\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,F(\pi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\,.}

(11.15)

By
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.32.5]
the upper sequence becomes a fibre sequence, too. Therefore the dotted arrow becomes an equivalence. Furthermore,
{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
is flasque by Lemma
2.20
so that

\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\simeq 0
by
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.32.7]
, and
j_{*}
becomes an equivalence.

We consider the following two natural transformations

\Sigma^{-1}K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\Sigma^{-1}K_{\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}}^{G,\mathrm{an}}\xrightarrow{j_{*}}K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{F(\pi)}

(11.16)

and

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{weogjkopfegwreffsv1}}}{{\simeq}}K_{{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}}^{G,\mathrm{an}}\xrightarrow{i_{*}}K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{F(\pi)}

(11.17)

of
\mathbf{Sp}
-valued functors on
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
, where
i_{*}
and
j_{*}
are induced by the morphisms
i
and
j
in (
11.14
).
Since by
[
BEL
, Prop. 1.26]
the restriction of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}\circ C_{0}(-)
to
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,hfin}}_{+}
takes values in ind-
G
-proper objects,
the restrictions of
j_{*}
in (
11.16
) and
i_{*}
in (
11.17
) to
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,hfin}}_{+}
are equivalences.

We apply the
(-)^{\mathrm{lf}}
-construction to the transformations in (
11.16
) and
(
11.17
) and get transformations

\Sigma^{-1}K^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to K_{F(\pi)}^{G,\mathrm{an},\mathrm{lf}}

(11.18)

and

K^{G,\mathrm{an},\mathrm{lf}}_{A}\to K_{F(\pi)}^{G,\mathrm{an},\mathrm{lf}}\,.

(11.19)

We now show that the evaluations of (
11.18
) and (
11.19
) at
X
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}
are equivalences. By homotopy invariance of the domains and targets we can assume that
X
is a countable
G
-CW-complex with proper
G
-action. By local compactness, it admits a cofinal family of open subsets
U
with
G
-compact closure
belonging to
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,hfin}}_{+}
. This implies that
j_{*}
in (
11.16
) and
i_{*}
in (
11.17
)
become equivalences after evaluation at such
U
. We get the equivalences (
11.18
) and (
11.19
) as limits of equivalences. The desired equivalence (
11.12
) is now defined as the suspension of the composition

(K^{G,\mathrm{an},\mathrm{lf}}_{A})_{|G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}}\xrightarrow{\eqref{qwefrfrfrfrfrfqweoifjqwioefeqwfq},\simeq}(K_{F(\pi)}^{G,\mathrm{an},\mathrm{lf}})_{|G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}}\xleftarrow{\simeq,\eqref{qwefrfrfrfrfrfqweoifjqwioewerwerwerrfeqwfq1}}(\Sigma^{-1}K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An},\mathrm{lf}})_{|G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop,\sigma hfin}}_{\mathrm{2nd},+}}\,.

(11.20)

It now remains to show that the canonical transformation
(
11.13
)
is an equivalence.
We can again assume that
X
is a countable
G
-CW-complex with proper
G
-action.
We let
(U_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be an exhaustion of
X
by an increasing family of invariant open subsets with
G
-compact closure. Then setting
Y_{n}:=X\setminus U_{n}
the family
(Y_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is a decreasing family of closed invariant subsets of
X
with
\bigcap_{n\in{\rm Nature}}Y_{n}=\emptyset
. By
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.15.3]
we get a diagram of maps of horizontal fibre sequences

\textstyle{\vdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\vdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\vdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(Y_{n+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(U_{n+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(Y_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(U_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}

Here we use that the inclusions
Y_{n}\to X
are split-closed by
[
BEL
, Prop. 5.1.1]
and our topological assumptions on
X
.
We now consider the fibre sequence obtained as the limit of this diagram in the vertical direction. Using that
A
is separable and

[
BEL
, Thm. 1.14.6]
the limit of the left column vanishes. Hence
we get an equivalence

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(U_{n+1})\simeq K_{A}^{G,\mathrm{an},\mathrm{lf}}(X)

as desired.
∎

12
Comparison with classical constructions

As explained already in the introduction
the classical definition of the domain of the Paschke morphism
does not involve a
C^{*}
-category of controlled Hilbert spaces but it involves the choice of
a single sufficiently large continuously controlled Hilbert space. So in order to compare the approach of the present paper with the classical one we
specialize to the case of trivial coefficients characterized by

{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
and
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})
.
According to Definition
2.8
the objects of
\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})^{(G)}
are pairs
(H,\rho)
of a Hilbert space

H
and a unitary representation
\rho\colon G^{\mathrm{op}}\to U(H)
,
g\mapsto\rho_{g}
.
The morphisms are given by
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})^{(G)}}}((H,\rho),(H^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}))=B(H,H^{\prime})
, the bounded linear operators from
H
to
H^{\prime}
.
The group
G
fixes the objects of
\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})^{(G)}
and acts on the morphisms
by
g\cdot A\coloneqq\rho_{g}^{\prime,-1}A\rho_{g}
.

We consider a second countable proper metric space
X
with an isometric action of the group
G
.
In the following we construct an exact sequence of
C^{*}
-categories

0\to{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(X)\to{\mathbf{D}}^{G}(X)\to\mathbf{Q}^{G}(X)\to 0\,.

(12.1)

We start with the definition of a
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{B}}(X)
with
G
-action. Its objects are triples
(H,\rho,\phi)
, where
(H,\rho)
is in
\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})^{(G)}
such that
H
is separable and

\phi\colon C_{0}(X)\to B(H)
is homomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras
satisfying the following properties:

1.

The representation
\phi
is equivariant, i.e., we have
g^{-1}\cdot\phi(f)=\phi(g^{*}f)
for all
f
in
C_{0}(X)
and
g
in
G
, see (
6.4
).

2.

The representation
\phi
is non-degenerate in the sense that
\overline{\phi(C_{0}(X))H}=H
.

3.

There exists an equivariant unitary isomorphism
(H,\rho)\cong(L^{2}(G)\otimes H^{\prime},\lambda\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{H})
, where
\lambda
is the left-regular representation of
G
on
L^{2}(G)
and
H^{\prime}
is some auxiliary separable Hilbert space.

The morphisms of
{\mathbf{B}}(X)
are inherited from
{\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})^{(G)}}
. The group
G
fixes the objects of
{\mathbf{B}}(X)
and acts on morphisms as in
{\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})^{(G)}}
.

Let
(H,\rho,\phi)
and
(H^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
be objects of
{\mathbf{B}}(X)
.
An operator
A
in
B(H,H^{\prime})
is called locally compact if
\phi^{\prime}(f)A
and
A\phi(f)
belong to
K(H,H^{\prime})

for all
f
in
C_{0}(X)
, where
K(H,H^{\prime})
denotes the set of compact linear operators from
H
to
H^{\prime}
. Further,
A
is called pseudolocal if
\phi^{\prime}(f)A-A\phi(f)\in K(H,H^{\prime})
for all
f
in
C_{0}(X)
.
Finally, it is called controlled if there exists
R
in
(0,\infty)
such that

d({\mathtt{supp}}(f^{\prime}),{\mathtt{supp}}(f))\geq R
implies that
\phi^{\prime}(f)A\phi(f)=0
.
The
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(X)
is the wide
C^{*}
-subcategory of
{\mathbf{B}}(X)

generated by the invariant, locally compact and controlled operators. Similarly the
C^{*}
-category

{\mathbf{D}}^{G}(X)
is generated by the invariant, pseudolocal and controlled operators.
Finally
\mathbf{Q}^{G}(X)
is defined as the quotient, see (
12.1
).
If
(H,\rho,\phi)
is an object of
{\mathbf{B}}(X)
, then the corresponding endomorphism algebras form an exact sequence

0\to C^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)\to D^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)\to Q^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)\to 0

which is the equivariant generalization of (
1.9
) from the introduction.

Definition 12.1
.

An object
(H,\rho,\phi)
of
{\mathbf{D}}^{G}(X)
is called absorbing if for every other
(H^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{D}}^{G}(X)
there exists an isometry
u\colon(H^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\to(H,\rho,\phi)

in
{\mathbf{D}}^{G}(X)
.

The existence of absorbing objects in the case of trivial
G
follows from
[
HR95
, Lem. 7.7]
.
7
7

7

We neither know a reference nor have a proof for the existence of absorbing objects in the equivariant case in full generality, see Remark
12.4
.

For the following discussion, we assume that we can choose an absorbing object
(H,\rho,\phi)
. We set
Q^{G}(X):=Q^{G}(H,\rho,\phi)
and let
Q(H)
be the Calkin algebra of

with the induced
G
-action.
With these choices we can define the Paschke morphism

p^{(H,\rho,\phi)}_{X}\coloneqq\mu_{X}\circ\delta_{X}\colon\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},Q^{G}(X))\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(X),Q(H))

as in

). We assume that there is a scale

.
We furthermore assume that

There exists a commutative square

\textstyle{K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Proof.

We use the identifications

and

The objects of
\mathbf{Q}_{\tau}(X)

\textstyle{K(H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where the horizontal maps are ideal inclusions. By the observation above the

The second morphism in (

This together with

This explains the lower horizontal equivalence in (
12.2
).

It is obvious from the definitions of the Paschke morphisms in (
1.12
) and Definition

that the diagram commutes.

In the following we assume that
(X,\tau)
satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
.
2
such that
p_{(X,\tau)}
is an equivalence.

Corollary 12.3
.

The morphism
\gamma
is an equivalence if and only if
p^{(H,\rho,\phi)}_{X}
is an equivalence.

This says that in all cases where the classical Paschke morphism
p^{(H,\rho,{\phi})}_{X}
is an equivalence it is equivalent
to our morphism
p_{(X,\tau)}
as a spectrum map.
An independent proof
8
8

8

We do not know a reference for such a proof.
that
\gamma
is an equivalence would then allow us to conclude from Theorem
1.4
.
2
that

p^{(H,\rho,{\phi})}_{X}
is an equivalence.

Remark 12.4
.

This is a remark about the existence of absorbing objects an in Definition
12.1
. First of all the discussion above depends on the existence of an absorbing object in
{\mathbf{D}}^{G}(X)
for which we neither have a reference nor a proof. Related results are
[
WY20
, Lem. 4.5.5 & Prop. 4.5.14]
. They are adapted for the approach based on localization algebras but
do not imply directly what we need.
A similar remark applies to
[
BR
, Thm. 1.3]
.

In the non-equivariant case the existence of absorbing objects is settled in, e.g.
[
HR95
, Lem. 7.7]

by an application of Voiculescu’s Theorem.

We furthermore do not know a reference for the fact that

p_{X}^{(H,\rho,{\phi})}
is an equivalence. In fact,

[
BR
, Thm. 1.5]
states a Paschke duality
isomorphism in the equivariant case. But it is not obvious how to identify the targets and the maps in
[
BR
, Thm. 1.5]
with
p_{X}^{(H,\rho,\phi)}
.

∎

13
Homotopy theoretic and analytic assembly maps

In this section we describe the homotopy theoretic and the analytic assembly maps which we will eventually compare in Theorem
1.8
. The homotopy theoretic assembly introduced in Definition
13.2
is a standard construction from equivariant homotopy theory
[
DL98
]
. For the historic development of the analytic assembly map we refer to
[
GAJV19
]
.
Our Definition
13.12
is a spectrum valued refinement of the assembly map of
[
Kas88
,
BCH94
]
which is new in this form.

We begin with the homotopy theoretic assembly map. Let
G\mathbf{Orb}
denote the orbit category of
G
and
\mathbf{M}
be some cocomplete stable
\infty
-category. Recall that by Definition
11.1

an equivariant
\mathbf{M}
-valued homology theory is simply a functor

E\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{M}\,.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a family of subgroups of
G
. By
G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}
we denote the full subcategory of the orbit category
G\mathbf{Orb}
of transitive
G
-sets with stabilizers in the family
{\mathcal{F}}
.
Since

*
is a final object of
G\mathbf{Orb}
we have a natural transformation
E\to\underline{E(*)}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{Orb},\mathbf{M})
. This transformation induces the homotopy theoretic assembly map:

Definition 13.1
.

The homotopy theoretic assembly map for
E
and
{\mathcal{F}}
is the canonical morphism

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{h}_{E,{\mathcal{F}}}\colon\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}E\to E(*)

in
\mathbf{M}
.

Recall that we can evaluate the equivariant homology theory
E

on
G
-topological spaces using (
11.3
).
For every
X
in
G{\mathbf{Top}}
we get a morphism

\mathrm{Asmbl}_{E,X}^{h}\colon E(X)\to E(*)

(13.1)

which is induced by the projection
X\to*
.
We let
E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}
be a
G
-CW complex representing the homotopy type of the classifying space for the family
{\mathcal{F}}
. It is characterized
essentially uniquely by the condition that

Y^{G}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})(S)\simeq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\emptyset&S\not\in G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}\,,\\
{*}&S\in G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}\,.\end{array}\right.

(13.2)

As a consequence of (
11.4
) we then get the equivalence

E(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}E
, and under this identification we have the equivalence

\mathrm{Asmbl}_{E,{\mathcal{F}}}^{h}\simeq\mathrm{Asmbl}_{E,E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}^{h}

(13.3)

of assembly maps.
Further below, in the special case of the functor
E=\hat{K}_{A}^{G}
introduced in Definition
16.10
for
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
we will use the notation

\mu^{DL}_{A,X}\coloneqq\mathrm{Asmbl}_{\hat{K}^{G}_{A},X}^{h}\,,\quad\mu^{DL}_{A,{\mathcal{F}}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Asmbl}_{\hat{K}^{G}_{A},{\mathcal{F}}}^{h}

(13.4)

indicating that

\mu^{DL}_{A,{\mathcal{F}}}
is the assembly map introduced by Davis–Lück
[
DL98
]
.

We have a functor

\iota\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to G\mathbf{BC}\,,\quad S\mapsto S_{min,max}\,,

(13.5)

where
S_{min,max}
is the
G
-set
S
equipped with the minimal coarse structure and the maximal bornology. For a coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
which is effectively additive and admits countable AV-sums we have an
equivariant coarse
K
-homology functor

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c,G_{can,min}}^{G}\colon G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}

(see Definition
3.4
for
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
and Definition
4.7
for the twist of an equivariant coarse homology theory by an object of
G\mathbf{BC}
, in the present case by
G_{can,min}
). The following is the technical definition of the functor described in (
1.17
).

Definition 13.2
.

We define the functor

K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\to}}G\mathbf{BC}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}\,.

We now apply the definitions of assembly maps explained above to the functor
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}
in place of
E
and introduce a shorter notation.

Definition 13.3
.

The homotopy theoretic assembly map associated to
G
,
{\mathcal{F}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}
is defined to be the map

\mathrm{Asmbl}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},{\mathcal{F}}}^{h}\coloneqq\mathrm{Asmbl}_{K{\mathbf{C}}^{G},{\mathcal{F}}}^{h}\colon\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}\to K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(*)\,.

More generally, for every
X
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{\mathbf{Top}})
, specializing (
13.1
), we have the morphism

\mathrm{Asmbl}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},X}^{h}\coloneqq\mathrm{Asmbl}^{h}_{K{\mathbf{C}}^{G},X}:K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(X)\to K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(*)

(13.6)

induced by the projection
X\to*
.
Since
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
depends naturally on the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
, see (
2.9
) for the definition of this category, so do the assembly maps

\mathrm{Asmbl}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},X}^{h}
and
\mathrm{Asmbl}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},{\mathcal{F}}}^{h}
.

We now turn to the analytic assembly map whose final definition will be stated in Definition
13.12
.
We start with introducing the notation for its domain. Recall that

G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
is the category of locally compact Hausdorff
G
-spaces with partially defined proper maps.

Definition 13.4
.

We denote by
G\mathbf{LCH}_{+,\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{prop}}
the full category of
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}

of spaces on which
G
acts properly and cocompactly.

We will describe the analytic assembly map

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},{\mathcal{F}}}
associated to
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and a family
{\mathcal{F}}
contained in
\mathbf{Fin}
. In analogy to (
13.1
) we will further describe a natural transformation

\mathrm{Asmbl}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}^{\mathrm{an}}\colon K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(-)\to\underline{\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\rtimes_{r}G)}

of functors from
G\mathbf{LCH}_{+,\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{prop}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}
.
Note that for infinite
G
the morphism

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},X}\colon K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(X)\to\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\rtimes_{r}G)

(13.7)

can not simply be induced by a map
X\to*
since
*
and therefore this map are not in the category
G\mathbf{LCH}_{+,\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{prop}}
.
If
E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}
happens to be in
G\mathbf{LCH}_{+,\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{prop}}
, then we will have an equivalence

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},{\mathcal{F}}}\simeq\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}
in analogy to (
13.3
).

The classical definition of the analytic assembly map is based on a construction of a family
(\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},X,*})_{X\in G\mathbf{LCH}_{+,\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{prop}}}
of homomorphisms in
{\mathbf{Ab}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}}

\mathrm{Asmbl}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},X,*}^{\mathrm{an}}\colon K_{{\mathbf{C}},*}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(X)=\mathrm{KK}_{*}^{G}(C_{0}(X),\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{*-1}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)\,,

(13.8)

which implement a natural transformation

K_{{\mathbf{C}},*}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(-)\to\underline{\mathrm{KK}_{*-1}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

(13.9)

of functors from
G\mathbf{LCH}_{+,\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{prop}}
to
{\mathbf{Ab}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}}
.

In the following we describe the details of the construction of
\mathrm{Asmbl}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},X,*}^{\mathrm{an}}
in (
13.8
)
thereby lifting it to the spectrum level.
The construction has three steps. The first is an application of functor
-\rtimes G
from
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.22.3]
, where
\rtimes
without subscript refers to the maximal crossed product. The second is a pull-back along the Kasparov projection given by (
13.16
) below.
The last step consists of changing target categories (
13.20
).

The following discussion will be used to get rid of the choice of cut-off functions involved in the Kasparov projection. Here we can take full advantage of the
\infty
-categorical set-up.
We let

\mathcal{R}\colon{G\mathbf{LCH}_{+,\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{prop}}}^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathbf{Set}}

be the following functor:

1.

objects: The functor
\mathcal{R}
sends
X
to the set
\mathcal{R}(X)
of all functions

\chi
in
C_{c}(X)
such that

Lemma 13.6

The projection

Proof.

By the pointwise formula for the left Kan extension
q_{!}
we must show that the projection

induces for every

{G\mathbf{LCH}_{+,\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{prop}}}

Since

preserves colimits it actually suffices to show that

in
\mathbf{Spc}
.
For a simplicial set

{\mathbf{Top}}

suffices to show that

is a trivial Kan fibration.
So we must show that

in
{\rm Nature}
a function

For the case

we observe that for any

{G\mathbf{LCH}_{+,\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{prop}}}
we have
\mathcal{R}(X)\not=\emptyset
. For
n\geq 1
,
using barycentric coordinates we can write a point in

in the form

where

and

. Then an extension of

is given by

\tilde{\chi}(\sigma t,x)\coloneqq\sqrt{\sigma\chi(t,x)^{2}+(1-\sigma)\chi(t_{0},x)^{2}}\,,

where

is the zero’th vertex of the simplex.

Lemma 13.9
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
is a Morita equivalence, then the induced morphism
\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathcal{F}}}}\to\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{{{\mathbf{D}},{\mathcal{F}}}}

Proof.

By the functoriality of the Kasparov assembly map we have a commutative square

\textstyle{RK^{G,{\mathrm{an}}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathcal{F}}}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{C}}\rtimes_{r}G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{RK^{G,{\mathrm{an}}}_{{{\mathbf{D}}}}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{{{\mathbf{D}},{\mathcal{F}}}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{D}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

It suffices to show that the vertical morphisms are equivalences. We start with the left vertical morphism.
Note that

RK^{G,{\mathrm{an}}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{W\subseteq E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(W),{\mathbf{C}})\,,

By
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.32.8]
the functor

sends relative Morita equivalences to equivalences.
Hence the left vertical arrow in the square above is equivalent to
the colimit of equivalences

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{W\subseteq E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(W),{\mathbf{C}})\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{W\subseteq E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(W),{\mathbf{D}})

and hence itself an equivalence.

The right vertical arrow in the square is an equivalence since

preserves Morita equivalences by
[
BEa
, Prop. 16.11]
, and

Lemma 14.3

The formula (

Proof.

We first observe that if
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
has controlled propagation
then the sum in (
14.3
) has finitely many non-zero terms which all belong to
{\mathbf{U}}
since
A
belongs to
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
.

It follows from Definition
2.12
.
2c
and
[
BEa
, Lem. 7.10]
that

C
is isomorphic to the orthogonal AV-sum of the images of the family of projections
(\mu_{g})_{g\in G}
. Using
[
BEa
, Lem. 7.8]
we therefore get a multiplier isometry

u\colon C\to\bigoplus_{g\in G}C\,,\quad u\coloneqq\sum_{g\in G}e_{g}\mu_{g}\,,

where the sum converges strictly.
We have an analogous multiplier isometry
u^{\prime}\colon C^{\prime}\to\bigoplus_{g\in G}C^{\prime}
.
Still assuming that
A
is controlled, we calculate by using (
2.3
) (saying that
g\cdot\mu_{h}=\mu_{gh}
for all
g,h
in
G
) and the
G
-invariance of
A
(saying that
g\cdot A=A
for all
g
in
G
) that

\Theta_{X}(A):=\sum_{g\in G}{\sigma(\mu^{\prime}_{g^{-1}}A\mu_{e},g)}=u^{\prime}Au^{*}\,.

(14.6)

Since
{\mathbf{U}}
is closed in
\mathbf{L}^{2}(G,{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})
, this formula shows that
\Theta_{X}
extends by continuity to an isometric map defined on all morphisms in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X\otimes G_{can,min})
with values in
{\mathbf{U}}
.
Using the equalities
uu^{*}=\tilde{p}
,
u^{\prime}u^{\prime,*}=\tilde{p}^{\prime}
and (
14.3
)
we see that

{\tilde{p}^{\prime}}\Theta_{X}(A)=\Theta_{X}(A)\tilde{p}=\Theta_{X}(A)\,.

Altogether we obtain an
isometric map

\Theta_{X}(-)\colon{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X\otimes G_{can,min})}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))\to{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{U}})}((C,\rho,\tilde{p}),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\tilde{p}^{\prime}))\,.

We finally show that
\Theta_{X}(-)
is compatible with the composition and the involution. Let
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime}\colon(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})\to(C^{\prime\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime},\mu^{\prime\prime})

be morphisms in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X\otimes G_{can,min})
.
Since
\Theta_{X}
is continuous, as shown above, we can assume for simplicity that the morphisms are controlled.
We then calculate using that
u

and
u^{\prime}
are isometries and (
14.6
) that

\Theta_{X}(A^{\prime})\Theta_{X}(A)=\Theta_{X}(A^{\prime}A)\,,\quad\Theta_{X}(A)^{*}=\Theta_{X}(A^{*})\,.\qed

In order to see the Assertion
14.2
.
2
we consider a map

f\colon X\to X^{\prime}
in
G\mathbf{BC}
. Then
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(f)(C,\rho,\mu)=(C,\rho,f_{*}\mu)
.
We observe by inspection of the definitions that

\Theta_{X^{\prime}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(f)(C,\rho,\mu))=\Theta_{X}(C,\rho,\mu)\,,\quad\Theta_{X}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(f)(A))=\Theta_{X}(A)\,.

We finally show Assertion
14.2
.
3
.
If
X
is bounded, then

X\times\{g\}
is a bounded subset of
X\otimes G_{can,min}
for every
g
in
G
.
Consequently,

\mu_{g}
belongs to
{{\mathbf{C}}}
, see the explanations in Remark
2.13
.
Every summand of
(
14.3
)
is a morphism in
{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\rtimes_{r}G
. Since

{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\rtimes_{r}G
is closed in
{\mathbf{U}}
we conclude that

\Theta_{X}
takes values in the wide subcategory

\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}_{\mathrm{std}}^{(G)}\rtimes_{r}G)
of
\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{U}})
, provided
X
is bounded.
∎

For
X
in
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{bd}}
we will also write

\Theta_{X}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,min})\to{\underline{\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}}

(14.7)

for the restriction of the functor
\Theta_{{\mathcal{O}}(X)}
to the ideal
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,min})
, see (
6.12
) for the notation.

We now apply the functor
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(-)=\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-)
to the transformations (
14.4
) and (
14.5
).
Using the
Definition
3.4
of
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
in order to rewrite the domain and the Morita invariance of
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(-)
together with
[
BEa
, Prop. 17.4 & 17.8]
in order to remove
\mathrm{Idem}(-)
in the target we get the assertions of the following corollary.

Corollary 14.4
.

1.

We have a natural transformation

\theta\colon K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}\to\underline{\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{U}}})}

(14.8)

of functors from
G\mathbf{BC}
to
\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}
.

2.

We have a natural transformation

\theta\colon K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}\to\underline{\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

(14.9)

of functors from
G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{bd}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}
.

Proposition 14.5
.

The morphism

\theta_{*}\colon K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}(*)\to\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)

(14.10)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to the proof of Proposition
10.6
. But the difference is that here
G
is infinite while in Proposition
10.6

H
was finite.
By definition the morphism in question is

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}(*)\simeq K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(G_{can,min}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Theta_{*})}}{{\to}}K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G))\,.

We will construct a factorization of
\Theta_{*}
as

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(G_{can,min})\to{\mathbf{D}}\to\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{D}})\to\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)\,,

where
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(G_{can,min})\to{\mathbf{D}}
is a weak Morita equivalence,
{\mathbf{D}}\to\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{D}})
is a relative idempotent completion, and
\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{D}})\to\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)
is a unitary equivalence. Since
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
sends functors with any of these properties to equivalences
[
BEa
, Sec. 14 -16]
the assertion then follows.

Lemma 14.6
.

\Theta_{*}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

By Lemma
14.3

the functor
\Theta_{*}
is an isometric inclusion on morphisms. It remains to show that it is surjective.

Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
and
(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
be two objects of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(G_{can,min})
.
Note that
\Theta_{*}(C,\rho,\mu)=(C,\rho,\tilde{p})
and
\Theta_{*}(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})=(C^{\prime}\rho^{\prime},\tilde{p}^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)
.
Let
A\colon(C,\rho,\tilde{p})\to(C^{\prime}\rho^{\prime},\tilde{p}^{\prime})
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)
. We will construct a morphism
\hat{A}\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(G_{can,min})
such that
\Theta_{*}(\hat{A})=A
.

Note that
A
is a morphism
(C,\rho)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
in
{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G
which in addition satisfies
\tilde{p}^{\prime}A\tilde{p}=A
.
There is a unique family
(A_{g})_{g\in G}
of morphisms
A_{g}\colon C\to C
in
{\mathbf{C}}
such that

A=\sum_{g\in G}\sigma(A_{g},g)\,,

where the sum converges in norm in
{\mathbf{U}}
. From the equality

\sum_{g\in G}\sigma(A_{g},g)=A=\tilde{p}^{\prime}A\tilde{p}=\sum_{g\in G}\sigma(\mu^{\prime}_{g^{-1}}A_{g}\mu_{e},g)

we conclude that

\mu^{\prime}_{g^{-1}}A_{g}\mu_{e}=A_{g}

(14.11)

for all
g
in
G
.
Using the notation from (
14.6
)
we define

\hat{A}:=u^{\prime,*}\sum_{g\in G}\sigma(A_{g},g)u

in
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}(C,C^{\prime})
. Inserting all definitions we get
\hat{A}=\sum_{k\in G}\sum_{g\in G}k\cdot A_{g}

where the
g
-sum converges in norm and the
k
-sum converges strictly. This formula shows that

\hat{A}
is
G
-invariant.
Furthermore, by (
14.11
) for every
g
in
G
the support of
\sum_{k\in G}k\cdot A_{g}
is the coarse entourage
G(\{(g^{-1},e)\})
of
G_{can,min}
. It follows that
\hat{A}

can be approximated in norm by controlled and invariant operators, i.e., we have
\hat{A}\in\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(G_{can,min})
.
By construction we have
\Theta_{*}(\hat{A})=A
.
This finishes the verification that
\Theta_{*}
is full faithful. ∎

For every free
G
-set
Y
,
every subset
F
of
Y
, and every object
(C,\rho,\mu)
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(Y_{min})

we can consider the projection

\tilde{p}_{F}:={\sigma}(\mu(F),e)
on
(C,\rho)
considered as an object of
{\mathbf{U}}
. We let
{\mathbf{D}}
be the full subcategory of

\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{{r}}G)
of objects of the form

(C,\rho,\tilde{p}_{F})
for some choice of
Y
,
F
and
(C,\rho,\mu)
as above.
We can consider
Y=G
and
F=\{e\}
. Then

\tilde{p}=\tilde{p}_{\{e\}}
so that
{\mathbf{D}}
contains the image of
\Theta_{*}
.

Recall the notion of a weak Morita equivalence from
[
BEa
, Def. 18.3]
.

Lemma 14.7
.

The functor

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(G_{can,min})\to{\mathbf{D}}
is a weak Morita equivalence.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
14.6
that the morphism in question is fully faithful. It remains to show that
set of objects
\Theta_{*}(\mathrm{Ob}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(G_{can,min})))
is weakly generating
[
BEa
, Def. 16.1]
.
In order to simplify the notation we write
\tilde{p}_{g}:=\tilde{p}_{\{g\}}
and note that
\tilde{p}=\tilde{p}_{e}
.
We have

{\sigma}({\mathtt{id}}_{C},g)\tilde{p}_{e}{\sigma}({\mathtt{id}}_{C},g)^{*}=\tilde{p}_{g}\,.

This shows that for every
g
in
G
and
(C,\rho,\mu)
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(G_{can,min})
the object

(C,\rho,\tilde{p}_{g})
in
\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)
is isomorphic to the object
(C,\rho,\tilde{p})

which belongs to the image of
\Theta_{*}
.
Furthermore,
for every finite subset
F
of some free
G
-set
Y

and
(C,\rho,\mu)
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(Y_{min})
the object

(C,\rho,\tilde{p}_{F})
is isomorphic to a finite sum of objects in the image of
\Theta_{*}
.

Let now
(C,\rho,\tilde{p}_{F})
be any object of
{\mathbf{D}}
and

(A_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite family of morphisms in
\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes G)
with target
(C,\rho,\tilde{p}_{F})
. Let
\epsilon
be in
(0,\infty)
.
We write
A_{i}=\sum_{h\in G}{\sigma}(A_{i,h},h)
where the
A_{i,h}
belong to
{{\mathbf{C}}}
. Since these sums converge in norm and
I
is finite
there exists a finite subset
F^{\prime}
of
G
such that

\|A_{i}-\sum_{h\in F^{\prime}}{\sigma}(A_{i,h},h)\|\leq\epsilon/2
for all
i
in
I
.
Since
\sum_{y\in Y}\mu(\{y\})
converges strictly to
{\mathtt{id}}_{C}

we can find a finite subset
F^{\prime\prime}
of
Y
such that

\|A_{i,g}-\mu(g^{-1}F^{\prime\prime})A_{i,g}\|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2|F^{\prime}|}

for all
i
in
I
and
g
in
F^{\prime}
.
Then

\|A_{i}-\tilde{p}_{F^{\prime\prime}}A_{i}\|\leq\epsilon
for all
i
in
I
.
∎

Recall the definition
[
BEa
, Def. 17.5]
of a relative idempotent completion.
In the following we let

{\mathbf{E}}
be
the full subcategory of
{\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{U}})}
with the same objects as
{\mathbf{D}}
. Then
{\mathbf{D}}
is an ideal in
{\mathbf{E}}
and
the idempotent completion

{\mathbf{D}}\to\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{D}})
is understood relative to
{\mathbf{E}}
. We summarize this in the following corollary:

Corollary 14.8
.

The functor

{\mathbf{D}}\to\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{D}})
is a relative idempotent completion.

Lemma 14.9
.

The inclusion

\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{D}})\to\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)
is a unitary equivalence.

Proof.

We apply the characterization of unitary equivalence given in
[
BEa
, Rem. 3.19.3]
. We consider the square

\textstyle{\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})}

Its horizontal morphisms are ideal inclusions by construction.
It remains to show that the right vertical morphism is a unitary equivalence in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
.
In fact, it is fully faithful by definition. Since
{\mathbf{E}}
contains the all objects of the form

(C,\rho,\tilde{p}_{Y})=(C,\rho)
for free
G
-sets
Y
and
(C,\rho,\mu)
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{lf}}(Y_{min})
conclude that it is also essentially surjective.
∎

This finishes the proof of Proposition
14.5
.
∎

We now apply the cone sequence (
4.6
) to the functor

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}
and obtain a boundary map

\partial^{{\mathtt{Cone}}}\colon K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))\to\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}(-)

Definition 14.10
.

We denote by

the full subcategory of

We have a forgetful functor

Let

G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{bd}}
.
We use the Corollary

.
2
in order to see the that the natural transformation defined below takes values in the correct target.

Definition 14.11
.

We define the natural transformation

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Theta}\coloneqq\theta\circ\partial^{{\mathtt{Cone}}}\colon K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))\to\underline{\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

to

.

We consider the functor

\tilde{\iota}\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{bd}}\,,\quad S\mapsto S_{min,max,disc}\,,

(14.12)

(compare with
\iota
in (

We use this identification in order to interpret the target of the map in Assertion
1

A
G
-simplicial complex is a simplicial complex with a simplicial
G
-action.
We assume that if
g
in
G
fixes a point in the interior of a simplex, then it fixes the whole simplex pointwise. This can always be ensured by going over to a barycentric subdivision.
We let
G\mathbf{Simpl}

denote the category of
G
-simplicial complexes and simplicial equivariant maps.

Let
G\mathbf{Simpl}^{\textrm{fin-dim}}

be the full subcategory of
G\mathbf{Simpl}
of finite-dimensional
G
-simplicial complexes.
We have a natural functor

\tilde{s}\colon G\mathbf{Simpl}\to G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{bd}}

which sends a
G
-simplicial complex

to the
G
-uniform bornological coarse space

\tilde{s}(X)

given by
X
with the coarse and the uniform structures induced by the spherical path metric, and with the maximal bornology. We have a commutative diagram
of canonical functors

\textstyle{G{\mathbf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\tilde{\iota}}}
\scriptstyle{{(1)}}
\textstyle{G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{bd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{(2)}}
\textstyle{G\mathbf{Simpl}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{s}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{t}}
\textstyle{G{\mathbf{Top}}}
\textstyle{G\mathbf{Simpl}^{\textrm{fin-dim}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\scriptstyle{t}

where arrow

interprets a
G

-simplicial set, and arrow

sends a uniform bornological space to the underlying

-topological space.

Proposition 14.12
.

1.

The transformation
{\tilde{\iota}}^{*}\partial^{{\mathtt{Cone}}}\colon{\tilde{\iota}}^{*}K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))\to\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(-)
of functors from

to
