is an equivalence.

2.

We have an equivalence

s^{*}K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))\simeq t^{*}\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(-)

(14.13)

of functors from
G\mathbf{Simpl}^{\textrm{fin-dim}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}
.

3.

We have a commutative square of natural transformations

\textstyle{{t^{*}}\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{t^{*}}\Sigma\mathrm{Asmbl}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}^{h}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
(
14.13
)
\textstyle{\underline{\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(*)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
14.5
)
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{s^{*}}K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{s^{*}}\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Theta}}
\textstyle{\underline{\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}}

(14.14)

between functors from
G\mathbf{Simpl}^{\textrm{fin-dim}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}

which depends naturally on the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
.

Proof.

The Assertion
14.12
.
1

is shown in
[
BEKW20a
, Prop. 9.35]
.

We now show Assertion
14.12
.
2
.

Remark 14.13
.

Note that
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(-)
in the statement is the restriction to
G\mathbf{Simpl}^{\textrm{fin-dim}}
of the evaluation of an equivariant homology theory defined on all of
G{\mathbf{Top}}
by (
11.3
). The
other functor
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))
is defined on
G\mathbf{UBC}
. By restriction of both functors
to
G\mathbf{Simpl}^{\textrm{fin-dim}}
we can consider them on the same domain. Assertion
14.12
.
1
then provides an equivalence between the further restriction of both functors to the orbit category.
The functor
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))
is homotopy invariant and excisive for closed decompositions,
but it is not the restriction of an equivariant homology theory. Therefore
we can not appeal to the classification of equivariant homologies in order to extend this transformation to
more general spaces.

One could use Corollary
11.9
applied to
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}
and
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))
in place of
E
and
F
and for the family
{\mathcal{F}}:=\mathbf{All}
in order to obtain an equivalence on all
G
-finite
G
-simplicial complexes.

Our argument below uses that Assertion
1
provides an equivalence on
G{\mathbf{Set}}
(as opposed to
G\mathbf{Orb}
) and yields a more general result:
A natural equivalence between the restrictions of two homotopy invariant and excisive functors to
G{\mathbf{Set}}
canonically extends to an equivalence between these functors at least on
G\mathbf{Simpl}^{\textrm{fin-dim}}
.
∎

We will construct the desired equivalence by induction with respect to the dimension.
We let
G\mathbf{Simpl}_{\leq n}
be the full subcategory of
G
-simplicial complexes of dimension
\leq n
.
We let
s_{n}
and
t_{n}
denote the restrictions of
s
and
t
to
G\mathbf{Simpl}_{\leq n}
.

The case of zero-dimensional simplicial complexes is done by Assertion
1
.

We assume now that we have constructed an equivalence

s_{n-1}^{*}K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))\simeq t_{n-1}^{*}\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(-)

(14.15)

for
n\geq 1
.
The induction step exploits the fact that

s_{n}(X)
in
G\mathbf{Simpl}_{\leq n}
has a canonical decomposition

(Y,Z)
, where
Z
is the disjoint union of
2/3
-scaled
n
-simplices,
and
Y
is the complement of the disjoint union of the interiors of the
1/3
-scaled
n
-simplices
(see the pictures in
[
BE20b
, P. 80]
).
We equip the subspaces
Y
and
Z
with the uniform bornological coarse structures induced from
s_{n}(X)
.

Since both functors
s^{*}_{n}K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))
and

t_{n}^{*}\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(-)
are excisive for such decompositions
we get push-out squares

\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({s}(Y\cap Z)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({s}(Z)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({s}(Y)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({s_{n}(X)}))}

(14.16)

and

\textstyle{\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}({t}(Y\cap Z))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}({t}(Z))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}({t}(Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}({t_{n}(X)})}

(14.17)

We now use that both functors are homotopy invariant.
The projection of
Z
to the
G
-set
Z_{0}
of barycenters is a homotopy equivalence in
G{\mathbf{Top}}
and
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{bd}}
. Similarly, there is a projection of
Y
to the
(n-1)
-skeleton
X_{n-1}
of
X
and a projection of
Y\cap Z
to a disjoint union
(Y\cap Z)_{n-1}
of boundaries of the
n
-simplices. These two maps are homotopy equivalences in
G{\mathbf{Top}}
and
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{bd}}
. These projections identify the bold parts of the push-out squares above canonically with the respective bold parts of the push-out squares below:

\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({s_{n-1}}(Y\cap Z)_{n-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({s_{0}}(Z_{0})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({s_{n-1}(X_{n-1})}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({s_{n}}(X)))}

(14.18)

and

\textstyle{\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}({t_{n-1}}(Y\cap Z)_{n-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}({t_{0}}(Z_{0}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}({t_{n-1}}(Y_{n-1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}({t_{n}}(X))}

(14.19)

The induction hypothesis now provides an equivalence between the bold parts of
(
14.18
) and (
14.19
). This equivalence then provides the desired equivalence of push-outs

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({s_{n}(}X)))\simeq\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}({t_{n}(}X))\,.

The whole construction is functorial in
X
. To see this interpret the symbols
X,Y,Z
as placeholders for entries of diagram valued functors.

Assertion

14.12
.
3
.
becomes obvious if we
expand the square (
14.14
) as follows

\textstyle{{t^{*}}\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{t^{*}}\mathrm{Asmbl}^{h}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}}
\textstyle{\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(*)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{*},\eqref{qwefweofjwpoefeqfeqwfqwe}}
\textstyle{{s^{*}}K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
(
14.13
)
\scriptstyle{{s^{*}}\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Theta}}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(*))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Theta}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{{\mathtt{Cone}}},\eqref{adsvkanjovasdvassdvavads}}
\textstyle{\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

(14.20)

The right horizontal maps in the square are induced by the natural transformation
(-)\to\underline{{\mathtt{const}}_{*}}
, and the upper-left square commutes by the naturality statement in Assertion
14.12
.
2
.
The upper right triangle commutes by the definition of
\mathrm{Asmbl}_{*}^{\Theta}
, and finally the lower triangle commutes by the naturality of
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Theta}
.
∎

15
\bm{C^{*}}
-categorical model for the analytic assembly map

The analytic assembly map
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}
in Definition
13.11
was obtained using a construction on the level of spectrum-valued
\mathrm{KK}
-theory. If we precompose this assembly map with the Paschke transformation
from Theorem
1.5
, then we get a functor whose domain is also expressed through
the coarse
K
-homology functor
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
and therefore in terms of
C^{*}
-categories of controlled objects.
In the present section we construct an assembly map

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Lambda}
in terms of a natural functor
\Lambda
between
C^{*}
-categories which models this composition.
We then relate
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Lambda}
with both
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Theta}
and
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}
.
The intermediate objects also depend on
{\mathbf{C}}
, but we again drop this subscript in their notation in order to simplify the notation.

Definition 15.1
.

We let
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}
denote the full subcategory of
G\mathbf{UBC}
of
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces which have the bornology of relative compact subsets and whose underlying
G
-topological space belongs to
G\mathbf{LCH}_{+,\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{prop}}
introduced in Definition
13.4
.

We consider
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and assume that it is effectively additive and admits countable orthogonal AV-sums.
Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}
and choose

\chi
in
\mathcal{R}(X)
, where the functor
\mathcal{R}
is as in (
13
). If
(C,\rho,\mu)
is an object in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})
, then we can consider the homomorphism
\phi\colon C_{0}(X)\to{{\mathtt{End}}_{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}(C)}
defined in (
6.1
). The sum

p_{\chi}\coloneqq\sum_{m\in G}{\sigma}(\phi(\chi)\phi(m^{*}\chi),m)

(15.1)

has finitely many non-zero terms and defines
a projection
on
(C,\rho)
considered as an object
in the
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{U}}
described in the Definition
2.21
, where
\sigma
is as in (
2.8
). We refer to Proposition
15.3
for the necessary verifications related with the following definition.

Definition 15.2
.

We define a functor

\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})\to\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})

in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
as follows:

1.

objects: The functor
\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}
sends the object

(C,\rho,\mu)
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})
to the object

(C,\rho,p_{\chi})
in
\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})
, where
p_{\chi}
is as in
(
15.1
).

2.

morphisms: The functor
\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}
sends the
morphism

A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})

to the morphism

\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A):=\sum_{m\in G}{\sigma}(\phi^{\prime}(m^{*}\chi)A\phi(\chi),m)

(15.2)

in
\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})
.

We refer to the proof of Lemma
15.4
below for the
interpretation of the infinite sum in (
15.2
).

In order to state the naturality of
\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}
we introduce the category

G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{pc}}
given by the Grothendieck construction of the functor
\mathcal{R}
. Its objects are pairs
(X,\chi)
of an object
X
in
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}
and
\chi
in
\mathcal{R}(X)
, and a morphism
f\colon(X,\chi)\to(X^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
in
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathcal{R}}
is a morphism
f\colon X\to X^{\prime}
in
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}
such that
f^{*}\chi^{\prime}=\chi
. We have a forgetful functor
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathcal{R}}\to G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}

which we will not write explicitly in formulas.

Proposition 15.3
.

1.

For every
(X,\chi)
in

G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathcal{R}}
,
the functor
\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}
is well-defined.

2.

The family
(\Lambda_{(X,\chi)})_{(X,\chi)\in G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathcal{R}}}
is a natural transformation

\Lambda\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(-)\otimes G_{can,max})\to\underline{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})}

of functors from
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathcal{R}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}
.

3.

The transformation restricts to a natural transformation

\Lambda\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(-)\otimes G_{can,max})\to\underline{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

(15.3)

of functors from
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathcal{R}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}
.

Proof.

The structure of this proof is the same as for Proposition
14.2
.

We first observe that
(C,\rho,p_{\chi})
is an object of
\mathrm{Idem}({\mathbf{U}})
.

Lemma 15.4
.

The formula (
15.2
) determines a continuous map of morphism spaces which is compatible with the composition and the involution.

Proof.

If
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})
is controlled, then the sum in (
15.2
) has finitely many non-zero terms which belong to
{\mathbf{U}}
. We will show that
\|\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)\|\leq\|A\|

for controlled
A
.
Since general morphisms in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})
can be approximated in norm by controlled morphisms and
{\mathbf{U}}
is closed,
we can then conclude that
\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}
extends by continuity to all of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})
, and that this extension takes values in morphisms of
{\mathbf{U}}
.
So from now on assume that
A
is controlled.

Using (
2.7
) and the rule
k\cdot\phi(\theta)=\phi(k^{-1,*}\theta)
for all
k
in
G
and
\theta
in
C_{0}(X)
we calculate that

\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)=\sum_{k,l\in G}e^{\prime}_{l}\phi^{\prime}(l^{-1,*}\chi)A\phi(k^{-1,*}\chi)e^{*}_{k}\ .

The family

(e^{\prime}_{l}\phi^{\prime}(l^{-1,*}\chi)A)_{l\in G}
is a mutually orthogonal family of morphisms
C\to\bigoplus_{l\in G}C^{\prime}
. In order to see that the sum of this family over
l
in
G
exists in
{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathrm{nu}}{\mathbf{C}}
, by
[
BEa
, Prop. 6.5]
we must check that this family is square summable.
To this end we observe that any finite subset
F
of
G
we have

\|\sum_{l\in F}(e^{\prime}_{l}\phi^{\prime}(l^{-1,*}\chi)A)^{*}e_{l}^{\prime}\phi^{\prime}(l^{-1,*}\chi)A\|=\|A^{*}\sum_{l\in F}\phi^{\prime}(l^{-1,*}\chi)^{2}A\|\leq\|A\|^{2}\,,

where the second inequality follows from
the operator inequality

0\leq\sum_{l\in F}\phi^{\prime}(l^{-1,*}\chi)^{2}\leq{\mathtt{id}}_{C^{\prime}}
.
We conclude that

B:=\sum_{l\in G}e^{\prime}_{l}\phi^{\prime}(l^{-1,*}\chi)A:C\to\bigoplus_{l\in G}C^{\prime}

exists in
{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathrm{nu}}{\mathbf{C}}
and is bounded in norm by
\|A\|
.

We consider the mutually orthogonal family

(B\phi(k^{-1,*}\chi)e^{*}_{k})_{k\in G}

of morphisms

\bigoplus_{k\in G}{C}\to\bigoplus_{l\in G}C^{\prime}
. In order to show that
sum of this family over
k
exists in
{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathrm{nu}}{\mathbf{C}}
we again show that this family is square summable.
For any finite subset
F
of
G
we calculate

\displaystyle\|\sum_{k\in F}(B\phi(k^{-1,*}\chi)e^{*}_{k})(B\phi(k^{-1,*}\chi)e^{*}_{k})^{*}\|

\displaystyle=\|B\sum_{k\in F}(\phi(k^{-1,*}\chi)e^{*}_{k}e_{k}\phi(k^{-1,*}\chi))B^{*}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|B\|^{2}\|\sum_{k\in F}(\phi(k^{-1,*}\chi)e^{*}_{k}e_{k}\phi(k^{-1,*}\chi)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|A\|^{2}\|\sum_{k\in F}\phi(k^{-1,*}\chi)^{2}\|\leq\|A\|^{2}

This finally shows that
\|\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)\|\leq\|A\|
.

Let
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime}\colon(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})\to(C^{\prime\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime},\mu^{\prime\prime})
be two morphisms in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})
. We assume that both are controlled. We calculate using (
13.10
) and the relation
g\cdot A=A
for all
g
in
G
that

\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A^{\prime})\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)=\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A^{\prime}A)\,,\quad\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)^{*}=\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A^{*})\,.

Finally recall that
\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)
must be a morphism from
(C,\rho,p_{\chi})
to
(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},p^{\prime}_{\chi})
in the category
\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})
. This is ensured by the following equalities

p^{\prime}_{\chi}\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)=\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)=\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)p_{\chi}

which are again shown for controlled
A
by a straightforward calculation.
∎

This finishes the verification of Assertion
15.3
.
1
.
We continue with Assertion
15.3
.
2
.
Let
f\colon(X,\chi)\to(X^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
be a morphism in
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathcal{R}}
and note
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(f)(C,\rho,\mu)=(C,\rho,f_{*}\mu)
. We let
f_{*}(\phi)\colon C_{0}(X^{\prime})\to{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C)
be the homomorphism defined with
f_{*}\mu
. Then we have the relation

f_{*}\phi(\theta^{\prime})=\phi(f^{*}\theta^{\prime})

for all
\theta^{\prime}
in
C_{0}(X^{\prime})
. In particular

(f_{*}\phi)(\chi^{\prime})=\phi(\chi)
.
This relation implies that

p_{\chi}=p_{\chi^{\prime}}
and
\Lambda_{(X^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(f)(A))=\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)
(note
Definition
3.3
.
2b
).
These equalities imply the assertion.

We finally verify Assertion
15.3
.
3
.
If
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
is a morphism in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})
, then
A\phi(\chi)
is in
{{\mathbf{C}}}
by Lemma
6.3
. This implies that
\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)
is a morphism in the ideal

{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}
.
∎

We now consider the cone sequence (
4.6
) for
E=K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}
whose boundary is the natural transformation

\partial^{{\mathtt{Cone}}}\colon K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))\to\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}(-)

(15.4)

of functors from
G\mathbf{UBC}
to
\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}
. The canonical inclusions

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X\otimes G_{can,min})\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,min})

give a further transformation

\Sigma K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}(-)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Sigma K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(-)\otimes G_{can,min}))

(15.5)

which is actually an equivalence (see the argument for the left vertical equivalence in (
15.10
) applied to the case
Y=G_{can,min}
).
The composition of the transformations (
15.4
) with the equivalence (
15.5
) will also be called the cone boundary transformation

\hat{\partial}^{{\mathtt{Cone}}}\colon K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))\to\Sigma K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(-)\otimes G_{can,max}))

of functors from
G\mathbf{UBC}
to
\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}
, but we add the
\hat{-}
in order to distinguish it from (
15.4
).

Definition 15.5
.

We define the natural transformation

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Lambda}\coloneqq K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\Lambda)\circ\hat{\partial}^{{\mathtt{Cone}}}:K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))\to\underline{\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

(15.6)

of functors from
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathcal{R}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}
.

If
X
is in
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}
(see Definition
15.1
), then it is
G
-bounded, but not necessarily bounded.
We let
X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}}
denote the object of
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{bd}}
(see Definition
14.10
) obtained from
X
by replacing the bornology of
X
by the maximal bornology.

Proposition 15.6
.

There is a canonical equivalence of functors

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}((-)_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}}))\simeq K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c,G_{can,max}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-)))

(15.7)

from
G\mathbf{UBC}_{{\mathrm{pc}}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}
.

Proof.

We employ the notion of continuous equivalence introduced in
[
BEKW20b
, Def. 3.21]
.
Recall the Definition
2.11
of a locally finite subset of a
G
-bornological space.
In the present situation we have a
G
-coarse space
Z
with two
G
-bornologies.
We denote the two objects in
G\mathbf{BC}
by
Z_{0}
and
Z_{1}
.
The identity map of
Z
is a continuous equivalence between
Z_{0}

and
Z_{1}
if the following conditions on every
G
-invariant subset
L
of
Z
are equivalent:

1.

L
is locally finite in

Z_{0}
.

2.

L
is locally finite in
Z_{1}
.

In this case we have an obvious equality in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z_{0})=\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z_{1})\,.

(15.8)

Lemma 15.7
.

If
X
is in
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc},G\mathrm{bd}}
, then the bornological coarse spaces

X\otimes G_{can,max}\quad\textrm{and}\quad X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}}\otimes G_{can,min}

are continuously equivalent, and

{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max}\quad\textrm{and}\quad{\mathcal{O}}(X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}})\otimes G_{can,min}

are continuously equivalent (in both cases by the identity map of the underlying sets).

Proof.

We consider the second case. The first is similar and simpler. Let
L
be a
G
-invariant subset of

[0,\infty)\times X{\times}G
. Let
B
be a bounded subset of
X
such that
GB=X
.
For
n
in
{\rm Nature}
and subset
A
of
X
we
consider the intersections
L_{n,e}:=L\cap([0,n]\times X\times\{e\})
and
L_{n,A}:=L\cap([0,n]\times A\times G)
.

1.

L
is locally finite in
{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max}
if and only if
L_{n,A}
is finite for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
and bounded subset
A
of
X
. In particular
L_{n,B}
is finite.
Hence
L
is locally finite in
{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max}
if and only if
L_{n,X}
consists of finitely many
G
-orbits for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

2.

If
L
is locally finite in
{\mathcal{O}}(X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\max}})\otimes G_{can,min}
, if and only if

L_{n,e}
is finite for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
. This is the case exactly if
L_{n,X}
consists of finitely many
G
-orbits. ∎

Let
Y
be any object in
G\mathbf{BC}
and
X
be in
G\mathbf{UBC}
.
Then we have a diagram in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}

\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X\otimes Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\otimes Y)}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\frac{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y)}{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

(15.9)

which is natural in
X
,
where the lower sequence is exact, and where the square commutes.
If we apply
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
and use the Definition
3.4
, then we get the (natural in
X
) commutative diagram

\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,Y}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,Y}({\mathcal{O}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,Y}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\otimes Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y)}{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y)}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(15.10)

The lower sequence is a fibre sequence by the exactness of
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
, and the upper sequence is an instance of the cone sequence (
4.6
).
We now argue that the left vertical morphism is an equivalence. First of all
for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
the inclusion

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z_{n})\simeq\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z_{n}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y)

is a unitary equivalence by
[
BEc
, Lem. 5.6]
, where
Z_{n}:=[0,n]\times X\times Y
has the structures induced from
{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y
. The inclusion
X\otimes Y\to Z_{n}
given by

(x,y)\mapsto(0,x,y)
is a coarse equivalence.
Hence the induced map

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X\otimes Y)\to K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z_{n}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z_{n}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y))

is an equivalence for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.
We now use that by definition

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y)\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z_{n}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y)

and that
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
commutes with filtered colimits by
[
BEa
, Thm. 14.4]
(asserting that
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
is finitary). Hence we get an equivalence

K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X\otimes Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes Y))

induced by the canonical inclusion. This is exactly the left vertical arrow in (
15.10
).

We now assume that
X
is in
G\mathbf{UBC}_{{\mathrm{pc}}}
.
Using two instances of the the diagram (
15.10
), one for
X
and
Y=G_{can,max}
, and one for
X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\max}}
and
Y=G_{can,min}
, and the equalities of
C^{*}
-categories resulting from Lemma
15.7
and (
15.8
) saying that the corresponding lower fibre sequences of the two diagrams are equivalent
we get the desired equivalence (
15.7
).
∎

Let
(X,\chi)
be in
G\mathbf{UBC}_{{\mathrm{pc}}}^{\mathcal{R}}
(see the text before Proposition
15.3
).

Proposition 15.8
.

We have a commuting diagram

\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\max}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Theta}_{X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}}}}
(
15.7
)
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,max}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Lambda}_{(X,\chi)}}
\textstyle{\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

(15.11)

which depends naturally on the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
.

Proof.

Recall the construction of the functor
\Theta
in Definition
14.1

(see also (
14.7
)) and of
\Lambda
in Definition
15.2
. We get the following morphism of exact sequences of
C^{*}
-categories.

The right vertical maps are induced from the universal property of quotients. The round equalities are consequences of Lemma
15.7

and (
15.8
). The right equality is responsible for the left vertical equivalence in
(

) up to identifications, see the proof of Proposition

. We apply
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
and consider the segment of the long exact sequences which involve the boundary map. We use the identification given by the
right vertical equivalences in the two instances of (

)
with

and

and

and

in order to
express the

-theory of the quotient categories in terms of coarse

-homology.

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-66.13133pt\raise-66.85199pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,min}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.8536pt\raise-60.24088pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{\partial}^{{\mathtt{Cone}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.13133pt\raise-66.85199pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 90.13133pt\raise-66.85199pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Alg}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}})\otimes G_{can,min}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 239.92824pt\raise-57.51282pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.67825pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447ptK^{C^{*}\mathrm{Alg}}(\Theta_{X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}}})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 310.28914pt\raise-41.426pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 345.96101pt\raise-66.85199pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\ .

The left square commutes since it is induced by an equality of exact sequences of
C^{*}
-categories. We must provide the filler of the right triangle.

This filler will be given by a unitary equivalence (see
[
BEa
, Def. 17.9]
for the definition of this notion in the non-unital case) of functors on the level of
C^{*}
-categories which will be induced from the equivalence provided by the following lemma.

Lemma 15.9
.

The following triangle is filled by a natural unitary equivalence

\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}(X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}})\otimes G_{can,min})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\Theta_{X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}}}}

Proof.

We consider an object
(C,\rho,\mu)
on the common domain of the functors. Recall that

\mu_{g}:=\mu([0,\infty)\times X\times\{g\})
. Then we define the morphism

U:=\sum_{m\in G}{\sigma}(\phi(\chi)\mu_{m},m^{-1})\colon(C,\rho)\to(C,\rho)

in
{{\mathbf{U}}}
. This sum converges strictly in
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}(\oplus_{g\in G}C,\oplus_{g\in G}C)
and defines a morphism in
{\mathbf{U}}
.
By a straightforward calculation we see that

UU^{*}=\tilde{p}\,,\quad U^{*}U=p_{\chi}\,,

where
\tilde{p}
and
p_{\chi}
are as in (
14.2
) and
(
15.1
), respectively.
We conclude that

Up_{\chi}=UU^{*}U=\tilde{p}U
and that
we therefore have a unitary isomorphism
U\colon(C,\tilde{\rho},p_{\chi})\to(C,\rho,\tilde{p})
in
\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})

as desired.

In order to verify that
U
implements a natural transformation we must check the
compatibility with morphisms.
Let
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
be a morphism in the domain of the functors.
We let
U^{\prime}
be defined as above for
(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
, assume that
A
is controlled, and then calculate
by inserting all definitions

U^{\prime}\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}(A)=\Theta_{X}(A)U
.
∎

In view of
[
BEa
, Rem. 17.10]
, the unitary equivalence from Lemma
15.9
implements a unitary equivalence filling

\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X)\otimes G_{can,max})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\Lambda_{(X,\chi)}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}(X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}})\otimes G_{can,min})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\Theta_{X_{{\mathcal{B}}_{max}}}}

We now use
[
BEa
, Lem. 17.11]
which provides the desired filler of the right triangle in (
15
).
∎

Remark 15.10
.

In Proposition
15.8
we could state a stronger assertion
saying that there is an equivalence of natural transformations from

G\mathbf{UBC}_{{\mathrm{pc}}}^{\mathcal{R}}
. The constructions on the
C^{*}
-category level done in the proof are sufficiently natural. But writing out the details would amount to write out large higher coherence diagrams.
Since we do not really need this naturality, we refrain from doing so.
∎

We consider
(X,\chi)
in
G\mathbf{UBC}_{\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathcal{R}}
.
We assume that
X
is in the image of
\iota^{\mathrm{scl}}
and choose a scale
\tau
for
X
such that we have
(X,\tau)\in G\mathbf{UBC}^{\mathrm{scl}}
, see Definition
5.6
.
Recall the Paschke morphism
p_{(X,\tau)}
from (
1.16
).
We use Definition
4.9
in order to rewrite the domain of
\mathrm{Asmbl}_{(X,\chi)}^{\Lambda}
introduced in Definition
15.5
.

Proposition 15.11
.

We have a commuting square

\textstyle{K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,{\mathcal{X}}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\Lambda}_{(X,\chi)}}
\scriptstyle{p_{(X,\tau)}}
\textstyle{\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{An}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}},X}}
\textstyle{\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

(15.25)

which depends naturally on the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
.

Proof.

), (

) and (

) of

and

The commutativity of the upper triangle reflects the definition of
\mathrm{Asmbl}_{(X,\chi)}^{\Lambda}
in Definition
15.5
. The filler of the middle hexagon is obtained from the naturality of boundary operators using a chase in the morphisms of fibre sequences obtained by applying
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}
to (
).
It has been shown in Lemma
7.1

that the upper left vertical morphism
K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\bar{i})
is an equivalence.
The lower triangle reflects the Definition
13.11
of
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X}
where also the notation appearing on the lower left horizontal arrow is explained.

So in order to produce a filler of the square
(
15.25
) we must provide a filler of the lower left square in (
). This is the assertion of the following lemma.

Lemma 15.12
.

We have a commuting square

\textstyle{K^{C^{*}\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbf{Q}_{\tau}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Unfolding the definitions we see that
the dotted morphism is induced by a functor

\Gamma_{(X,\tau,\chi)}\colon\mathbf{Q}_{\tau}(X)\to\frac{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})}{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

(15.50)

which has the following description:

1.

objects: The functor
\Gamma_{(X,\tau,\chi)}
sends the object
(C,\rho,\mu)
in
\mathbf{Q}_{\tau}(X)
to the object
(C,\rho,{{\mathtt{id}}_{C}})
in
\frac{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})}{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}
.

2.

morphisms: The functor
\Gamma_{(X,\tau,\chi)}
sends a morphism
[A]\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{Q}_{\tau}(X)
to the morphism

{[\sum_{g\in G}\sigma(\phi^{\prime}(\chi)\phi^{\prime}(g^{*}\chi)A,g)]}\colon(C,\rho,{{\mathtt{id}}_{C}})\to(C^{\prime},\rho,{{\mathtt{id}}_{C^{\prime}}})

(15.51)

in
\frac{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})}{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

Note that the sum in (

In order to show Lemma

(15.52)

where

\bar{\Lambda}_{(X,\chi)}\circ\bar{i}\colon\mathbf{Q}_{\tau}(X)\to\frac{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{U}}})}{\mathrm{Idem}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)}

1.

Recall the notion of a MvN equivalence of functors from

We claim that

The MvN equivalence

v=({[}v_{(C,\rho,\mu)}{]})_{(C,\rho,\mu)\in\mathbf{Q}_{\tau}(X)}
, where

{\mathbf{U}}
.
Note that in the summands in (

∎

16
Davis–Lück functors and the argument of Kranz

In this section we review the argument of Kranz
[
Kra
]
for the comparison of the Davis–Lück assembly map with the Kasparov assembly map which involves the Meyer–Nest assembly map as an intermediate step.
In more detail, Kranz compares the Davis–Lück assembly map with the Meyer–Nest assembly map, which is known to coincide with the analytical assembly map. We will review these comparisons below. In fact, Kranz’ paper has two separate parts. On the one hand, he shows that the Davis–Lück assembly map associated to a functor

K^{G}\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{Orb},\mathbf{Sp})

satisfying certain axioms (stated in Assumption
16.6
) is equivalent to the Meyer–Nest assembly map. On the other hand, he provides a concrete construction of such a functor
K^{G}
.
We recall this construction in detail with the goal of showing that it only involves
formal manipulations using the calculus of equivariant
\mathrm{KK}
-theory as developed in
[
BEL
]
.

We first recall the Meyer–Nest approach to the Baum–Connes assembly map
[
MN06
]
. Given the results of
[
BEL
]
and the present paper, we will give an almost self-contained treatment, the only exception is the usage of
[
MN06
, Prop. 4.6]
in the proof of Proposition
16.2
below. We interpret the terminology introduced in
[
MN06
]
in the stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
introduced in
[
BEL
, Def. 1.8]
instead of the triangulated homotopy category of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
as considered by Meyer–Nest.
We call a subcategory of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
localizing
10
10

10

Usually, localizing subcategories are stable, cocomplete subcategories of stable, cocomplete
\infty
-categories. Since
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is only known to admit countable colimits, we must use this ad-hoc definition.
if it is thick and closed under countable direct sums. In the following we use the restriction, induction and crossed-product functors on the level of stable
\infty
-categories as introduced in
[
BEL
, Sec. 1.5]
.

Definition 16.1
.

1.

We define
\mathcal{CI}
as the localizing subcategory of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
generated by the objects of the form
\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}(A)
for all finite subgroups
H
of
G
and objects
A
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H}
. The objects of
\mathcal{CI}
will be called compactly induced.

2.

We
define
\mathcal{CC}
as the localizing subcategory of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
given by all objects
A
with
\mathrm{Res}_{H,s}^{G}(A)=0
for all finite subgroups
H
of
G
.

We note here that
\mathcal{CC}
is localising because the restriction functors commute with countable sums
[
BEL
, Lem. 4.3]
.
The proof of the following proposition is based on a general adjoint functor theorem applicable in this situation.

Proposition 16.2
.

There exists an adjunction

\mathrm{incl}:\mathcal{CI}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}:C

(16.1)

Proof.

For any object
A
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
by
[
MN06
, Prop. 4.6]
there is an object
\tilde{A}
in
\mathcal{CI}
with a morphism
\tilde{A}\to A
(called the Dirac morphism) inducing an equivalence of functors
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(-,\tilde{A})\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(-,A)
from
\mathcal{CI}^{\mathrm{op}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}
.
Hence for any
A
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
the functor

\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(-,A)_{|\mathcal{CI}^{{\mathrm{op}}}}\colon\mathcal{CI}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is representable by an object of
\mathcal{CI}
. This implies the existence of the right adjoint
C
to
\mathrm{incl}
as follows for instance from
[
Lan21
, Prop. 5.1.10]
.
∎

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a stable
\infty
-category. Recall that a semi-orthogonal decomposition of
{\mathcal{C}}
is a pair
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}})
of full stable subcategories such that

{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}(A,B)\simeq 0
for all
A
in
{\mathcal{A}}
and
B
in
{\mathcal{B}}
, and such that
for every object
C
of
{\mathcal{C}}
there exists a fibre sequence

A\to C\to B
with
A
in
{\mathcal{A}}
and
B
in
{\mathcal{B}}
. For the sake of completeness of the presentation, we give the following list of equivalent conditions on a pair
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}})
of stable subcategories, and refer for more details to
[
Lur
, Sec. 7.2.1]
:

1.

The pair
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}})
is a semi-orthogonal decomposition of
{\mathcal{C}}
.

2.

The pair
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}})
is a
t
-structure on
{\mathcal{C}}
.

3.

The inclusion
{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
has a right adjoint and
{\mathcal{B}}
is the right orthogonal complement of
{\mathcal{A}}
.

4.

The inclusion
{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
has a left adjoint and
{\mathcal{A}}
is the left orthogonal complement of
{\mathcal{B}}
.

Proposition 16.3
.

The pair
(\mathcal{CI},\mathcal{CC})
is a semi-orthogonal decomposition of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
.

Proof.

For every subgroup
H
of
G
we have an adjunction

\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}:\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}:\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H,s}

which can be obtained from

[
BEL
, Thm. 1.23.1]
by restriction to the separable subcategories.
It is an
immediate consequence of the existence of these adjunctions
that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A,B)\simeq 0
for all
A
in
{\mathcal{CI}}
and
B
in
\mathcal{CC}
. We get in fact the following stronger assertion that
\mathcal{CC}
consists precisely of the objects

B
of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
with
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A,B){\simeq 0}
for all
A
in
\mathcal{CI}
, i.e. that
\mathcal{CC}
is the right orthogonal complement to
\mathcal{CI}
.

In view of Proposition
16.2
, the following is precisely a specialization of the argument that Condition
3
above
implies Condition
1
.
We must show that for any object
A
of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
, there is a fibre sequence

C(A)\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow N(A)\

(16.2)

with
C(A)
in
\mathcal{CI}
and
N(A)
in
\mathcal{CC}
.
By Proposition
16.2
we have a fibre sequence of functors

C\to{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}\to N
, where
N\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is defined as the
cofibre of the counit of the adjunction in (
16.1
). It suffices to show that
N
takes values in
\mathcal{CC}
. Let
A
be in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
. Then
for every
B
in
\mathcal{CI}
we have

\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B,N(A))\simeq\mathrm{cofib}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B,C(A))\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B,A))
.
But
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B,C(A))\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B,A)

is an equivalence by the construction of
C

so that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B,N(A))\simeq 0
. Since, as seen above,
\mathcal{CC}
is precisely the right-orthogonal complement of
\mathcal{CI}

this implies that
N(A)
belongs to
\mathcal{CC}
.
∎

Let
A
be in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
.

Definition 16.4
.

The Meyer–Nest assembly map for
G

is the map

\mu_{\ast}^{\mathrm{MN}}\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}},C(A)\rtimes_{r}G)\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}},A\rtimes_{r}G)

induced by
C(A)\to A
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
.

The following theorem is an immediate consequence of
[
MN06
, Prop. 5.2]

which yields the comparison of the Meyer–Nest assembly map and Kasparov’s assembly map.

Theorem 16.5
.

There is a commutative square

\textstyle{{RK^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{C(A)}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\simeq}}
\scriptstyle{{\simeq}}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{C(A)}}
\textstyle{{RK^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{A}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}},C(A)\rtimes_{r}G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\mathrm{MN}}_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}},A\rtimes_{r}G)}

where the vertical maps are instances of Kasparov’s assembly map of Definition
13.8
for the family of finite subgroups, and the horizontal maps are induced by the morphism
C(A)\to A
.

Proof.

First we note that the square commutes by the naturality of the Kasparov assembly map with respect to morphisms between coefficients.
Using Definition
13.7
the upper horizontal map is equivalent to the map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{W\subseteq E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(C_{0}(W),C(A))\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{W\subseteq E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(C_{0}(W),A)\,,

where the colimits run over the
G
-finite sub-complexes of
E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}
.
It is an equivalence by the definition of
C(A)\to A
, since
C_{0}(W)
belongs to
\mathcal{CI}
for every
W
appearing in the colimit.

The verification of the fact that
\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{C(A)}
is an equivalence is more complicated. The reference
[
MN06
]

employs the work of
[
OO97
]

(isomorphism of the induction map) and

[
CE01
, Prop. 2.3]
(compatibility of induction with the Kasparov assembly map).
Using the results of the present paper, Theorem
17.1
gives an independent proof of this fact in the case of discrete groups. Note that
[
MN06
]
considers the more general case of locally compact groups.

∎

We now consider a family
(K^{H})_{H\subseteq G}
of functors

K^{H}\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(H\mathbf{Orb},\mathbf{Sp})\,,\quad A\mapsto K_{A}^{H}

indexed by the subgroups
H
of
G
.
In order to formulate the properties of this family required for Kranz’ argument we consider the functor

i_{H}^{G}\colon H\mathbf{Orb}\to G\mathbf{Orb}\,,\quad S\mapsto G\times_{H}S

and let
i_{H,!}^{G}
denote the left Kan extension functor along
i_{H}^{G}
.
We assume
(K^{H})_{H\subseteq G}
has the following properties:

Assumption 16.6
.

1.

K^{G}
preserves countable colimits.

2.

For every
A
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
and subgroup
H
of
G
we have
an equivalence
11
11

11

The subscript

at various functors indicates their restriction to the subcategory of separable algebras.

K^{G}_{A}(G/H)\simeq\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}},(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H,s}(A)\rtimes_{r}H)_{s})\,.

(16.3)

3.

For any subgroup
H

of
G
we have a commutative square

\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{K^{H}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H,s}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(H\mathbf{Orb},\mathbf{Sp})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{G}_{H,!}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{K^{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{Orb},\mathbf{Sp})}

(16.4)

Note that we are mainly interested in the member
K^{G}
of the family
(K^{H})_{H\subseteq G}
.
The other members are only used to formulate Assumption
16.6
.
3
.
In the example of the family
(K^{H})_{H\subseteq G}
used below the functors

K^{H}
are constructed by applying

to

. In this case

.

In view of Definition
11.1
we consider
K^{G}
as a functor from
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
to the stable
\infty
-category of
\mathbf{Sp}
-valued equivariant homology theories. In particular, for

and

G{\mathbf{Top}}

in

.

The argument of Kranz is then based on the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{K^{G}_{C(A)}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\mathrm{MN}}_{A,E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{C(A),E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}}
\textstyle{K^{G}_{A}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{A},E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}
\textstyle{K^{G}_{C(A)}(\ast)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\mathrm{MN}}_{A,\ast}}
\textstyle{K^{G}_{A}(\ast)}

(16.5)

Here the vertical Davis–Lück assembly maps (

) are induced by the map
E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}\to\ast
.
Moreover, the horizontal Mayer–Nest assembly maps are induced by the map
C(A)\to A
. By Assumption
16.6
.
2
the map

is indeed the map from Definition
16.4
.

Theorem 16.7

(Kranz)
.

We have an equivalence
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{A,E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}\simeq\mu_{A,\ast}^{\mathrm{MN}}
.

Proof.

The square in (
16.5
) yields an equivalence of
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{A,E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}
with
\mu^{\mathrm{MN}}_{A,*}
provided one can show that
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{C(A),E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}
and
\mu^{\mathrm{MN}}_{A,E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}
are equivalences. This is the content of the following two lemmas.

Let
A
be in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
.

Lemma 16.8
.

The Meyer–Nest assembly map

\mu^{MN}_{A,E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Since
K^{G}
is exact by Assumption
16.6
.
1
,
using (
16.2
) we see that it suffices to show that

K^{G}_{N(A)}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\simeq 0\,.

(16.6)

Since
N(A)
belongs to
\mathcal{CC}
we have
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H,s}(N(A))\simeq 0
for all
H
in
\mathbf{Fin}
. As a consequence of
(
16.3
) we conclude

K^{G}_{N(A)}(G/H)\simeq 0
for every
H
in
\mathbf{Fin}
.
On the other hand, by the characterization (
13.2
) of the homotopy type of
E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}
we have
Y^{G}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})(G/H)\simeq 0
(see (
11.2
) for
Y^{G}
) provided
H\not\in\mathbf{Fin}
.
As an immediate consequence of the formula (
11.4
) for the evaluation of a homology theory on a
G
-topological space we get the desired equivalence (
16.6
).
∎

Let
A
be in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
.

Lemma 16.9
.

If
A
is in
\mathcal{CI}
, then the Davis–Lück assembly map
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{A,E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Since
K^{G}
preserves countable colimits and
\mathcal{CI}
is generated by
\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}(B)
for all
B
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
and all finite subgroups
H
of
G
it suffices to show that

\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}(B),E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}
is an equivalence for such data.
By Diagram (
16.4
) we have an equivalence

K^{G}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}(B)}\simeq i^{G}_{H,!}K^{G}_{B}
.
It is now a general fact (see e.g.
[
BEa
, Lem. 17.25]
for an argument) that for a functor
E\colon H\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{M}
with cocomplete stable target
\mathbf{M}
we have a natural equivalence of functors

i_{H,!}^{G}E\simeq E\circ\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}\colon G{\mathbf{Top}}\to\mathbf{M}\,.

We therefore get the commutative square

\textstyle{K^{G}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}(B)}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}(B),E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{K^{G}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}(B)}(*)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{K^{H}_{B}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{!}
\textstyle{K^{H}_{B}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(*))}

Since

\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\to\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(*)
is a homotopy equivalence in
H{\mathbf{Top}}
we conclude that the map marked by
!
is an equivalence.
This implies that the map
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}(B),E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}
is an equivalence.
∎

This finishes the proof of Theorem
16.7
.
∎

We now discuss the construction of the functor
K^{G}
. It is based on the ideas of Kranz
[
Kra
]
, but we reformulate the construction such that it only uses the formal aspects of the calculus of equivariant
\mathrm{KK}
-theory as developed in
[
BEL
]
.
We give full details since we use them crucially in the argument for Proposition
17.2
, which in turn is used in Theorem
17.1
.

We start with the adjunction

{\mathbb{C}}[-]:G{\mathbf{Set}}\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}):\mathrm{Ob}

(16.7)

whose left adjoint sends a
G
-set
S
to the
G
-
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbb{C}}[S]
with the
G
-set
S
of objects and morphisms generated by the identities
[
Bun
, Lem. 3.8]
.
By
[
Bun
, Lem. 3.7]
the inclusion

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is again a left-adjoint.
By post-composition with this inclusion we therefore get a left-adjoint functor

{\mathbb{C}}[-]\colon G{\mathbf{Set}}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})

which we denote by the same symbol for simplicity.

Recall the functor
y^{G}:\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}
from
[
BEL
, Def. 1.8]
.

Definition 16.10
.

We define the functors

\hat{K}^{G}\colon\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(G\mathbf{Orb},\mathbf{Sp})\,,\quad A\mapsto K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}((A\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}({\mathbb{C}}[-]))\rtimes_{r}G)

and

K^{G}\coloneqq\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\xrightarrow{y^{G}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}\xrightarrow{\hat{K}^{G}}\mathbf{Sp}\,.

In order to verify that
K^{G}
satisfies the Assumption
16.6
we analyse the construction of these functors through various intermediate constructs.
The most difficult part is thereby Assumption
16.4
.
3
.
If one is not interested in the details of the argument one could skip the material until Theorem
16.18
and just accept its statement.

We start with the functor

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})\times G{\mathbf{Set}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})}\times{\mathbb{C}}[-]}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})\times{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})

(16.8)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle-\otimes_{\max}-}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}\,.

Using the exponential law, the above defines a functor

R^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(G{\mathbf{Set}},\mathrm{KK}^{G})\,,\quad{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto R^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\,.

Let
i_{\omega}\colon G{\mathbf{Set}}_{\omega}\to G{\mathbf{Set}}
denote the inclusion of the full subcategory of countable
G
-sets, and let
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\coprod_{\omega}}
the denote the full subcategory of a functor category of countable coproduct preserving functors. Let

be as in
[
BEL
, Def. 1.8]
.

Lemma 16.11
.

1.

R^{G}
is
s
-finitary.

The restriction of
R^{G}
to
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
sends unitary equivalences to equivalences.

3.

The functor

sends weak Morita equivalences to equivalences.

4.

We have a canonical factorization

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The functor

preserves colimits.

We have a factorization

\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(G{\mathbf{Set}}_{\omega},\mathrm{KK}^{G})}

(16.9)

such that
F_{s}^{{G}}
preserves countable colimits.

Proof.

Using the fact that
\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}
is symmetric monoidal
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.35]
we can rewrite the functor in (
16.8
) as

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})\times G{\mathbf{Set}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}\times\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}({\mathbb{C}}[-])}}{{\to}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}\times\mathrm{KK}^{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle-\otimes_{\max}-}}{{\to}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}\,.

(16.10)

The Assertions
1
,
2
and
3
now follow from the corresponding properties of the functor

\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}
stated in
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.32]
, where for

we also use that
the tensor structure on
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
preserves colimits in each variable.
In order to show Assertion
4
we again use the Formula (
16.10
). It is then clear that we must define
F^{G}
by the composition

\displaystyle\mathclap{F^{G}\colon\mathrm{KK}^{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\times\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}({\mathbb{C}}[-])}}{{\to}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}\times{\mathbf{Fun}}(G{\mathbf{Set}},\mathrm{KK}^{G})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle-\otimes_{\max}-}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(G{\mathbf{Set}},\mathrm{KK}^{G})\,,\quad A\mapsto F^{G}_{A}}

Since
-\otimes_{\max}-
is bi-exact and preserves colimits in each argument we conclude Assertion
5
.

We finally show Assertion
6
.
We let
{\mathbb{C}}_{s}[-]
denote the restriction of
{\mathbb{C}}[-]
to countable sets. We consider
{\mathbb{C}}_{s}[-]
as a functor with values in the full subcategory
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
of
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of
C^{*}
-categories with countably many objects and separable morphism spaces. The functor
{\mathbb{C}}_{s}[-]
is still a left-adjoint.
The restriction of the adjunction

A^{f}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\leftrightarrows C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathrm{incl}

(see e.g.
[
Bun
, Lem. 3.9]
) to separable objects
yields an adjunction

A^{f}_{s}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\leftrightarrows C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}:\mathrm{incl}\,.

We define

F^{G}_{s}
by the formula

F^{G}_{s,(-)}(-)\coloneqq(-)\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A_{s}^{f}({\mathbb{C}}_{s}[-]))\,.

(16.12)

The following chain of equivalences yields the commutative square (
16.9
), where for the moment we ignore the superscript

\coprod_{\omega}
at the lower left corner

\displaystyle\mathclap{y^{G}\circ F^{G}_{s,(-)}(-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{\simeq}}y^{G}((-)\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A^{f}_{s}({\mathbb{C}}_{s}[-])))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\simeq}}y^{G}(-)\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}({\mathbb{C}}[i_{\omega}(-)])))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{\simeq}}F^{G}_{y^{G}(-)}(i_{\omega}(-))\,.}

For the marked equivalence we use that
y^{G}
is symmetric monoidal and the obvious equivalence
y^{G}(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A_{s}^{f}({\mathbb{C}}_{s}[-])))\simeq\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}({\mathbb{C}}[i_{\omega}(-)])

of functors from
G{\mathbf{Set}}_{\omega}
to
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.

It remains to show that for any
A
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
the functor

F_{s,A}^{G}
preserves countable coproducts.
By definition, we have an equivalence

F^{G}_{s,A}(-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{\simeq}}A\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A_{s}^{f}({\mathbb{C}}_{s}[-]))

of functors from
G{\mathbf{Set}}_{\omega}
to
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
.
Because
{\mathbb{C}}_{s}[-]
is a left-adjoint it preserves countable coproducts.
The functor

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\circ A^{f}_{s}
sends the relevant countable coproducts to sums by

[
BEL
, Lem. 6.6]
. Finally, by
[
BEL
, Prop. 1.7]

the tensor product
-\otimes_{\max}-
on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves countable sums in each argument. This finishes the construction of the factorization
F^{G}_{s}
asserted in
6
.

It immediately follows from the definition in (
16.12
) that
the functor
F_{s}^{G}
preserves countable colimits.
Here we use again that
-\otimes_{\max}-
on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves countable colimits in each argument
[
BEL
, Prop. 1.7]
.
This finishes the verification of Assertion
6
.
∎

Corollary 16.17
.

1.

preserves colimits.

.
1
, the fact that

) and the definitions.

In order to show Assertion

17
The generalized Green–Julg Theorem

In this section we show
a version of the generalized Green–Julg theorem, see
[
GHT00
, Thm. 13.1]

stating that the Kasparov assembly map for the family
\mathbf{Fin}
and proper
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras
is an equivalence. In our statement we replace the condition that the separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
is proper by the weaker (see
[
MN06
, Cor. 7.3]
) homotopy theoretic condition that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A)
belongs to the set
\mathcal{CI}
generated by the compactly induced objects, see Definition
16.1
. That
the Kasparov assembly map is an equivalence for compactly induced coefficients was shown
more generally for locally compact groups
G
in
[
CE01
]
. Our proof for discrete groups is logically independent of the results of
[
CE01
]

and also different from the one in
[
GHT00
]
.
In particular, it makes the proof of Theorem
16.5
independent of
[
CE01
]
.
Our approach is based on the equivalence between the analytic and Davis–Lück assembly maps and that the analoguous assertion
for the latter is known.

We consider
A
be in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
.

Theorem 17.1
.

If
A
belongs to
\mathcal{CI}
, then the Kasparov assembly map

\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{A,\mathbf{Fin}}\colon RK^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\to\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},A\rtimes_{r}G)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The proof of this theorem is based on a chain of comparison results of independent interest which eventually will be combined to provide an equivalence between
\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{A,\mathbf{Fin}}
and
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{A,\mathbf{Fin}}
. The latter is known to be an equivalence by Lemma
16.9
.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
so that
K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is given by Definition
13.2
.
We then form
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
and
\hat{K}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{u}}\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{Sp}
by Definition
16.10
.

Recall the Definition
13.3
of
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{h}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathcal{F}}}
and
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{u},{\mathcal{F}}}
from (
13.4
).

Proposition 17.2
.

We have a canonical equivalence

K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}\simeq\hat{K}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{u}}
and therefore for any family
{\mathcal{F}}
of subgroups of
G
a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\hat{K}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{u}}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}\hat{K}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{u}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{u},{\mathcal{F}}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\hat{K}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{u}}^{G}(*)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Asmbl}^{h}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathcal{F}}}}
\textstyle{K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}(*)}

(17.1)

which is natural for
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
.

Proof.

For any effectively additive
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
we define a functor

{\mathbf{D}}^{u}[-]\colon{\mathbf{Set}}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\,.

It sends a set

X
to the
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{D}}^{u}[X]
whose objects are pairs

(D,(p_{x})_{x\in X})
of an object
D
of
{\mathbf{D}}^{u}
and a family of mutually orthogonal
effective projections on
D
such that
\{x\in X\mid p_{x}\not=0\}
is finite and
\sum_{x\in X}p_{x}={\mathtt{id}}_{D}
.
The morphisms
(D,(p_{x})_{x\in X})\to(D^{\prime},(p^{\prime}_{x})_{x\in X})
in
{\mathbf{D}}^{u}[X]
are morphisms
a\colon D\to D^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
such that for all
x,x^{\prime}
in
X
with
x\not=x^{\prime}
we have

p^{\prime}_{x^{\prime}}ap_{x}=0
. A morphism
f\colon X\to X^{\prime}
of sets induces a unital functor

{\mathbf{D}}^{u}[X]\to{\mathbf{D}}^{u}[X^{\prime}]
which sends

(D,(p_{x})_{x\in X})
to
(D,(\sum_{x\in f^{-1}(x^{\prime})}p_{x})_{x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}})
(here we use the assumption that
{\mathbf{D}}
is effectively additive) and acts as identity on morphisms.

The construction of
{\mathbf{D}}^{u}[-]
from
{\mathbf{D}}
is functorial in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd}}
. If
G
acts on
X
and
{\mathbf{D}}
, then we get an induced action on
{\mathbf{D}}^{u}[X]
by functoriality.
We have thus defined a functor from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd}})

to
{\mathbf{Fun}}(G{\mathbf{Set}},{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}))
.

In
[
BEc
, Prop. 8.2.1]
we have constructed an isomorphism

\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}((-)_{min,max})\cong{\mathbf{C}}^{u}[-]

of functors from
G{\mathbf{Set}}
to
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
. For
X
in
G{\mathbf{Set}}
it sends the object
(C,\mu)
in
\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X_{min,max})
to the object

(C,(\mu(\{x\}))_{x\in X})
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}[X]
and acts as identity on morphisms. This isomorphism is clearly natural for
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd}})
. Restricting along
G\mathbf{Orb}\subseteq G{\mathbf{Set}}
we therefore get an equivalence

K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}(\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}((-)_{min,max})\rtimes_{r}G)\simeq K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}({\mathbf{C}}^{u}[-]\rtimes_{r}G)

(17.2)

of functors from
G\mathbf{Orb}
to
\mathbf{Sp}

which is natural for
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd}})
.

We now use that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits countable

-sums.
By
[
BEc
, Prop. 8.1 and Prop. 8.2.3]
we have a unitary equivalence

\phi\colon\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}((-)_{min,max})\rtimes_{r}G\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathbf{\bar{C}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}((-)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min})

of functors from
G{\mathbf{Set}}
to

.
This construction is not natural in

since the first step in the proof of

.
But if
\kappa\colon{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
is a morphism in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
, then it preserves AV-sums and for every object

of
{\mathbf{C}}
we have a unique multiplier unitary

u_{C}\colon\bigoplus_{g\in G}gC\to\bigoplus_{g\in G}g\kappa(C)
such that

for every

in
G
.
These unitaries induce a unitary filler of the square of
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
-valued functors

\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}((-)_{min,max})\rtimes_{r}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{C}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}((-)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C^{\prime}}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}((-)_{min,max})\rtimes_{r}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{C^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}((-)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min})}

whose vertical maps are induced by
\kappa
. We therefore get an equivalence
of functors from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
to
{\mathbf{Fun}}(G{\mathbf{Set}},C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{2,1})
.
Since
K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
factorizes over the localization
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{2,1}
at unitary equivalences,
after applying
K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
, restricting along

, and using Definitions

and

K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}(\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}((-)_{min,max})\rtimes_{r}G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}((-)_{min,max}\otimes G_{can,min}))\simeq K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}

(17.3)

which is natural for
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
.

We have a natural transformation

v\colon{\mathbf{C}}^{u}\otimes_{\max}{\mathbb{C}}[-]\to{\mathbf{C}}^{u}[-]\,,

(17.4)

see (

) for
{\mathbb{C}}[-]
,
of functors from

to

. Its

X
in
{G}{\mathbf{Set}}
is the functor

v_{X}\colon{\mathbf{C}}^{u}\otimes_{\max}{\mathbb{C}}[X]\to{\mathbf{C}}^{u}[{X}]\,,

which
sends the object

(C,y)
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}\otimes_{\max}{\mathbb{C}}[X]
to the object
(C,(p^{y})_{x\in X})

with

p_{x}^{y}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}{\mathtt{id}}_{C}&x=y,\\
0&x\not=y,\end{array}\right.

and which acts by
a\otimes z\mapsto za
on morphisms.
The functor
v_{X}
is a Morita equivalence: It is fully faithful, and every object of

{\mathbf{C}}^{u}[{X}]
is isomorphic to a finite sum of objects in the image of
v_{X}
.
Since
K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
is Morita invariant and
-\rtimes_{r}G
preserves Morita equivalences by
[
BEa
, Prop. 16.11]
, after restriction along
G\mathbf{Orb}\subseteq G{\mathbf{Set}}
we get a natural transformation
of functors

\hat{K}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{u}}\simeq K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}(({\mathbf{C}}^{u}\otimes_{\max}{\mathbb{C}}[-])\rtimes_{r}G)\simeq K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}({\mathbf{C}}^{u}[-]\rtimes_{r}G)

(17.5)

from
G\mathbf{Orb}
to
\mathbf{Sp}

where we use Definition
16.10
in order to see the first equivalence.
Since the transformation (
17.4
) is clearly natural for
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
, so is
(
17.5
).

Combining (
17.5
), (
17.3
) and (
17.2
)
we get the equivalence asserted in the proposition.
∎

Proposition 17.3
.

If
{\mathcal{F}}\subseteq\mathbf{Fin}
, then have a commutative square

\textstyle{\Sigma RK^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)},{\mathcal{F}}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\Sigma\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}\rtimes_{r}G)}
\textstyle{RK^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathcal{F}}}}
\textstyle{{\Sigma}\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\rtimes_{r}G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\simeq}}

(17.6)

which is natural in
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
.

Proof.

We start with the construction of the square (
17.6
).
Its left vertical morphism will be induced by a zig-zag and therefore does not have a preferred direction. We expand the square into the following commutative diagram:
