3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.43404pt\raise-123.29063pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.58946pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathcal{F}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 234.45677pt\raise-105.37328pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 72.74843pt\raise-133.2823pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 149.93448pt\raise-133.2823pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 275.40962pt\raise-133.2823pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}

The two upper rows of vertical maps are induced by the zig-zag

({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std},+}\leftarrow{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}

(see (
11.10
)), where the first map is a weak Morita equivalence and the second is a split relative Morita equivalence.
We use (see below for details) that the functors
RK^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{-}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})
and
\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-\rtimes_{r}G)
send weak Morita equivalences and split relative Morita equivalences to equivalences.

1.

Recall that
RK^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}})\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{W\subseteq E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(W)
, where the colimit runs over the filtered poset of
G
-finite
G
-CW subcomplexes of
E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G
. For fixed
W
the functor
{\mathbf{D}}\mapsto K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(W)
sends relative Morita equivalences
to equivalences by Lemma
7.2
.
3
.
Its sends weak Morita equivalences to equivalences by
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.32.3]
.

2.

Since we have the equivalence
\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-\rtimes G)\simeq\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-)\circ(-\rtimes G)\circ\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
of functors from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
to
\mathbf{Sp}
,
the functor
\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-\rtimes G)
sends weak Morita equivalences to equivalences since already
\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
does so by
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.32.3]
. Hence the middle upper vertical arrow is an equivalence.
One could also show that the other vertical arrow in this column is an equivalence, but since this is not needed in our argument we will not go through the details here.

3.

Since
\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-\rtimes_{r}G)\simeq\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-)\circ(-\rtimes_{r}G)\circ\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
, as in the previous point,
the functor
\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-\rtimes_{r}G)
sends weak Morita equivalences to equivalences. Since

-\rtimes_{r}G
preserves Morita equivalences by
[
BEa
, Prop. 16.11]
and
\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-)=K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
sends Morita equivalences to equivalences by
[
BEa
, Prop. 16.18]
we see that

\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-\rtimes_{r}G)
sends Morita equivalences to equivalences.
In order to see that it also sends split relative Morita equivalences to equivalences we apply

-\rtimes_{r}G
to the diagram (
2.6
). In view of the existence of splits for
p
and
q
,
exactness of the horizontal sequences is preserved. Because
-\rtimes_{r}G
preserves Morita equivalences the resulting diagram shows that

\phi\rtimes_{r}G\colon{\mathbf{D}}\rtimes_{r}G\to{\mathbf{E}}\rtimes_{r}G
is a relative Morita equivalence.
Since
\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-)=K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
is a Morita invariant homological functor,
it sends relative Morita equivalences to equivalences by
[
BEa
, Prop. 17.4]
.

The two upper right squares
are provided by the natural transformation
-\rtimes G\to-\rtimes_{r}G
.
The two lower left vertical arrows are induced by
the boundary map of the fibre sequence
associated to the
exact sequence
0\to{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to\mathbf{Q}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}\to 0
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
, see the proof of Proposition
11.15
. This connecting map is an equivalence
since
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
is flasque. The three left squares commute by the
naturality of the Kasparov assembly map with respect to the coefficients in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.
The upper triangle and the lower triangle reflect the Definitions
13.8
and
13.12
of

\mu_{({\mathbf{C}}^{u})^{(G)},{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathrm{Kasp}}
and
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathcal{F}}}
.
∎

Let
A
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and consider the split unitalization sequence

0\to A\to A^{+}\xrightarrow{p}{\mathbb{C}}\to 0

whose split will be denoted by

e\colon{\mathbb{C}}\to A^{+}
.

Proposition 17.4
.

There exists the following data:

1.

{\mathbf{C}}_{+}
,
{\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
,

2.

q\colon{\mathbf{C}}_{+}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
,

3.

s\colon{\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{+}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
,

4.

i\colon A^{+}\to({\mathbf{C}}_{+}^{u})^{(G)}
and
j\colon{\mathbb{C}}\to({\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{u})^{(G)}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
,

with the following properties:

1.

The squares

\textstyle{A^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{({\mathbf{C}}_{+}^{u})^{(G)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(q^{u})^{(G)}}
\textstyle{({\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{u})^{(G)}}

and

\textstyle{A^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathbb{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{({\mathbf{C}}^{u}_{+})^{(G)}}
\textstyle{({\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{u})^{(G)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(s^{u})^{(G)}}

commute.

2.

G
weakly fixes the objects of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}_{+}
and
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
, see Definition
2.9
.

3.

i
and
j
are Morita equivalences.

4.

q
is a quotient and
q\circ s={\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}}
.

Proof.

We let
\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}^{+}}
be the full subcategory of
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A^{+})
on the objects which are isomorphic to

\hat{A}^{+}
, see Example
2.17
. Since the object
\hat{A}^{+}
has an extension
(\hat{A}^{+},\kappa)
in
((\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}^{+}})^{u})^{(G)}
we have unitary isomorphisms
\kappa_{g}\colon\hat{A}^{+}\to g\hat{A}^{+}
in
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A^{+})
for all
g
in
G
. It follows that

\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}^{+}}
is
G
-invariant and
therefore inherits a
G
-action from
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A^{+})
.
Furthermore, we have
\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}^{+}}=(\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}^{+}})^{u}
and

G
weakly fixes the objects of
(\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}^{+}})^{u}
.

We set

{\mathbf{C}}_{+}\coloneqq\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}^{+}}\otimes_{\max}\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})

with the
G
-action induced from the first factor. We furthermore let
{\mathbf{F}}
be the
G
-
C^{*}
-category with the same objects as
\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}^{+}}
but morphism spaces isomorphic to
{\mathbb{C}}
between any two objects. We have a canonical projection

q^{\prime}\colon\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}^{+}}\to{\mathbf{F}}
involving
p
and a split
s^{\prime}\colon{\mathbf{F}}\to\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}^{+}}
involving the units of
A^{+}
. We set

{\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\coloneqq{\mathbf{F}}\otimes_{\max}\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})\,.

Then we have a quotient projection
q\coloneqq q^{\prime}\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})}\colon{\mathbf{C}}_{+}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
and the split functor

s\coloneqq s^{\prime}\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})}\colon{\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{+}
such that
q\circ s={\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}}
.
Because of this equality the condition that
q
is a quotient simply means that it is bijective on objects.

We define
j\colon{\mathbb{C}}\to({\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{u})^{(G)}
using the object
((\hat{A}^{+},{\mathbb{C}}),\kappa\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}})
and the canonical identification

{\mathtt{End}}_{({\mathbf{C}}^{u}_{{\mathbb{C}}})^{(G)}}(((\hat{A}^{+},{\mathbb{C}}),\kappa\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}))\cong{\mathbb{C}}
.
We further define

i\colon A^{+}\to({\mathbf{C}}^{u}_{+})^{(G)}
using the object

((\hat{A}^{+},{\mathbb{C}}),\kappa\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}})
and the canonical
G
-equivariant identification
{\mathtt{End}}_{({\mathbf{C}}_{+}^{u})^{(G)}}(((\hat{A}^{+},{\mathbb{C}}),\kappa\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}))\cong A^{+}
.
Then the two squares commute.

If we forget the
G
-action, then
{\mathbf{C}}_{+}
is isomorphic to
A^{+}\otimes_{\max}\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
. We can conclude that
{\mathbf{C}}_{+}
admits all AV-sums and is therefore effectively additive.
A similar reasoning applies to
{\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
.

The functor
q
is fully faithful and hence non-degenerate.
The split
s^{\prime}\colon{\mathbf{F}}\to\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}^{+}}
is unital and hence also non-degenerate.
This implies that
s
is non-degenerate.

In order to show that
i
is a Morita equivalence we note that any object in
({\mathbf{C}}_{+}^{u})^{(G)}
is unitarily isomorphic to an object

((\hat{A},H),\kappa\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{H})
for some finite-dimensional Hilbert space
H
. It is therefore
unitarily isomorphic to a finite sum of copies of
i(A^{+})
. The same reasoning applies to show that
j
is a Morita equivalence.
∎

We now finish the proof of the Theorem
17.1
.

The statement of the theorem depends on an object
A
of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
which is assumed to belong to
\mathcal{CI}
.
We can choose an object of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
which realizes
A
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
upon applying
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
. So from now on
A
denotes this
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra.

We apply Proposition
17.4
to
A
in order to get the asserted data.
For any functor
F
from

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg},\mathrm{eadd},\omega\mathrm{add}})
to an additive category
we get a decomposition

F({\mathbf{C}}_{+})\simeq F^{\mathrm{intrs}}\oplus F({\mathbf{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}})\,,

where the projection to and inclusion of the second summand are given by
F(q)
and
F(s)
.
We call
F^{\mathrm{intrs}}
the interesting summand. A natural transformation
f\colon F\to F^{\prime}
of functors
induces a map
f^{\mathrm{intrs}}\colon F^{\mathrm{intrs}}\to F^{\prime,\mathrm{intrs}}
between the interesting summands.
We call
f^{\mathrm{intrs}}
the interesting summand of
f
.
Finally, a natural equivalence
f\simeq f^{\prime}
between natural transformations
induces a natural equivalence
f^{\mathrm{intrs}}\simeq f^{\prime,\mathrm{intrs}}
between the interesting summands.
We now have the following facts:

1.

The interesting summand of
\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{({\mathbf{C}}^{u}_{+})^{(G)},\mathbf{Fin}}
is equivalent to the interesting summand of

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{+},\mathbf{Fin}}
by Proposition
17.3
.

2.

By Theorem
1.8
the interesting summand of
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{\mathrm{an}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{+},\mathbf{Fin}}
is an equivalence if and only if
the interesting summand of

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{h}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{+},\mathbf{Fin}}
is an equivalence.

3.

The interesting summand of

\mathrm{Asmbl}^{h}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{+},\mathbf{Fin}}
is equivalent to the interesting summand of
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{+}^{u},\mathbf{Fin}}

by Proposition
17.2
.

4.

The interesting summand of
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{({\mathbf{C}}_{+}^{u})^{(G)},\mathbf{Fin}}
is
equivalent to the interesting summand of
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{+}^{u},\mathbf{Fin}}
by Lemma
2.10
. Here we use Property
2
of the data from Proposition
17.4
.

5.

We note that the Davis–Lück assembly map

\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{-,\mathbf{Fin}}
depends functorially on an object of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.
The pair of morphisms
p\colon A^{+}\to{\mathbb{C}}
and
e\colon{\mathbb{C}}\to A^{+}
provides a decomposition

\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A^{+})\simeq\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)\oplus\mathrm{kk}^{G}({\mathbb{C}})
. The commutative squares
in Property
1

of the data from Proposition
17.4
provide a decomposition of the transformation

\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{i,\mathbf{Fin}}
into a sum
(\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{i,\mathbf{Fin}})^{\mathrm{intrs}}\oplus\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{j,\mathbf{Fin}}
.
Since
i
is a Morita equivalence and the transformation between the Davis–Lück assembly maps depends on
\hat{K}^{G}_{i}
, by Corollary
16.17
.
3
the transformations
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{i,\mathbf{Fin}}
and hence
(\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{i,\mathbf{Fin}})^{\mathrm{intrs}}
are equivalences.
We conclude that the interesting summand of
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{({\mathbf{C}}_{+}^{u})^{(G)},\mathbf{Fin}}
is equivalent to

\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{A,\mathbf{Fin}}
.

6.

By a completely analogous argument the interesting summand of
\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{({\mathbf{C}}_{+})^{(G)},\mathbf{Fin}}
is equivalent to
\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{A,\mathbf{Fin}}
. Here we use
that the domain and target
RK^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{-}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{cw})
and

\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-\rtimes_{r}G)
of
\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{-,\mathbf{Fin}}
considered as functors on
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})

via
\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}
send Morita equivalences to equivalences.
For
\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-\rtimes_{r}G)
this has been observed above in the proof of Corollary
16.17
.
3
. For the other functor we use the formula

RK^{G,an}_{-}(E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{cw})\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{W\subseteq E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(W),-)\,,

where
W
runs over the
G
-finite subcomplexes of
E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G^{\mathrm{CW}}
,
and Lemma
7.2
.
3
saying that

\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(W),-)
sends Morita equivalences to equivalences for every
W
.

By a combination of these facts we see that
\mu^{\mathrm{Kasp}}_{A,\mathbf{Fin}}
is an equivalence if and only if
\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{A,\mathbf{Fin}}
is an equivalence. Under the assumption that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A)
belongs to
\mathcal{CI}
we know that

\mu^{\mathrm{DL}}_{A,\mathbf{Fin}}
is an equivalence by Lemma
16.9
.
∎
